# PlexUI for Vue: full reference
> PlexUI for Vue 3: the same components, props, markup and styles as the React library.

Vue 3 components with the same names, props, defaults and markup as the React library. Quick overview: https://plexui.com/llms-vue.txt

Install: npm install @plexui/vue @plexui/styles
Docs: https://plexui.com
Components: 62

## Setup

```css
@layer theme, base, components, utilities;
@import "tailwindcss";
@import "@plexui/styles/css";
@source "../node_modules/@plexui/vue";
```

Wrap your app with <PlexUIProvider :link-component="RouterLink"> from "@plexui/vue/components/PlexUIProvider"

Size scale: 3xs, 2xs, xs, sm, md, lg, xl, 2xl, 3xl
Colors: primary, secondary, danger, success, warning, caution, discovery, info
Variants: solid, soft, outline, ghost

---

## Accordion
Vertically stacked panels that expand to reveal content, with one or several items open at a time and full keyboard navigation.
Import: import { Accordion, AccordionContent, AccordionItem, AccordionTrigger } from "@plexui/vue/components/Accordion"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/accordion
Props:
  - type ("single" | "multiple") — Required. Whether one item or many can be open at a time
  - v-model:value / value (string | string[]) — Controlled open item(s), named like the React prop
  - default-value (string | string[]) — Uncontrolled initial open item(s)
  - collapsible (boolean) default: false — When type="single", allow closing the open item
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable every item
  - dir ("ltr" | "rtl") default: "ltr" — Reading direction
  - orientation ("horizontal" | "vertical") default: "vertical" — Layout axis
  - class (string) — Applied to the root <div>
  - @update:value ((value: string | string[]) => void) — Fires when the open item(s) change; closing the open item of a single accordion reports ""
  AccordionItem:
    - value (string) — Required. Unique identifier used by value / default-value
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable this item
    - class (string) — Applied to the item wrapper

### Overview
Set type to multiple when readers can keep several sections open at once: settings pages, long FAQs, step-by-step references. A single accordion with collapsible lets readers close the open item; without it one item is always open. card composes the accordion inside Card, the most common landing-page FAQ pattern; on a phone the card is a section of the grouped list. The returnsDisabled control disables only the return-policy item. It remains visible and keeps its open state, while keyboard navigation skips its trigger.
```vue
<script setup>
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Accordion, AccordionItem, AccordionTrigger, AccordionContent } from '@plexui/vue/components/Accordion';

const returnsDisabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<Accordion type="single" collapsible default-value="shipping" style="width: 100%; max-width: 384px">
  <AccordionItem value="shipping">
    <AccordionTrigger>What are your shipping options?</AccordionTrigger>
    <AccordionContent>
      We offer standard (5–7 business days), express (2–3 days), and
      overnight shipping. Free standard shipping on orders over $50.
    </AccordionContent>
  </AccordionItem>
  <AccordionItem value="returns" :disabled="returnsDisabled">
    <AccordionTrigger>What is your return policy?</AccordionTrigger>
    <AccordionContent>
      Returns are accepted within 30 days of delivery. Items must be unused
      and in original packaging. Refunds are processed within 5–7 business
      days of receipt.
    </AccordionContent>
  </AccordionItem>
  <AccordionItem value="support">
    <AccordionTrigger>How can I contact customer support?</AccordionTrigger>
    <AccordionContent>
      Reach us by email, live chat, or phone Monday through Friday, 9am–6pm
      ET. Weekend tickets are handled within 24 hours.
    </AccordionContent>
  </AccordionItem>
</Accordion>
</template>
```

### Multiple
Use type="multiple" when readers can keep several sections open at once: settings pages, long FAQs, step-by-step references.
```vue
<script setup>
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Accordion, AccordionItem, AccordionTrigger, AccordionContent } from '@plexui/vue/components/Accordion';

const returnsDisabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<Accordion type="multiple" :default-value="['shipping']" style="width: 100%; max-width: 384px">
  <AccordionItem value="shipping">
    <AccordionTrigger>What are your shipping options?</AccordionTrigger>
    <AccordionContent>
      We offer standard (5–7 business days), express (2–3 days), and
      overnight shipping. Free standard shipping on orders over $50.
    </AccordionContent>
  </AccordionItem>
  <AccordionItem value="returns" :disabled="returnsDisabled">
    <AccordionTrigger>What is your return policy?</AccordionTrigger>
    <AccordionContent>
      Returns are accepted within 30 days of delivery. Items must be unused
      and in original packaging. Refunds are processed within 5–7 business
      days of receipt.
    </AccordionContent>
  </AccordionItem>
  <AccordionItem value="support">
    <AccordionTrigger>How can I contact customer support?</AccordionTrigger>
    <AccordionContent>
      Reach us by email, live chat, or phone Monday through Friday, 9am–6pm
      ET. Weekend tickets are handled within 24 hours.
    </AccordionContent>
  </AccordionItem>
</Accordion>
</template>
```

### Card
Composed inside Card, the most common landing-page FAQ pattern.
```vue
<script setup>
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Accordion, AccordionItem, AccordionTrigger, AccordionContent } from '@plexui/vue/components/Accordion';
import { Card, CardHeader, CardTitle, CardDescription, CardContent } from '@plexui/vue/components/Card';

const returnsDisabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<Card style="width: 100%; max-width: 384px">
  <CardHeader>
    <CardTitle>Orders & returns</CardTitle>
    <CardDescription>Shipping, returns, and support at a glance.</CardDescription>
  </CardHeader>
  <CardContent>
    <Accordion type="single" collapsible default-value="shipping" style="width: 100%; max-width: 384px">
      <AccordionItem value="shipping">
        <AccordionTrigger>What are your shipping options?</AccordionTrigger>
        <AccordionContent>
          We offer standard (5–7 business days), express (2–3 days), and
          overnight shipping. Free standard shipping on orders over $50.
        </AccordionContent>
      </AccordionItem>
      <AccordionItem value="returns" :disabled="returnsDisabled">
        <AccordionTrigger>What is your return policy?</AccordionTrigger>
        <AccordionContent>
          Returns are accepted within 30 days of delivery. Items must be unused
          and in original packaging. Refunds are processed within 5–7 business
          days of receipt.
        </AccordionContent>
      </AccordionItem>
      <AccordionItem value="support">
        <AccordionTrigger>How can I contact customer support?</AccordionTrigger>
        <AccordionContent>
          Reach us by email, live chat, or phone Monday through Friday, 9am–6pm
          ET. Weekend tickets are handled within 24 hours.
        </AccordionContent>
      </AccordionItem>
    </Accordion>
  </CardContent>
</Card>
</template>
```

## Alert
Inline message for notices and warnings with semantic colors, variants, a title, a description, action slots, and a dismiss control.
Import: import { Alert } from "@plexui/vue/components/Alert"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/alert
Props:
  - title (string | slot) — Title displayed in the alert
  - description (string | slot) — Description text
  - actions (slot) — Actions (for example buttons)
  - actions-placement ("end" | "bottom") — Placement of actions; automatic from sizing when unset
  - mobile-actions ("capsules" | "own") default: "capsules" — At 767px and below: 50px capsules as in an iOS tip, or the buttons at their own size, for an alert inside a block of its own buttons
  - actions-class-name (string) — Class applied to the actions container
  - color (SemanticColors) default: "primary" — Color scheme
  - variant ("solid" | "soft" | "outline") default: "outline" — Style variant
  - indicator (slot | false) — Custom indicator (slot), or :indicator="false" to hide it
  - @dismiss (() => void) — Shows the dismiss button when set
  - banner (boolean) default: false — Full-width strip: square corners, lines above and below only

### Overview
color and variant style the alert. It shows a title, a description or both; each takes text, or content in the #title and #description slots, such as a &lt;TextLink color="currentcolor"&gt; to a status page. It has an indicator icon by default, chosen from its color: the indicator slot replaces it, and :indicator="false" hides it. The actions slot takes buttons or other content. They are placed automatically, at the end or, when they take more than a third of the alert's width, under the message, unless actions-placement is 'end' or 'bottom'. @dismiss adds a Dismiss button in the alert's chrome (this preview binds it as :on-dismiss, which can be left unset); for a dismissible notice, labeled buttons in actions are the suggested approach. A solid alert is for a message alone: buttons, Dismiss among them, do not read on its color, so this preview offers them on soft and outline alerts. Use banner for a full-width strip across the top or bottom of a page or panel (offline, read-only, a new version): it drops the rounded corners and keeps only the lines above and below. On a phone an alert takes the shape and text of an iOS tip (TipKit), the system's message inside a screen: 26px corners (a banner stays square), the title at 17/22 semibold and the description at 15/20, and a close button in its corner instead of Dismiss. A title or a description alone stands its first line on the middle of the icon. It keeps its icon, colors and variants, and its actions always stand under the message as 50px capsules side by side, so actions-placement is a desktop setting. With mobile-actions="own" the actions keep the size you give the buttons, for an alert inside a block of its own buttons.
```vue
<Alert
  :color="color"
  :variant="variant"
  :title="title ? 'Scheduled maintenance' : undefined"
  :description="description ? 'We\'ll be offline 2 - 4 AM UTC on July 14 while we upgrade our database.' : undefined"
  :indicator="indicator === 'none' ? false : undefined"
  :actions-placement="actionsPlacement === 'auto' ? undefined : actionsPlacement"
  :mobile-actions="mobileActions"
  :on-dismiss="onDismiss ? () => {} : undefined"
  :banner="banner"
>
  <template v-if="indicator === 'custom'" #indicator>
    <Lightbulb />
  </template>
  <template v-if="actions" #actions>
    <Button color="primary" variant="soft" pill>Set reminder</Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="solid" pill>View status</Button>
  </template>
</Alert>
```

## Avatar
An avatar for user identities that shows an initial, a photo or an icon, with overflow counts, semantic colors and roundness options.
Import: import { Avatar } from "@plexui/vue/components/Avatar"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/avatar
Props:
  - name (string) — Display name; used for text initial when no image/icon
  - image-url (string) — Absolute URL for image avatar
  - Icon (Component) — Icon component for icon avatar, bound as :Icon
  - size (number) — Width and height in pixels
  - color ("primary" | "secondary" | "success" | "info" | "discovery" | "danger") default: "secondary" — Semantic color for text/icon avatar
  - variant ("soft" | "solid") default: "soft" — Style variant
  - overflow-count (number) — Show overflow count (e.g. +9)
  - class (string) — Applied to the avatar root
  - @click ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Makes avatar interactive (renders a button)
  - @pointerdown ((event: PointerEvent) => void) — Makes avatar interactive (renders a button)

### Overview
An avatar shows one of four things. Without other props it shows the first letter of name. With image-url, an absolute URL, it shows the photo with a soft fade once it loads, and the initial if it fails. With Icon, an icon component such as Robot from @plexui/vue/components/Icon, it shows the icon; name is not shown, and only names the avatar when it is a button. With overflow-count it shows the count, formatted and scaled to fit. size sets the width and height in pixels, and the initial, icon and count scale with it. color and variant style every avatar except a loaded photo, which covers them. Avatars are fully rounded by default; a Tailwind rounding class in class overrides that. Listening to @click (or @pointerdown) makes the avatar a button with interaction styles and its name as the accessible name.
```vue
<Avatar
  :name="type === 'icon' ? 'Tech support' : type === 'overflow' ? overflowCount + ' more' : 'Tyler'"
  :image-url="type === 'image' ? 'https://gravatar.com/avatar/9531b260b9693f3394bea8646c6ea141ce58fe5a138b7db7729d60a4c5dde552' : undefined"
  :Icon="type === 'icon' ? Robot : undefined"
  :overflow-count="type === 'overflow' ? overflowCount : undefined"
  :size="size"
  :color="color"
  :variant="variant"
  :class="{ full: 'rounded-full', xl: 'rounded-xl', lg: 'rounded-lg', md: 'rounded-md', sm: 'rounded-sm', none: 'rounded-none' }[roundness]"
  v-on="onClick ? { click: saySup } : {}"
/>
```

## Avatar Group
Stacks user avatars into a single overlapping cluster, with a configurable stacking direction and one size for the whole group.
Import: import { Avatar, AvatarGroup } from "@plexui/vue/components/Avatar"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/avatar-group
Props:
  - default slot (VNode[]) — The avatars of the group
  - size (number) — Size of all avatars in the group
  - stack ("start" | "end") default: "start" — Stacking direction
  - class (string) — Applied to the group container; other attributes are not forwarded

### Overview
```vue
<AvatarGroup :size="42">
  <Avatar name="Tyler" image-url="https://gravatar.com/avatar/9531b260b9693f3394bea8646c6ea141ce58fe5a138b7db7729d60a4c5dde552" />
  <Avatar name="Jane" color="primary" variant="solid" />
  <Avatar name="Tech support" :Icon="Robot" variant="solid" />
  <Avatar :overflow-count="5" />
</AvatarGroup>
```

### Stacking direction
You can control the stacking direction using the stack prop, as either "start" or "end".
```vue
<AvatarGroup :stack="stack" :size="42">
  <Avatar name="Tyler" image-url="https://gravatar.com/avatar/9531b260b9693f3394bea8646c6ea141ce58fe5a138b7db7729d60a4c5dde552" />
  <Avatar name="Will" />
  <Avatar name="Tech support" :Icon="Robot" variant="solid" />
  <Avatar :overflow-count="5" variant="soft" />
</AvatarGroup>
```

### Group sizing
You can control the size of all avatars in the group by passing size directly to the &lt;AvatarGroup&gt; component.
```vue
<AvatarGroup :size="size">
  <Avatar name="Tyler" color="info" />
  <Avatar name="Jane" color="discovery" />
  <Avatar name="Will" color="danger" />
  <Avatar :overflow-count="5" />
</AvatarGroup>
```

## Badge
Compact label for statuses and inline metadata, with semantic colors, three variants and sizes, icon support, and a loading state.
Import: import { Badge } from "@plexui/vue/components/Badge"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/badge
Props:
  - default slot (VNode[]) — Content inside the badge
  - @remove ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Adds a cross at the end of the badge that calls this when pressed
  - remove-label (string) default: "Remove" — Accessible name of the cross
  - removable (boolean) default: false — Shows the cross as a mark, for a badge inside a control that takes the press
  - color ("secondary" | "success" | "danger" | "warning" | "info" | "discovery" | "caution") default: "secondary" — Semantic color
  - variant ("solid" | "soft" | "outline") default: "soft" — Style variant
  - size ("sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl") default: "sm" — Badge size
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded
  - class (string) — Class applied to the badge

### Overview
Style a badge with color and variant, and set its size; pill rounds it fully and adds horizontal gutter. Its content can be composed: an icon before the text takes the badge's color, size and spacing, and a LoadingIndicator marks a task in progress, with a wider gap-1.5 gap. @remove adds a cross at the end, with its own hover and focus, named by remove-label. To add the cross only at times, bind the handler as :on-remove and give it undefined otherwise, as here: Vue wraps an inline @remove expression in a function, so a listener written that way is always there. At size="xl" a badge has the height of a control, for a row of removable badges beside a button. Long text ends in an ellipsis and the cross stays.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';

const color = ref("info"); // "secondary", "success", "warning", "danger", "info", "discovery", "caution"
const variant = ref("soft"); // or "solid", "outline"
const size = ref("md"); // "sm" to "xl"
const pill = ref(false);
const leading = ref("none"); // or "icon", "loading"
const onRemove = ref(false);
const shown = ref(true);
const labels = { none: "New", icon: "Beta", loading: "In progress" };
</script>

<template>
<Badge
  v-if="shown"
  :color="color"
  :variant="variant"
  :size="size"
  :pill="pill"
  :class="leading === 'loading' ? 'gap-1.5' : undefined"
  :remove-label="'Remove ' + labels[leading]"
  :on-remove="onRemove ? () => (shown = false) : undefined"
>
  <Flask v-if="leading === 'icon'" />
  <LoadingIndicator v-if="leading === 'loading'" />
  {{ labels[leading] }}
</Badge>
<Button v-else color="secondary" variant="outline" size="sm" @click="shown = true">Show again</Button>
</template>
```

## Breadcrumb
The trail of pages that leads to the current one, above a page title, with links, plain steps and the current page.
Import: import { Breadcrumb, BreadcrumbItem } from "@plexui/vue/components/Breadcrumb"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/breadcrumb
Props:
  - size ("sm" | "md") default: "md" — Text size: sm above a compact header, md above a page title
  - aria-label (string) default: "Breadcrumb" — Name of the navigation landmark
  - separator slot (VNode[]) default: a small chevron — What stands between two items
  - default slot (VNode[]) — The BreadcrumbItem elements
  BreadcrumbItem:
    - href (string) — Link destination. Internal paths use the link component from PlexUIProvider
    - to (string | RouteLocation) — Router destination, for link components that take to
    - current (boolean) default: false — The current page: plain text marked aria-current="page"
    - @click ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Makes an item without a destination act as a button
    - class (string) — Applied to the li element

### Overview
Breadcrumb renders a nav landmark named "Breadcrumb" around an ordered list. An item with href or to is a link (internal paths use the link component from PlexUIProvider, such as vue-router's RouterLink); the current item is plain text marked aria-current="page". iOS has no breadcrumb, a phone screen goes back with the back button of its Toolbar. A trail kept on a phone takes the 15/20 text of iOS and gives each link a 44px touch target.
```vue
<Breadcrumb>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Settings</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem current>Members</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumb>
```

### Separator
A small chevron stands between items by default. The separator slot replaces it, such as with a slash for file paths. Separators are hidden from assistive technology.
```vue
<Breadcrumb>
  <template #separator>/</template>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">acme</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">design-system</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem current>README.md</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumb>
```

### Long trails
A trail that does not fit wraps onto the next line instead of leaving the screen. An item without href is a plain step; listen to @click to make it act as a button.
```vue
<div class="w-[260px]">
  <Breadcrumb>
    <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Workspace</BreadcrumbItem>
    <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Customer accounts</BreadcrumbItem>
    <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Northwind Traders</BreadcrumbItem>
    <BreadcrumbItem>Archived</BreadcrumbItem>
    <BreadcrumbItem current>Invoice 2024-0193</BreadcrumbItem>
  </Breadcrumb>
</div>
```

### Sizes
Use size="sm" for a trail above a compact header; the default md sits above a page title. On a phone both sizes take the 15/20 text of iOS, so this example is desktop only.
```vue
<Breadcrumb size="sm">
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Projects</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Website</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem current>Deployments</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumb>
<Breadcrumb>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Projects</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Website</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem current>Deployments</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumb>
```

## Button
Button with four variants (solid, soft, outline, ghost), eight semantic colors, icon slots, loading, and block layout, and ButtonLink, a link styled as a button.
Import: import { Button, ButtonLink } from "@plexui/vue/components/Button"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/button
Props:
  - color ("primary" | "secondary" | "danger" | "success" | "info" | "discovery" | "caution" | "warning") default: "primary" — Visual color scheme
  - variant ("solid" | "soft" | "outline" | "ghost") default: "solid" — Button style variant
  - size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl" | "3xl") default: "md" — Control height and default gutter/icon size
  - mobileMaterial ("glass" | "flat") — Phone surface for neutral soft/ghost buttons
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded corners
  - block (boolean) default: false — Full width
  - uniform (boolean) default: false — Square icon button (width equals height)
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Visually and accessibly disabled; click is not emitted
  - disabled-tone ("relaxed") — Default cursor instead of not-allowed while disabled
  - loading (boolean) default: false — Shows loading indicator
  - inert (boolean) default: loading — Accessibly disabled without visual change
  - selected (boolean) default: false — Selected state styling
  - optically-align ("start" | "end") — Offset gutter for optical alignment
  - gutter-size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl") — Horizontal padding size; defaults from size
  - icon-size ("sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl") — Size of icons in the slot; defaults from size
  - type ("button" | "submit" | "reset") default: "button" — Native button type
  - @click ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Emitted on click, unless disabled
  ButtonLink:
    - default slot (VNode[]) — Content inside the link button
    - color ("primary" | "secondary" | "danger" | "success" | "info" | "discovery" | "caution" | "warning") default: "primary" — Visual color
    - variant ("solid" | "soft" | "outline" | "ghost") default: "solid" — Style variant
    - size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl" | "3xl") default: "md" — Control height and typography scale
    - gutter-size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl") — Override horizontal padding
    - icon-size ("sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl") — Override icon size
    - href (string) — Link URL
    - to (string | RouteLocationRaw) — Router link destination (internal)
    - as (Component) — Link component override (e.g. RouterLink, NuxtLink)
    - external (boolean) — Force external-link behavior
    - optically-align ("start" | "end") — Apply optical negative margin alignment
    - block (boolean) default: false — Full width
    - mobileMaterial ("glass" | "flat") — Phone surface for neutral soft/ghost buttons
    - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable link
    - disabled-tone ("relaxed") — Use relaxed visual tone for disabled state
    - @click ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Emitted on click, unless disabled

### Overview
One button with every setting. color and variant style it: eight semantic colors, each solid, soft, outline or ghost. On a phone with touch.css iOS draws solid as Filled, soft as Tinted, or Gray in secondary, and ghost as Plain; outline is Plex UI's own. The Phone preview offers the colors of iOS buttons: accent (primary), gray (secondary) and destructive (danger). size sets the control height, and gutter-size the horizontal padding. Each size provides default values for gutter-size and icon-size, as well as font-size; each can be overridden on its own. pill rounds the corners fully and adds horizontal gutter. Button shares the unified 9-step size scale with Input, Select, SelectControl and SegmentedControl. On a phone with touch.css a button takes one of the three heights of iOS buttons: small 28 (3xs to sm), medium 34 (md, lg) and large 50 (xl to 3xl), always a capsule: the small one with a 15px label and 10px gutters, the medium and large ones with a 17px label and 12 and 20px gutters, as iOS 27.2 sets them, so the Phone preview offers sm, md and xl and neither pill nor gutter-size. Without size a button is md, the medium one; a screen's actions take xl. The label is Medium (510) and Regular on a ghost button, as the iOS 27 kit sets its buttons on glass and its plain ones, and as Apple's apps set a filled action apart from a plain one. Icons go in the default slot: icon here puts one before the label, after it, or alone, when the button needs an aria-label. Their size is automatic, from size, unless icon-size sets it. For a button of an icon alone, uniform makes it square and ignores gutter-size; with pill it is a circle. On a desktop a smaller gutter-size than size makes a well-sized icon button that is not exactly square. On a phone with touch.css the icon follows the three iOS sizes unless icon-size is set: 3xs to sm show the 18px icon and md to 3xl the 20px one, as iOS sets a button's symbol at the size of its label (15 and 17px). A button of an icon alone is the kit's symbol button, a circle as tall as a text button of its size, 28, 34 or 50px, with or without uniform, and gutter-size does not change it, so the Phone preview does not offer uniform. A toolbar, the bars of Page and Dialog, a ButtonGroup of uniform buttons alone and a button beside a field keep the 44px circle of a bar button in the kit (50px at 3xl). Between an icon and a label a button leaves 8px at every size, as iOS 27.2 does. block makes the button as wide as its container, a 290px column here. On a phone a screen's main action is the large button as wide as the screen, xl and block; the example stands at the top of the screen, next to its controls. disabled disables the button visually and accessibly. loading shows a spinner and prevents interaction through its default inert state; the controls show these props and start no asynchronous action. inert blocks a button without any visual change: it applies disabled for accessibility, and the look of a disabled button comes only from [data-disabled]. On a phone a disabled button labels in the tertiary label of iOS on the gray of a gray button, as iOS 27.2 draws a disabled filled or gray button; a ghost one has no fill, an outline one keeps its border with no fill, and a glass one keeps the glass. A loading button shows the iOS spinner there, 18px in the small button and 20px in the medium and large ones. selected shows that a button is open or selected; each variant shows it its own way. Menu and Popover set it on their trigger while open, through [aria-expanded="true"] or [data-state="open"]. iOS buttons show no selected state, so the Phone preview does not offer it; the prop still applies there.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { ArrowRight, Mail } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';

const color = ref<'primary' | 'secondary' | 'danger' | 'info' | 'discovery' | 'success' | 'caution' | 'warning'>('primary');
const variant = ref<'solid' | 'soft' | 'outline' | 'ghost'>('solid');
const size = ref<'3xs' | '2xs' | 'xs' | 'sm' | 'md' | 'lg' | 'xl' | '2xl' | '3xl'>('md');
const pill = ref(false);
const icon = ref<'none' | 'start' | 'end' | 'only'>('none');
const uniform = ref(false);
const iconSize = ref<'sm' | 'md' | 'lg' | 'xl' | '2xl'>();
const gutterSize = ref<'2xs' | 'xs' | 'sm' | 'md' | 'lg' | 'xl'>();
const block = ref(false);
const disabled = ref(false);
const loading = ref(false);
const selected = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
  <Button
    :color="color"
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :pill="pill"
    :uniform="uniform"
    :gutter-size="gutterSize"
    :icon-size="iconSize"
    :block="block"
    :disabled="disabled"
    :loading="loading"
    :selected="selected"
    :aria-label="icon === 'only' ? 'Send' : undefined"
  >
    <Mail v-if="icon === 'start' || icon === 'only'" />
    <span v-if="icon !== 'only'">Send</span>
    <ArrowRight v-if="icon === 'end'" />
  </Button>
</template>
```

### Link
ButtonLink is a link styled as a button: an &lt;a&gt;, or your router's link, for an action that goes somewhere, such as another page, where a Button does something in place. A link opens in a new tab with a modifier click, shows its address, and is announced as a link. It takes the settings of the Overview but uniform, loading and selected, and on a phone it is the same iOS button. A relative href or to goes through the link component configured on PlexUIProvider, so your router handles navigation (see ButtonLink below). An absolute URL, or external, renders an &lt;a&gt; with target="_blank" and rel="noopener noreferrer"; set external when a link is not identified on its own. The example's arrow then points up and out, as an external link's does. disabled disables the link visually and accessibly: it renders a span[role="link"], and click is not emitted.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { ButtonLink } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { ArrowRight, ArrowUpRight } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';

const external = ref(false);
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
  <ButtonLink href="#" :external="external" :disabled="disabled">
    View API Keys
    <ArrowUpRight v-if="external" />
    <ArrowRight v-else />
  </ButtonLink>
</template>
```

### Optical alignment
Use optically-align with either "start" or "end" to offset the gutter of the button. On a phone a button under text starts its label where the text does, as Forgot your password? under the Apple Account sign-in, whose text starts 40px from the screen's edge; a soft button shows its capsule reaching past the text. The button is as wide as its label: a full-width button centers its label, which leaves nothing to align.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="flex flex-col gap-3">
  <div class="border border-dashed border-alpha/20 rounded-md py-4 px-6">
    <div class="mb-2 text-secondary text-sm">Default gutters</div>
    <Button variant="ghost">Ghost button</Button>
  </div>
  <div class="border border-dashed border-alpha/20 rounded-md py-4 px-6">
    <div class="mb-2 text-secondary text-sm">optically-align="start"</div>
    <Button variant="ghost" optically-align="start">Ghost button</Button>
  </div>
</div>

<!-- Phone: the label starts where the text above it does, 40px from the screen's edge -->
<div class="flex w-full flex-col items-start gap-4 px-6">
  <p class="text-[17px] leading-[22px] text-secondary">Sign in with your email or phone number to continue.</p>
  <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" size="xl" :optically-align="opticallyAlign === 'start' ? 'start' : undefined">Forgot your password?</Button>
</div>
```

## Button Group
Join buttons into a single visual unit for toolbars, steppers, split buttons and search affordances.
Import: import { ButtonGroup, ButtonGroupSeparator, ButtonGroupText } from "@plexui/vue/components/ButtonGroup"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/button-group
Props:
  - orientation ("horizontal" | "vertical") default: "horizontal" — Direction of the joined items
  - mobile-material ("glass" | "flat") — Phone surface with touch.css: "glass" makes the group one glass capsule outside a Toolbar, and a group that holds a field comes apart into glass shapes; "flat" keeps the joined buttons in one
  - class (string) — Applied to the root <div>
  ButtonGroupText:
    - as-child (boolean) default: false — Render the slotted child (e.g. a <label> tied to an input) instead of a <div>
    - class (string) — Applied to the wrapper

### Overview
Set size on every Button inside the group so corner radii match; the size setting here sets all of them. Mix icon-only (uniform) with text buttons; each child keeps its own padding. Place a ButtonGroup inside a ButtonGroup to make sub-groups, as Back, Pin and Tag, Bookmark and More here, or a pager's pages beside its arrows: the outer group sets a gap between them (--button-group-nested-gap, 8px) and each sub-group stays joined. On a phone with touch.css a group in the content keeps one height. Beside text buttons its icon-only buttons take the heights of the iOS small (28px, sm) and medium (34px, md and lg) buttons; a group of icon-only buttons keeps their 44px, and beside a text field or a select every button takes the field's 44px. Joined segments have no iOS control of their own. On a phone the example is the bars of a note, as in iOS Notes: Back alone, the note's actions in one glass capsule on top, the editing tools in one at the bottom beside New Note. A ButtonGroup in a Toolbar is that capsule, as iOS 27.2 sets it: 44px buttons 10px apart in a 154px capsule on top, 48px ones 8px apart in a 164px capsule at the bottom, the symbols in the primary label color. Groups side by side stand apart as the bar's items do. The bar sets its buttons' size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup>
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size" uniform aria-label="Go back">
      <ArrowLeftSm />
    </Button>
  </ButtonGroup>
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size">Pin</Button>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size">Tag</Button>
  </ButtonGroup>
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size">Bookmark</Button>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size" uniform aria-label="More options">
      <DotsHorizontal />
    </Button>
  </ButtonGroup>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: the bars of a note -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <ButtonGroup aria-label="Note actions">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Pin"><Pin /></Button>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Tag"><Tag /></Button>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>
    <div class="flex flex-col gap-3 text-[17px] leading-[22px]">
      <p class="text-[28px] leading-[34px] font-semibold">Weekend trip</p>
      …
    </div>
  </PageContent>
  <Toolbar placement="bottom">
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <ButtonGroup aria-label="Editing tools">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Checklist"><NotebookCheck /></Button>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Attach"><Clip /></Button>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Markup"><Pens /></Button>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="New note"><PencilSquare /></Button>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
```

### Orientation
Pass orientation="vertical" to stack the buttons, useful for + / − steppers, zoom controls, or any vertical toolbar. On a phone the example is zoom controls floating over a canvas, as map controls do: with mobile-material="glass" the group is the vertical Button Group of the iOS 27 kit, a 48px wide capsule of 48px buttons 6px apart. Each button takes its share of the gap and the padding, so its pressed shape fills its part of the capsule and the first and last are as round as its ends. A phone takes the large plus and minus (PlusLg, MinusLg) with icon-size="sm", the plus of an iOS 27.2 bar: about 18px of ink with a 2px stroke. In the content a group of icon-only buttons keeps their 44px at each size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup orientation="vertical" aria-label="Zoom controls">
  <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size" uniform aria-label="Zoom in">
    <PlusSm />
  </Button>
  <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size" uniform aria-label="Zoom out">
    <Minus />
  </Button>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: zoom controls over a canvas -->
<ButtonGroup orientation="vertical" mobile-material="glass" aria-label="Zoom controls" class="absolute top-2 right-4">
  <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill icon-size="sm" aria-label="Zoom in"><PlusLg /></Button>
  <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill icon-size="sm" aria-label="Zoom out"><MinusLg /></Button>
</ButtonGroup>
```

### Input group
Richer composition for message composers: wrap each slot (leading action, flexible Input) in its own ButtonGroup so the outer group inserts a gap between them. The voice toggle rides inside the input via the endAdornment slot. Use InputAdornmentButton: it sizes off the input's own size token so the button stays concentric with the pill at every size. On a phone the example is the composer of iOS Messages at the bottom of the screen. With mobile-material="glass" a group that holds a field comes apart: the plus is a 40px glass circle with the large plus (PlusLg, icon-size="sm") and the field a 40px glass capsule with 16px gutters and 17px text, 12px apart, as iOS 27.2 sets them. The voice button inside the field keeps its 44px target without growing the field. The group sets the sizes, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup style="width: 100%; max-width: 280px">
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" pill :size="size" uniform aria-label="Add attachment">
      <Plus />
    </Button>
  </ButtonGroup>
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Input
      pill
      :size="size"
      :placeholder="voiceEnabled ? 'Recording your idea…' : 'Describe a component…'"
      :disabled="voiceEnabled"
    >
      <template #endAdornment>
        <InputAdornmentButton
          :color="voiceEnabled ? 'caution' : undefined"
          aria-label="Toggle voice mode"
          :aria-pressed="voiceEnabled"
          @click="voiceEnabled = !voiceEnabled"
        >
          <Mic />
        </InputAdornmentButton>
      </template>
    </Input>
  </ButtonGroup>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: the composer at the bottom of the screen -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarTitle>New Message</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <NavigationButton action="close" />
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>…</PageContent>
  <PageFooter>
    <ButtonGroup mobile-material="glass" aria-label="Message">
      <ButtonGroup>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill icon-size="sm" aria-label="Add attachment"><PlusLg /></Button>
      </ButtonGroup>
      <ButtonGroup>
        <Input
          pill
          aria-label="Message"
          :placeholder="voiceEnabled ? 'Listening…' : 'Text Message'"
          :disabled="voiceEnabled"
        >
          <template #endAdornment>
            <InputAdornmentButton
              :color="voiceEnabled ? 'caution' : undefined"
              aria-label="Toggle voice mode"
              :aria-pressed="voiceEnabled"
              @click="voiceEnabled = !voiceEnabled"
            >
              <Mic />
            </InputAdornmentButton>
          </template>
        </Input>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ButtonGroup>
  </PageFooter>
</Page>
```

### Separator
Drop a ButtonGroupSeparator between items to add a visible divider, commonly used for Undo / Redo pairs or any "two related actions" toolbar. The same divider makes the classic split button: an action joined to a secondary affordance, as Save / Save As, Filter / Add filter or Delete / Undo. On a phone the example is Undo and Redo in the top bar of a drawing, as iOS 27.2 sets a document's bar: Back, the title, and the group as one glass capsule. The capsule of iOS draws no line between its buttons, so in a glass group the separator draws nothing. The bar sets its buttons' size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup>
  <Button variant="soft" color="secondary" :size="size"><Undo /> Undo</Button>
  <ButtonGroupSeparator />
  <Button variant="soft" color="secondary" :size="size">Redo <Redo /></Button>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: the top bar of a drawing -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Sketch</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <ButtonGroup aria-label="History">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Undo"><Undo /></Button>
        <ButtonGroupSeparator />
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Redo"><Redo /></Button>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>…</PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Input
Join an Input to a trailing Button for inline search, token entry, or URL shorteners. The input flexes to fill the row; the button hugs its content. On a phone the example is the bottom bar of a list, as iOS 27.2 Contacts holds its search field and New Contact. In a Toolbar a group that holds a field comes apart: the field is a glass capsule and the button a glass circle, both the bar's 48px and 12px apart, as the bar's items. A phone searches as you type, so the button beside the field is an action of its own; icon-size="sm" draws the large plus of iOS. The bar sets the sizes, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup>
  <Input placeholder="Search docs..." :size="size" />
  <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size" aria-label="Search">
    <SearchSm />
  </Button>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: the bottom bar of a list -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>…</PageContent>
  <Toolbar placement="bottom">
    <ToolbarContent fill>
      <ButtonGroup aria-label="Search orders">
        <Input variant="soft" pill placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders">
          <template #startAdornment>
            <SearchSm />
          </template>
        </Input>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill icon-size="sm" aria-label="New order"><PlusLg /></Button>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ToolbarContent>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
```

### Dropdown menu
Compose a primary action with a Menu trigger for follow-up options. Common for Export / Format, Save / Save-As, Share / …. On a phone the action and its menu are symbols in one glass capsule of the top bar, as iOS 27.2 groups a bar's symbols (Add, Share and More in one glass). iOS never puts a label and a symbol in one capsule, and a bar opens a menu from More (…), not from a chevron: here Share exports, More opens the formats. The bar sets its buttons' size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup>
  <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :pill="false" :size="size">Export</Button>
  <Menu>
    <MenuTrigger>
      <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :pill="false" :size="size" uniform aria-label="More export options">
        <ChevronDownMd />
      </Button>
    </MenuTrigger>
    <MenuContent align="end" :min-width="200">
      <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileImage /> PNG image</MenuItem>
      <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileCode /> SVG vector</MenuItem>
      <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileDocument /> PDF document</MenuItem>
      <MenuSeparator />
      <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Copy /> Copy as code</MenuItem>
      <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Link /> Copy share link</MenuItem>
    </MenuContent>
  </Menu>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: in the top bar of a report -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Report</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <ButtonGroup aria-label="Export">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Export"><Share /></Button>
        <Menu>
          <MenuTrigger>
            <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More export options"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
          </MenuTrigger>
          <MenuContent align="end" :min-width="200">
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileImage /> PNG image</MenuItem>
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileCode /> SVG vector</MenuItem>
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileDocument /> PDF document</MenuItem>
            <MenuSeparator />
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Copy /> Copy as code</MenuItem>
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Link /> Copy share link</MenuItem>
          </MenuContent>
        </Menu>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>…</PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Select
Mix a Select and an Input inside one ButtonGroup for unit pickers, currency entry, or any "qualifier + value" pattern. Wrap each slot in a nested ButtonGroup to get a gap between the compound field and the send affordance. iOS has no group of a menu, a field and a button, in a bar or in the content. A phone form sets a value and its qualifier as rows of a grouped list: the field is the value of its Field row, the unit is the pop-up at the end of its row, as on the Select page, and the bar's confirm takes the place of the send button. The rows set the sizes, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup>
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Select
      :options="unitOptions"
      v-model:value="unit"
      variant="outline"
      :size="size"
      :block="false"
    />
    <Input placeholder="2.50" :size="size" inputmode="decimal" />
  </ButtonGroup>
  <ButtonGroup>
    <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :size="size" uniform aria-label="Log weight">
      <ArrowRight />
    </Button>
  </ButtonGroup>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: a form of rows; the bar's confirm logs the weight -->
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="close" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Weight</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <NavigationButton action="confirm" aria-label="Log weight" />
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>
    <FieldGroup>
      <Field row label="Weight">
        <Input placeholder="2.50" inputmode="decimal" />
      </Field>
      <Field row label="Unit">
        <Select :options="unitNames" v-model:value="unit" :block="false" align="end" />
      </Field>
    </FieldGroup>
  </PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Popover
Same split-button shape, but the trailing trigger opens a Popover instead of a menu, useful for richer panels (prompt composers, notification preferences, link previews). On a phone, as the Dropdown menu, the action and More are symbols in one glass capsule of the top bar. A panel of fields from a bar button is a sheet on a phone (Dialog with mobile-variant="sheet"), as the Column configuration of Popover opens: its header and body set the insets, and the title takes focus so no keyboard comes up. Its field is a row of a grouped list, as the Edit profile sheet of Dialog: the text area is a vertical Field row with a ghost Textarea, the note is the list's footer and the header's confirm runs it. The bar sets its buttons' size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<ButtonGroup>
  <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :pill="false" :size="size"><Sparkles /> Generate</Button>
  <Popover>
    <PopoverTrigger>
      <Button variant="outline" color="secondary" :pill="false" :size="size" uniform aria-label="More generate options">
        <ChevronDownMd />
      </Button>
    </PopoverTrigger>
    <PopoverContent :min-width="320" side="bottom" align="end" class="p-4">
      <Card variant="ghost" size="sm">
        <CardHeader>
          <CardTitle>Generate a component</CardTitle>
          <CardDescription>Describe what to build in plain English.</CardDescription>
        </CardHeader>
        <CardContent>
          <Textarea placeholder="A login form with email and password…" :rows="3" />
        </CardContent>
        <CardFooter>
          <CardDescription>Plex will draft the code and preview it live.</CardDescription>
        </CardFooter>
      </Card>
    </PopoverContent>
  </Popover>
</ButtonGroup>

<!-- Phone: in the top bar of a draft; More opens a sheet -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Draft</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <ButtonGroup aria-label="Generate">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Generate"><Sparkles /></Button>
        <Dialog v-model:open="open">
          <DialogTrigger>
            <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More generate options"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
          </DialogTrigger>
          <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" surface="grouped" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })">
            <DialogHeader>
              <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>Generate a component</DialogTitle></div>
              <DialogDescription class="sr-only">Describe what to build in plain English.</DialogDescription>
              <DialogHeaderAction>
                <NavigationButton action="confirm" aria-label="Generate" @click="open = false" />
              </DialogHeaderAction>
            </DialogHeader>
            <DialogBody>
              <FieldGroup>
                <Field row orientation="vertical" label="Component" hide-label>
                  <Textarea variant="ghost" placeholder="A login form with email and password…" :rows="3" />
                </Field>
                <template #footer>Describe what to build in plain English.</template>
              </FieldGroup>
            </DialogBody>
          </DialogContent>
        </Dialog>
      </ButtonGroup>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>…</PageContent>
</Page>
```

## Card
Card surface for composing login flows, sign-up forms, pricing tiles, payment methods, empty states, and stat showcases.
Import: import { Card, CardAction, CardContent, CardDescription, CardFooter, CardHeader, CardTitle } from "@plexui/vue/components/Card"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/card
Props:
  - variant ("outline" | "solid" | "ghost") default: "outline" — Visual variant
  - size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Padding scale
  - interactive (boolean) default: false — Adds hover and focus affordances. Automatic when href is set.
  - href (string) — Renders the Card as a link via the linkComponent of PlexUIProvider. A link component that takes to, such as RouterLink, gets it as to.
  - external (boolean) default: auto-detected — Forces target="_blank" and rel="noopener noreferrer" when href is set.
  - as (Component | "a") — Overrides the link component.
  - class (string) — Applied to the root element

### Overview
```vue
<Card style="max-width: 380px">
  <CardHeader>
    <CardTitle>Project activity</CardTitle>
    <CardDescription>
      Your team opened 12 issues and closed 8 pull requests over the last 7 days.
    </CardDescription>
  </CardHeader>
  <CardFooter>
    <Button color="primary" variant="solid" size="sm">View report</Button>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" size="sm">Dismiss</Button>
  </CardFooter>
</Card>
```

### Login
A classic login form with a primary Log in CTA and an inline Sign up link below.
```vue
<Card style="max-width: 380px">
  <CardHeader>
    <CardTitle>Log in to your account</CardTitle>
    <CardDescription>Enter your email below to log in to your account.</CardDescription>
  </CardHeader>
  <CardContent>
    <div style="display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 20px">
      <Field label="Email">
        <Input type="email" placeholder="you@example.com" />
      </Field>
      <div style="display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 6px">
        <div style="display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: space-between; gap: 8px">
          <Label for="card-login-password">Password</Label>
          <TextLink href="#" primary>Forgot your password?</TextLink>
        </div>
        <Input id="card-login-password" type="password" />
      </div>
    </div>
  </CardContent>
  <CardFooter style="flex-direction: column; align-items: stretch; gap: 12px">
    <Button color="primary" variant="solid" block>Log in</Button>
    <!-- Description sits flush under a title by default; here it follows a button -->
    <CardDescription class="text-center" style="margin: 0">
      Don't have an account?
      <TextLink href="#" primary>Sign up</TextLink>
    </CardDescription>
  </CardFooter>
</Card>
```
