## Checkbox
Checkbox for binary on/off selection with solid, pill, and ghost variants, an indeterminate state, and rich labels with inline descriptions.
Import: import { Checkbox } from "@plexui/vue/components/Checkbox"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/checkbox
Props:
  - variant ("solid" | "ghost") default: "solid" — Visual style: solid (filled background) or ghost (checkmark only)
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded indicator corners
  - checked / v-model:checked (boolean | "indeterminate") — Controlled state
  - default-checked (boolean | "indeterminate") — Initial state (uncontrolled)
  - label (string) — Accessible label rendered next to the checkbox
  - label slot (VNode[]) — Rich label content; takes precedence over the label prop
  - description (string) — Second line below the label, linked via aria-describedby (or the description slot)
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Prevent interaction
  - required (boolean) — Required for form submission
  - name (string) — Form field name
  - value (string) default: "on" — Value when submitted
  - orientation ("left" | "right") default: "left" — Checkbox position relative to the label
  - class (string) — CSS classes applied to the wrapper node
  - id (string) — The id of the checkbox element (generated when omitted)
  - @update:checked ((next: boolean | "indeterminate") => void) — Emitted with the next state when it changes (a click always emits a boolean)
  - @focus ((event: FocusEvent) => void) — Emitted when the checkbox gains focus
  - @blur ((event: FocusEvent) => void) — Emitted when the checkbox loses focus

### Overview
A checkbox is checked, unchecked or indeterminate. Pass checked="indeterminate", or default-checked="indeterminate", for a partly selected state: a "select all" box whose items are only partly checked, as in Group with select all. solid (default) fills the box; ghost renders only the checkmark, in the text color, without a border or background, for subtle inline selections or task lists. pill rounds the box (border-radius: full): the solid fill, and on a ghost box its hover tint and focus ring. Use description (or the description slot) for a second line under the label. It sits outside the label, so the checkbox keeps the label as its name and gets the description through aria-describedby. By default the box is on the left of its label; orientation="right" places it after the label. Both act only next to a label, so they are offered only with one. Without a label the checkbox is a standalone control: pass aria-label to name it. disabled keeps the checked and indeterminate states. On a phone with touch.css a solid checkbox is the 22px selection circle of iOS with or without pill, so the phone view has no pill setting. A ghost checkbox is the checkmark of an iOS list row.
```vue
<div :class="label ? (description ? 'flex flex-col gap-4 max-w-sm' : 'flex flex-col gap-3') : 'flex gap-4'">
  <Checkbox
    v-for="option in [
      { label: 'Share usage data', description: 'Help improve the product with anonymous usage reports.', defaultChecked: true },
      { label: 'Enable notifications', description: 'You can enable or disable notifications at any time from settings.', defaultChecked: false },
      { label: 'Sync all folders', description: 'Some of its folders are synced.', defaultChecked: 'indeterminate' },
    ]"
    :key="option.label"
    :variant="variant"
    :pill="pill"
    :disabled="disabled"
    :default-checked="option.defaultChecked"
    :label="label ? option.label : undefined"
    :description="label && description ? option.description : undefined"
    :orientation="orientation"
    :aria-label="label ? undefined : option.label"
  />
</div>
```

### Group with select all
Combine a parent checkbox with child checkboxes to create a "select all" pattern. The parent shows an indeterminate state when only some children are checked. Each child binds v-model:checked to its item in items.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col gap-3">
  <Checkbox
    label="Permissions"
    :checked="items.every((i) => i.checked) ? true : items.some((i) => i.checked) ? 'indeterminate' : false"
    @update:checked="(next) => items.forEach((i) => (i.checked = next))"
  />
  <div class="flex flex-col gap-3 pl-6">
    <Checkbox
      v-for="item in items"
      :key="item.id"
      :label="item.label"
      v-model:checked="item.checked"
    />
  </div>
</div>
```

### Group with description
Wrap a group of checkboxes in a Field for a shared label and description. This is a common pattern for preference lists and settings panels. On a phone the group is a FieldGroup whose title and description name the list, with a row for each option.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="max-w-sm w-full">
  <Field label="Download over cellular" description="Choose what can use mobile data." v-slot="{ id }">
    <div class="flex flex-col gap-3">
      <Checkbox v-for="item in items" :key="item.id" :label="item.label" v-model:checked="item.checked" />
    </div>
  </Field>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup title="Download over cellular" description="Choose what can use mobile data.">
  <Field v-for="item in items" :key="item.id" row :label="item.label"><Checkbox v-model:checked="item.checked" /></Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### With Field
Wrap checkboxes in a Field component for form layout with label, description, and error message support. A single control passed as the child gets aria-describedby and aria-invalid automatically; with v-slot the slot props carry them, so bind them onto the control that should carry them. On a phone the options are rows of a FieldGroup and a FieldError in its footer shows the message; link it to the checkbox it is about with aria-describedby.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="flex flex-col gap-4 max-w-sm w-full">
  <Field
    label="Terms"
    description="Please review and accept the terms before continuing."
    :error-message="showError ? 'You must accept the terms to continue.' : undefined"
    v-slot="{ id }"
  >
    <div class="flex flex-col gap-3 mt-1">
      <Checkbox label="I agree to the Terms of Service" v-model:checked="agreed" />
      <Checkbox label="I agree to the Privacy Policy" />
    </div>
  </Field>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup title="Terms" description="Please review and accept the terms before continuing.">
  <Field row label="I agree to the Terms of Service">
    <Checkbox
      v-model:checked="agreed"
      :aria-invalid="showError || undefined"
      :aria-describedby="showError ? 'terms-error' : undefined"
    />
  </Field>
  <Field row label="I agree to the Privacy Policy"><Checkbox /></Field>
  <template v-if="showError" #footer>
    <FieldError id="terms-error">You must accept the terms to continue.</FieldError>
  </template>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Grouped sections
Use dividers to separate checkbox groups into logical sections. Each section can be wrapped in a Field with its own label and description.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped" class="h-dvh" data-form-screen style="--page-content-padding-top:10px; --page-content-padding-bottom:16px; --page-footer-gutter:24px; --page-footer-padding-bottom:max(24px, env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px))">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading><DemoBackButton /></ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Notifications</ToolbarTitle>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent data-form-content>
    <form ref="screenForm" class="flex flex-col gap-6" @submit.prevent="" >
      <FieldGroup title="Email notifications" description="Configure which emails you receive.">
        <Field row label="Marketing" description="Receive emails about new products and features."><Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.emailMarketing" /></Field>
        <Field row label="Security" description="Receive emails about account activity and security."><Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.emailSecurity" /></Field>
      </FieldGroup>
      <FieldGroup title="Push notifications" description="Configure which push notifications you receive.">
        <Field row label="Direct messages"><Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.pushMessages" /></Field>
        <Field row label="Mentions"><Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.pushMentions" /></Field>
        <Field row label="Comments"><Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.pushComments" /></Field>
      </FieldGroup>
    </form>
  </PageContent>

</Page>
```

### With Radio Group
Place Checkbox alongside RadioGroup controls to verify that indicator sizes, label baselines, and spacing align consistently in both horizontal and vertical layouts. This check is desktop only: on a phone both live in rows, where the radio is a checkmark and the checkbox a circle at the end of the row.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col gap-6 w-full max-w-md">
  <Field label="Notification channel" description="Choose how you'd like to be notified." v-slot="{ id }">
    <RadioGroup :direction="direction" default-value="email" aria-label="Notification channel">
      <RadioGroupItem
        v-for="channel in [
          { value: 'email', label: 'Email', description: 'Messages delivered to your inbox.' },
          { value: 'sms', label: 'SMS', description: 'Text messages to your phone number.' },
          { value: 'push', label: 'Push', description: 'Real-time alerts on your device.' },
        ]"
        :key="channel.value"
        :value="channel.value"
      >
        <div v-if="description === 'true'" class="flex flex-col">
          <span class="font-medium">{{ channel.label }}</span>
          <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">{{ channel.description }}</span>
        </div>
        <template v-else>{{ channel.label }}</template>
      </RadioGroupItem>
    </RadioGroup>
  </Field>
  <Field label="Topics" description="Select the topics you want to follow." v-slot="{ id }">
    <div :class="direction === 'row' ? 'flex flex-row gap-5' : 'flex flex-col gap-2.5'">
      <Checkbox
        v-for="topic in [
          { label: 'Product updates', description: 'New features and improvements.', checked: true },
          { label: 'Security alerts', description: 'Account activity and login notifications.', checked: true },
          { label: 'Community', description: 'Events, meetups, and discussions.', checked: false },
        ]"
        :key="topic.label"
        :default-checked="topic.checked"
      >
        <template #label>
          <div v-if="description === 'true'" class="flex flex-col">
            <span class="font-medium">{{ topic.label }}</span>
            <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">{{ topic.description }}</span>
          </div>
          <template v-else>{{ topic.label }}</template>
        </template>
      </Checkbox>
    </div>
  </Field>
</div>
```

### Descriptions
Combine Field-level label and description with item-level descriptions for options that need additional context. Use a plain label without descriptions when the options are self-explanatory. On a phone the group title, its description and row descriptions do this, as Group with description and Grouped sections show, so this example is desktop only.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col max-w-sm w-full divide-y divide-alpha/15">
  <div class="pb-5">
    <Field label="Export contents" description="Choose what the archive includes." v-slot="{ id }">
      <div class="flex flex-col gap-2.5">
        <Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.projects">
          <template #label>
            <div class="flex flex-col">
              <span class="font-medium">Projects</span>
              <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">Files, comments, and version history.</span>
            </div>
          </template>
        </Checkbox>
        <Checkbox v-model:checked="settings.members">
          <template #label>
            <div class="flex flex-col">
              <span class="font-medium">Members</span>
              <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">Names, roles, and email addresses.</span>
            </div>
          </template>
        </Checkbox>
      </div>
    </Field>
  </div>
  <div class="pt-5">
    <Field label="File formats" v-slot="{ id }">
      <div class="flex flex-col gap-3">
        <Checkbox label="CSV" v-model:checked="settings.csv" />
        <Checkbox label="JSON" v-model:checked="settings.json" />
        <Checkbox label="PDF" v-model:checked="settings.pdf" />
      </div>
    </Field>
  </div>
</div>
```

## Code Block
Code block with syntax highlighting, a copy button, and collapsible long snippets with configurable expanded height limits.
Import: import { CodeBlock } from "@plexui/vue/components/CodeBlock"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/code-block
Props:
  - default slot (string) — Code content (text only; interpolate a string to keep its whitespace)
  - code (string) — Code content, used when the default slot is empty
  - language (string) — Prism language name
  - class (string) — Additional wrapper class
  CollapsibleCodeBlock:
    - default slot / code (string) — Code content
    - language (string) — Prism language name
    - default-expanded (boolean) default: false — Start expanded
    - collapsed-height (number) default: 140 — Height when collapsed (px)
    - max-expanded-height (number) — Max height when expanded (px); taller code scrolls
    - copyable (boolean) default: true — Show copy button
    - class (string) — Additional wrapper class

### Overview
The code is the text of the default slot. Interpolate a string ({"{{ code }}"}) so its whitespace is kept, or pass it with the code prop.
```vue
<CodeBlock language="javascript">{{ codeSnippet }}</CodeBlock>
```

### Collapsible
Use CollapsibleCodeBlock to display code in a compact view with gradient overlay and an expand action.
```vue
<CollapsibleCodeBlock language="tsx">{{ code }}</CollapsibleCodeBlock>
```

### Expanded by default
```vue
<CollapsibleCodeBlock language="tsx" default-expanded>{{ code }}</CollapsibleCodeBlock>
```

### Custom collapsed height
```vue
<CollapsibleCodeBlock language="typescript" :collapsed-height="60">{{ code }}</CollapsibleCodeBlock>
```

### Max expanded height
```vue
<CollapsibleCodeBlock language="typescript" default-expanded :max-expanded-height="200">{{ code }}</CollapsibleCodeBlock>
```

### Without copy button
```vue
<CollapsibleCodeBlock language="python" default-expanded :copyable="false">{{ code }}</CollapsibleCodeBlock>
```

### Supported languages
A pragmatic set of Prism grammars are pre-registered out of the box.
```vue
<CodeBlock language="javascript">{{ code }}</CodeBlock>
```

## Color Picker
"The iOS 27.2 system color picker: Grid, Spectrum and Sliders, an Opacity slider and saved colors, and the color well that opens it in a popover or, on a phone, a sheet."
Import: import { ColorPicker, ColorWell } from "@plexui/vue/components/ColorPicker"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/color-picker
Props:
  - value (string) — The controlled color, `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa`; bind with v-model:value
  - default-value (string) default: "#000000" — The color when it is initially rendered
  - supports-alpha (boolean) default: true — Shows the Opacity slider
  - mode ("grid" | "spectrum" | "sliders") — The controlled way to choose a color; bind with v-model:mode
  - default-mode ("grid" | "spectrum" | "sliders") default: "grid" — The way to choose a color when it is initially rendered
  - swatches (string[]) — The controlled saved colors; bind with v-model:swatches
  - default-swatches (string[]) default: [] — The saved colors when it is initially rendered
  - aria-label (string) default: "Colors" — Accessible name of the picker
  ColorWell:
    - value (string | null) — The controlled color, `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa`; bind with v-model:value. `null` shows the well with no color
    - default-value (string | null) default: null — The color when it is initially rendered
    - size ("sm" | "lg") default: "sm" — 28px or 36px
    - title (string) default: "Colors" — The title of the picker
    - supports-eyedropper (boolean) default: true — Shows the eyedropper where the browser has the EyeDropper API
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Prevents opening the picker
    - name (string) — Submits the color with the owning form
    - aria-label (string) default: "Color" — Accessible name of the well
    - aria-labelledby (string) — The `id` of the visible label of the well

### Color well
The well shows the chosen color inside a rainbow ring, or the full rainbow when there is no color yet (null). It opens the picker in a popover from the well, and on a phone in a sheet. The eyedropper appears where the browser can pick a color from the screen; on a phone the sheet's bar has Close first and the eyedropper last, as iOS 27.2 sets them. Both wells here share their saved colors. On a phone the rows sit in an iOS settings screen under its toolbar.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
const tint = ref('#af52de')
const swatches = ref(['#000000', '#007aff', '#34c759'])
</script>

<template>
  <FieldGroup>
    <Field row label="Tint">
      <ColorWell aria-label="Tint" v-model:value="tint" v-model:swatches="swatches" />
    </Field>
  </FieldGroup>
</template>
```

### Sizes and states
The kit's two sizes: sm 28px and lg 36px.
```vue
<div class="flex items-center gap-4">
  <ColorWell aria-label="Small, no color" />
  <ColorWell aria-label="Small" default-value="#af52de" />
  <ColorWell aria-label="Large, no color" size="lg" />
  <ColorWell aria-label="Large" size="lg" default-value="#af52de" />
  <ColorWell aria-label="Disabled" default-value="#007aff" disabled />
</div>
```

### Picker
The picker on its own. Grid shows the kit's 120 colors; Spectrum runs the hues from top to bottom, from white through the full hue to black across; Sliders sets red, green and blue, or a hex color. Add saves the current color; Delete or Backspace, or Delete in a saved color's context menu, removes one. Saved colors fill pages of twelve, two rows of six, that scroll sideways, as in iOS 27.2.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
const color = ref('#be38f3')
</script>

<template>
  <ColorPicker v-model:value="color" :default-swatches="['#000000', '#007aff', '#34c759']" />
</template>
```

### Without opacity
With :supports-alpha="false" there is no Opacity slider, and every color is opaque.
```vue
<ColorPicker default-value="#007aff" default-mode="sliders" :supports-alpha="false" />
```

## Context Menu
"A menu of actions for an element: a secondary click opens it at the pointer, a touch and hold opens it beside the element over a dimmed page, as iOS 26 context menus do. Its items are the ones of Menu."
Import: import { ContextMenu, ContextMenuContent, ContextMenuTrigger } from "@plexui/vue/components/ContextMenu"
Import: import { MenuItem, MenuSeparator } from "@plexui/vue/components/Menu"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/context-menu
Props:
  - force-open (boolean) — Controls the menu: true keeps it open under the element, or beside its lifted preview on a phone, and false keeps it closed
  ContextMenuTrigger:
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — The element keeps the browser's own context menu
  ContextMenuContent:
    - width (number | string) — Defines the width of the menu
    - min-width (number | string) — Defines the min-width of the menu, in pixels
    - max-height (number) — Defines the max-height of the menu, in pixels
    - pill (boolean) default: false — Rounder list with fully rounded rows
    - aria-label (string) — Names the menu for assistive technology, in place of the name of its element

### Overview
Wrap the element in ContextMenuTrigger: it keeps its own role and focus. On a touch screen, hold for half a second: the element stays clear above the dimmed, blurred page and the menu opens 16px below it, as in the iOS 27 kit, on the side where the element stands, as iOS 27.2 places it: at the element's start edge in the first third of the screen, at its end edge in the last third, and centred under it in the middle. The press that opened the menu does not activate the element. An element in the lower half of the screen opens its menu 16px above it where it fits, and stays where it is. When the menu fits on neither side, it opens below and the element moves up with it, as far as the room above allows, and settles back when the menu closes, as a lifted preview of iOS does. A tap on the element while its menu stands does what a tap on it always does, and closes the menu. On a phone the rows sit on the grouped background of an iOS list.
```vue
<ContextMenu>
  <ContextMenuTrigger>
    <div tabindex="0">Groceries</div>
  </ContextMenuTrigger>
  <ContextMenuContent>
    <MenuItem @select="copy"><Copy /> Copy</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="share"><Share /> Share</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="pin"><Pin /> Pin</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem danger @select="remove"><RemoveTrash /> Delete</MenuItem>
  </ContextMenuContent>
</ContextMenu>
```

### Rounded elements
The element is cut out of the dimming with its own corners. An element without corners of its own gets the 30px corner of the kit's preview, at most half its height.
```vue
<ContextMenu>
  <ContextMenuTrigger>
    <div tabindex="0" role="img" aria-label="Photos" class="rounded-2xl">
      <Images />
    </div>
  </ContextMenuTrigger>
  <ContextMenuContent>...</ContextMenuContent>
</ContextMenu>
```

### In a sortable list
A row that has a context menu and drags in a Sortable list takes one touch and hold for both, as a lifted preview of iOS does: the hold opens the menu, and moving the finger on from there closes it and drags the row. A grip shown by handles drags at once and opens no menu. With a mouse, a secondary click opens the menu and a drag moves the row.
```vue
<FieldGroup>
  <Sortable v-model:value="notes">
    <ContextMenu v-for="note in notes" :key="note">
      <ContextMenuTrigger>
        <div data-row tabindex="0">
          {{ note }}
          <SortableHandle :aria-label="'Reorder ' + note" />
        </div>
      </ContextMenuTrigger>
      <ContextMenuContent>...</ContextMenuContent>
    </ContextMenu>
  </Sortable>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Copy Tooltip
A copy-to-clipboard tooltip that wraps any trigger element and confirms when the value has been copied.
Import: import { CopyTooltip } from "@plexui/vue/components/Tooltip"
Import: import { Button } from "@plexui/vue/components/Button"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/copy-tooltip
Props:
  - default slot (VNode) — Trigger element (for example TooltipTriggerDecorator)
  - copy-value (string) — Required. Content to copy to clipboard
  - open-delay (number) default: 150 — Tooltip open delay in milliseconds
  - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Preferred tooltip alignment against the trigger
  - align-offset (number) default: 0 — Offset from start/end alignment in pixels
  - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "top" — Preferred side of the trigger
  - side-offset (number) default: 5 — Distance from trigger in pixels
  - @copy ((copied: boolean) => void) — Emitted once the clipboard write settles, with whether it succeeded. The tooltip says Copied! only after a successful write

### Overview
Hovering the trigger shows Copy after open-delay, keyboard focus shows it at once; a click copies the value and the tooltip says Copied! On a phone a tap copies and shows Copied! at once, so the delay applies to a pointer only.
```vue
<CopyTooltip
  copy-value="sess_12345abcdefg"
  :open-delay="openDelay"
  :side-offset="sideOffset"
  :align-offset="alignOffset"
  :side="side"
  :align="align"
>
  <Button color="secondary" variant="ghost" size="xl" aria-label="Copy session ID">
    sess_12345abcdefg
  </Button>
</CopyTooltip>
```

## Date Input
Masked text input for typing dates and times, with automatic formatting, min and max limits, and validation.
Import: import { DateInput } from "@plexui/vue/components/DateInput"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/date-input
Props:
  - value / v-model:value (DateTime | null) — Selected date (Luxon DateTime). The field shows its date and time in its own zone, and a typed date is made in that zone (Luxon's default zone without a value)
  - @update:value ((date: DateTime | null) => void) — Emitted when a complete valid date is entered or the field is cleared
  - @change ((date: DateTime | null) => void) — Same payload as update:value, named after React's onChange
  - mode (MaskitoDateMode) default: "dd/mm/yyyy" — Date format mode: dd/mm/yyyy, mm/dd/yyyy, yyyy/mm/dd, etc.
  - separator (string) default: "." — Character between date parts
  - time-mode (MaskitoTimeMode) — Enable time input: HH:MM, HH:MM:SS, etc.
  - date-time-separator (string) default: ", " — Separator between date and time parts
  - time-step (number) default: 0 — Arrow-key increment step for time segments
  - min (DateTime) — Earliest allowed date
  - max (DateTime) — Latest allowed date
  - clearable (boolean) default: false — Show clear button when input has a value
  - size (ControlSize) default: "md" — Input size (3xs to 3xl)
  - variant ("outline" | "soft") default: "outline" — Visual style
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded corners
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable input
  - invalid (boolean) default: false — Mark as invalid
  - placeholder (string) — Generated from the format if not set

### Overview
Bind a Luxon DateTime (or null) with v-model:value. It updates once a complete, valid date is typed, and becomes null when the field is emptied or cleared; @change carries the same value. Leaving the field with a partly typed date shows the value again. mode orders the day, month and year ("mm/dd/yyyy" in the US, "yyyy/mm/dd" for ISO 8601 with separator="-"), and separator is the character between them. time-mode adds a time to the date in one field, "HH:MM", "HH:MM:SS" for seconds or "HH:MM AA" for a 12-hour time, whose AM or PM completes the date; with a time, date-time-separator stands between the date and the time, and time-step lets the arrow keys step the time segment under the caret. min and max restrict the dates: a date typed past one becomes that limit (here 30 days around today, DateTime.local()). The placeholder follows the format unless you set placeholder.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { DateTime } from 'luxon';
import { DateInput } from '@plexui/vue/components/DateInput';

const today = DateTime.local();
const date = ref<DateTime | null>(null);
const variant = ref("outline"); // or "soft"
const size = ref("md"); // "3xs" to "3xl"
const pill = ref(false);
const mode = ref("dd/mm/yyyy"); // or "mm/dd/yyyy", "yyyy/mm/dd"
const separator = ref("."); // or "/", "-"
const timeMode = ref(""); // or "HH:MM", "HH:MM:SS", "HH:MM AA"
const dateTimeSeparator = ref(", "); // or " "; with a time
const timeStep = ref(0); // the arrow keys' step on a time segment, 0 for none; with a time
const limits = ref(false); // min and max, 30 days around today
const clearable = ref(true);
const invalid = ref(false);
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<!-- Desktop: wide enough for the date, and for its time -->
<div :class="!timeMode ? 'w-[200px]' : timeMode === 'HH:MM' ? 'w-[260px]' : 'w-[300px]'">
  <DateInput
    v-model:value="date"
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :pill="pill"
    :mode="mode"
    :separator="separator"
    :time-mode="timeMode || undefined"
    :date-time-separator="dateTimeSeparator"
    :time-step="timeStep"
    :min="limits ? today.minus({ days: 30 }).startOf('day') : undefined"
    :max="limits ? today.plus({ days: 30 }).endOf('day') : undefined"
    :clearable="clearable"
    :invalid="invalid"
    :disabled="disabled"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone: the date is the value of a row, which is its frame -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <DateInput
      v-model:value="date"
      :mode="mode"
      :separator="separator"
      :time-mode="timeMode || undefined"
      :date-time-separator="dateTimeSeparator"
      :time-step="timeStep"
      :min="limits ? today.minus({ days: 30 }).startOf('day') : undefined"
      :max="limits ? today.plus({ days: 30 }).endOf('day') : undefined"
      :clearable="clearable"
      :disabled="disabled"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
</template>
```

### With Field
Wrap with Field to add a label, description, and automatic accessibility attributes.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-[200px]">
  <Field label="Date of birth" description="Enter your date of birth">
    <DateInput v-model:value="date" clearable />
  </Field>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date of birth" description="Enter your date of birth">
    <DateInput v-model:value="date" clearable />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Validation
Pass error-message to Field to display a validation error.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-[200px]">
  <Field label="Date of birth" :error-message="!date ? 'Date is required' : undefined">
    <DateInput v-model:value="date" clearable />
  </Field>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date of birth" :error-message="!date ? 'Date is required' : undefined">
    <DateInput v-model:value="date" clearable />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Date Picker
Date picker with an interactive calendar, time picker, year and month dropdowns, min/max limits, and date-of-birth presets.
Import: import { DatePicker } from "@plexui/vue/components/DatePicker"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/date-picker
Props:
  - value / v-model:value (DateTime | null) default: null — Selected date (luxon)
  - @change ((date: DateTime | null) => void) — Emitted when the date changes, with the same value as update:value
  - id (string) — Id of the trigger, for forms and labels
  - min (DateTime) — Earliest selectable date (inclusive)
  - max (DateTime) — Latest selectable date (inclusive). Must not be before min
  - caption-layout ("buttons" | "dropdown") default: "buttons" — Calendar header mode. Use dropdown for date of birth
  - show-time (boolean) default: false — Show time input below calendar
  - year-range ([number, number]) default: last 120 years — Min/max years for dropdown caption
  - size (ControlSize) default: "md" — Trigger size (3xs to 3xl)
  - variant ("outline" | "soft" | "ghost") default: "outline" — Trigger style
  - pill (boolean) default: true — Rounded trigger
  - block (boolean) default: false — Full-width trigger
  - clearable (boolean) default: false — Show clear button
  - trigger-show-icon (boolean) default: true — Show calendar icon
  - trigger-date-format (string) default: "MM/dd/yy" — Date format in trigger. Auto-includes time when show-time is enabled and trigger-time-format is not set
  - trigger-time-format (string) — With show-time, the time format after the date in the trigger. The date and the time become two parts; in a phone row, two capsules
  - trigger-class-name (string) — Class applied to the trigger
  - dropdown-icon-type ("dropdown" | "chevronDown" | "none") default: "dropdown" — Icon at the end of the trigger
  - placeholder (string) default: "Select date..." — Trigger placeholder
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable picker
  - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "bottom" — Preferred side of the calendar popover. On phones, left and right prefer bottom so all seven day targets fit. Top and bottom keep their preference; collisions can still flip placement.
  - side-offset (number) default: 8 — Distance from the trigger in pixels
  - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Alignment against the trigger
  - align-offset (number) default: 0 (center), -5 (start, end) — Offset from start/end alignment in pixels

### Overview
Bind the date (a luxon DateTime, or null) with v-model:value. The @change event carries the same value, like React's onChange. show-time adds hour and minute fields under the calendar, which then stays open after a date is picked so the time can be set; the arrow keys step the field they are in. trigger-time-format writes the time after the date as a part of its own: on Phone, in a row, the date and the time are two capsules, as iOS shows an event's start. Without it, a show-time trigger writes "MM/dd/yy HH:mm" unless you set trigger-date-format. caption-layout="dropdown" adds month and year dropdowns beside the arrows, for a date of birth or a date years away; year-range sets their years (the last 120 by default); with min or max the years are those they allow, so here it is offered without limits. min and max limit the dates that can be picked: here 30 days either side of today, or up to today for a date of birth. Equal bounds leave one day.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { DateTime } from 'luxon';
import { DatePicker } from '@plexui/vue/components/DatePicker';

const today = DateTime.local();
const minDate = today.minus({ days: 30 }).startOf('day');
const maxDate = today.plus({ days: 30 }).endOf('day');
const endOfToday = today.endOf('day');
const selectedDate = ref<DateTime | null>(null);
const variant = ref("outline"); // or "soft", "ghost"
const size = ref("lg"); // "3xs" to "3xl"
const pill = ref(true);
const block = ref(false);
const clearable = ref(true);
const triggerShowIcon = ref(true);
const dropdownIconType = ref("dropdown"); // or "chevronDown", "none"
const triggerDateFormat = ref("MM/dd/yy"); // or "MMM d, yyyy", "MMMM d, yyyy"
const showTime = ref(false);
const triggerTimeFormat = ref("HH:mm"); // or "h:mm a"; with show-time
const captionLayout = ref("buttons"); // or "dropdown"
const yearRange = ref("default"); // or "2020-2030"; with the dropdown caption and no limits
const limits = ref("none"); // or "around" (30 days either side of today), "past" (up to today)
const side = ref("bottom"); // or "top", "right", "left"
const sideOffset = ref(8);
const align = ref("center"); // or "start", "end"
const alignOffset = ref(-5); // with a start or end alignment
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="flex w-[320px] justify-center">
  <DatePicker
    id="date-picker-base"
    placeholder="Select date"
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :pill="pill"
    :block="block"
    :clearable="clearable"
    :trigger-show-icon="triggerShowIcon"
    :dropdown-icon-type="dropdownIconType"
    :trigger-date-format="triggerDateFormat"
    :show-time="showTime"
    :trigger-time-format="showTime ? triggerTimeFormat : undefined"
    :caption-layout="captionLayout"
    :year-range="limits === 'none' && yearRange === '2020-2030' ? [2020, 2030] : undefined"
    :min="limits === 'around' ? minDate : undefined"
    :max="limits === 'around' ? maxDate : limits === 'past' ? endOfToday : undefined"
    :side="side"
    :side-offset="sideOffset"
    :align="align"
    :align-offset="align === 'center' ? undefined : alignOffset"
    :disabled="disabled"
    v-model:value="selectedDate"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone: the date is the value of a row, in the compact date that opens the calendar -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <DatePicker
      id="date-picker-base"
      placeholder="Select date"
      trigger-date-format="MMM d, yyyy"
      :trigger-show-icon="false"
      :show-time="showTime"
      :trigger-time-format="showTime ? triggerTimeFormat : undefined"
      :caption-layout="captionLayout"
      :year-range="limits === 'none' && yearRange === '2020-2030' ? [2020, 2030] : undefined"
      :min="limits === 'around' ? minDate : undefined"
      :max="limits === 'around' ? maxDate : limits === 'past' ? endOfToday : undefined"
      :clearable="clearable"
      :disabled="disabled"
      v-model:value="selectedDate"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
</template>
```

### Two fields
Compose DatePicker and Input side by side for maximum flexibility in form layouts. Each field operates independently while sharing the same DateTime value. Here formatTime masks the typed time as HH:MM, applyTime moves the date to a complete typed time, and withTime gives a newly picked date the typed time.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="display: flex; gap: 8px; align-items: center">
  <DatePicker
    id="date-picker-two-field-date"
    trigger-date-format="MMM d, yyyy"
    size="lg"
    pill
    caption-layout="dropdown"
    :trigger-show-icon="false"
    dropdown-icon-type="none"
    placeholder="Select date"
    :value="selectedDate"
    @update:value="selectedDate = withTime($event, time)"
  />
  <Input
    id="date-picker-two-field-time"
    size="lg"
    pill
    :value="time"
    @update:value="time = formatTime($event); selectedDate = applyTime(selectedDate, time)"
    placeholder="HH:MM"
    maxlength="5"
    style="width: 72px"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone: two rows, the compact date and the time typed at the row's end -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <DatePicker
      id="date-picker-two-field-date"
      trigger-date-format="MMM d, yyyy"
      caption-layout="dropdown"
      :trigger-show-icon="false"
      placeholder="Select date"
      :value="selectedDate"
      @update:value="selectedDate = withTime($event, time)"
    />
  </Field>
  <Field row label="Time">
    <Input
      id="date-picker-two-field-time"
      :value="time"
      @update:value="time = formatTime($event); selectedDate = applyTime(selectedDate, time)"
      placeholder="HH:MM"
      maxlength="5"
      inputmode="numeric"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Date input
For typing a date directly instead of picking it, use DateInput, a masked input that auto-formats as you type. With time-mode="HH:MM" it takes the time in the same field.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-[200px]">
  <DateInput v-model:value="selectedDate" mode="mm/dd/yyyy" separator="/" aria-label="Date" />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <DateInput v-model:value="selectedDate" mode="mm/dd/yyyy" separator="/" />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Date Range Picker
Date range picker with an interactive calendar, min/max limits, preset shortcuts, and a month stepper for selecting start and end dates.
Import: import { DateRangePicker } from "@plexui/vue/components/DateRangePicker"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/date-range-picker
Props:
  - value / v-model:value (DateRange | null) — Selected range ([start, end], inclusive)
  - @change ((next: DateRange | null, shortcut?: DateRangeShortcut) => void) — Emitted when the range changes
  - min (DateTime) — Earliest selectable date (inclusive)
  - max (DateTime) — Latest selectable date (inclusive). Must not be before min
  - max-range-days (number) — Max length of range in days (inclusive)
  - week-starts-on (0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6) default: 0 — The day each week starts on, from 0 (Sunday) to 6 (Saturday)
  - shortcuts (DateRangeShortcut[]) — Quick selection shortcuts ({ label, getDateRange }), shown next to the calendar from the md breakpoint up
  - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "bottom" — Preferred side of the calendar popover. On phones, left and right prefer bottom so all seven day targets fit. Top and bottom keep their preference; collisions can still flip placement.
  - side-offset (number) default: 8 — Distance from trigger in pixels
  - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Preferred popover alignment
  - align-offset (number) default: 0 (center), -5 (start, end) — Offset from start/end alignment in pixels
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable picker interactions
  - placeholder (string) default: "Select date range..." — Placeholder in trigger when value is empty
  - variant ("soft" | "outline" | "ghost") default: "outline" — Trigger style variant
  - pill (boolean) default: true — Rounded trigger shape
  - size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl" | "3xl") default: "md" — Trigger size scale
  - dropdown-icon-type ("chevronDown" | "dropdown" | "none") default: "dropdown" — Icon shown at trigger end
  - trigger-class-name (string) — Custom class for trigger
  - trigger-show-icon (boolean) default: true — Show calendar icon at trigger start
  - trigger-stepper-unit ("month") — Enable month stepper mode in trigger
  - trigger-date-format (string) default: "MM/dd/yy" — Date formatting in trigger text
  - clearable (boolean) default: false — Show clear button in trigger
  - block (boolean) default: false — Stretch trigger to full available width

### Overview
Bind the range ([start, end] luxon DateTimes, or null) with v-model:value. The @change event carries the same range.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: fit-content">
  <DateRangePicker
    size="lg"
    pill
    clearable
    :disabled="disabled"
    v-model:value="value"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Dates">
    <DateRangePicker
      :trigger-show-icon="false"
      trigger-date-format="MMM d"
      placeholder="None"
      :disabled="disabled"
      v-model:value="value"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Limits
Use min and max to limit the selectable range. Use max-range-days to limit the range length. Here minDate is 30 days ago (start of day) and maxDate is the end of today.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: fit-content">
  <DateRangePicker
    size="lg"
    pill
    trigger-show-icon
    v-model:value="value"
    :min="minDate"
    :max="maxDate"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Dates">
    <DateRangePicker
      :trigger-show-icon="false"
      trigger-date-format="MMM d"
      placeholder="None"
      v-model:value="value"
      :min="minDate"
      :max="maxDate"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Shortcuts
Use the shortcuts array to provide quick date range options. When a shortcut is selected, its label is shown in the trigger and @change carries that shortcut as its second argument, like React's onChange(range, shortcut). Each shortcut has a label and a getDateRange() function; dayShortcuts here holds Today, Yesterday, Last 7 days and Last 30 days. A shortcut whose range falls outside min and max, or is longer than max-range-days, is disabled. The calendar doesn't show shortcuts below the md breakpoint. On a phone, offer them in a row of their own, as iOS settings offer presets: here a Period row with a Select of the shortcut labels (periodOptions) above the Dates row. periodOf(value) is the label of the shortcut the dates match, or an empty string, so the Period row reads Custom for any other range and None without dates.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: fit-content">
  <DateRangePicker
    size="lg"
    pill
    clearable
    v-model:value="value"
    :shortcuts="dayShortcuts"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Period">
    <Select
      :value="periodOf(value)"
      :options="periodOptions"
      :placeholder="value ? 'Custom' : 'None'"
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      @change="({ value: label }) => (value = dayShortcuts.find((item) => item.label === label)?.getDateRange() ?? value)"
    />
  </Field>
  <Field row label="Dates">
    <DateRangePicker
      :trigger-show-icon="false"
      trigger-date-format="MMM d"
      placeholder="None"
      v-model:value="value"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Month stepper
Enable a stepper in the trigger with trigger-stepper-unit="month". When the selected range matches the unit, the trigger shows the stepper.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: fit-content">
  <DateRangePicker
    :size="size"
    clearable
    :pill="pill"
    trigger-stepper-unit="month"
    v-model:value="value"
    :max="maxDate"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Month">
    <DateRangePicker
      trigger-stepper-unit="month"
      v-model:value="value"
      :max="maxDate"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Dialog
Modal dialog for confirmations, forms, and destructive alerts, with a focus trap, escape handling, and an overlay scrim.
Import: import { Dialog, DialogBody, DialogClose, DialogContent, DialogDescription, DialogFooter, DialogHeader, DialogHeaderAction, DialogTitle, DialogTrigger } from "@plexui/vue/components/Dialog"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/dialog
Props:
  - open / v-model:open (boolean) — Controlled open state; uncontrolled when omitted
  - @update:open ((open: boolean) => void) — Fires when the dialog opens or closes
  DialogContent:
    - surface ("plain" | "grouped") default: "plain" — Mobile panel and field-group palette. Dark dialogs use elevated surface levels; desktop colors stay unchanged
    - width (number | string) default: 480 — Panel width. Mobile alert uses its compact width; a bottom sheet fills the viewport width
    - show-close (boolean) default: true — Show the built-in close button. A mobile sheet places it at the leading edge, unless a leading DialogHeaderAction takes that place; a mobile alert hides it
    - class (string) — Additional CSS class on the panel
    - placement ("center" | "top" | "bottom" | "start" | "end" | "fullscreen") default: "center" — Where the panel sits: centered, near the top, a bottom sheet, a side panel, or the whole screen
    - mobile-variant ("alert" | "sheet") default: "sheet" — The iOS presentation at 767px and below. A phone has two modal views: a sheet, which iOS gives any view it presents, and an alert, a short question with its answers, compact and centered
    - mobile-placement ("center" | "bottom" | "fullscreen") default: "bottom" — Where a phone sheet stands: at the bottom, or over the whole screen (the default for a fullscreen dialog). center is the alert's place: without mobile-variant it makes the dialog an alert
    - @open-auto-focus ((event: Event) => void) — Called when focus moves into the dialog on open; call event.preventDefault() to place it yourself, for example on the panel instead of the first field
    - @close-auto-focus ((event: Event) => void) — Called when focus returns on close; call event.preventDefault() to keep it elsewhere
    - @escape-key-down ((event: KeyboardEvent) => void) — Called on Escape before the dialog closes; call event.preventDefault() to keep it open
    - @interact-outside ((event: DialogInteractOutsideEvent) => void) — Called on a press or a focus outside the dialog, and on a phone sheet pulled down to close, before it closes; call event.preventDefault() to keep it open (the sheet returns). event.detail.originalEvent is the browser's event, a TouchEvent for the sheet
    - z-index (number) default: 50 — Stack order of the overlay and the dialog, for a page whose own layers sit above 50
    - root-props (DialogLayerProps) — class, style and data attributes for the fixed layer that holds the panel. Data attributes on DialogContent land there too
    - overlay-props (DialogLayerProps) — class, style and data attributes for the overlay
    - layout ("auto" | "phone") default: "auto" — phone lays the dialog out as on a phone at any window width, with the dialogs it opens. Inside a PhoneLayout it always is
  DialogHeaderAction:
    - side ("leading" | "trailing") default: "trailing" — leading puts the action before the title, such as the Back of a step: in a phone Sheet it takes the place of Close, as the back button of a screen pushed inside an iOS sheet
  DialogFooter:
    - background ("soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "blur") default: "soft" — How the phone Sheet's bar meets the body scrolled under it, as on a Toolbar. Also available on the header; desktop and Alert surfaces are unchanged. solid is the earlier name of hard
    - mobile-layout ("inline" | "stacked") — Action layout at 767px and below. Defaults to inline for an alert of two actions, stacked otherwise, as for three or more alert actions. Inline actions wrap when they do not fit. A stacked alert keeps the written order; a stacked sheet puts its last action on top
  DialogSteps:
    - step (string | number) — The step on screen. A new value brings its content in
    - direction ("forward" | "back") default: "forward" — forward brings the new step in from the trailing edge over the one before; back returns to a step before it
    - class (string) — Additional CSS class on the steps

### Overview
On a phone a dialog is one of the two modal views of iOS: an Alert (mobile-variant="alert"), a short question with its answers, or a Sheet, which it is by default (see Placement). The alert takes what the iOS 27 kit and UIAlertController offer. One, two or three actions: two stand side by side with Cancel first, and three or more stack in their order with Cancel last, as iOS sets them. DialogFooter mobile-layout="stacked" stacks two, for long labels. The main action is preferred (solid: the accent fill on a phone), plain (soft), or destructive (color="danger": a red label on the alert's fill on a phone). A title without a message stands centered. A field asks for one short value: a 48px capsule on a phone, its text selected when the alert opens, and Rename unavailable while it is empty. Label actions in title case, as iOS does.
```vue
<script setup>
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Dialog, DialogTrigger, DialogContent, DialogHeader, DialogTitle, DialogDescription, DialogBody, DialogFooter, DialogClose } from '@plexui/vue/components/Dialog';
import { Input } from '@plexui/vue/components/Input';

const open = ref(false);
const actions = ref('2'); // or '1', '3'
const destructive = ref(false); // with two actions
const preferred = ref(true); // the main action is filled
const message = ref(true);
const field = ref(false); // with two actions, not destructive
const stacked = ref(false); // two actions, one under the other on a phone
const name = ref('Untitled');

// What the alert says for each kind: a field renames, one action tells, a destructive one deletes
const texts = {
  rename: { title: 'Rename document', message: 'Enter a new name for this document.', action: 'Rename' },
  notice: { title: 'Unable to save', message: 'Check your connection and try again.', action: 'OK' },
  delete: { title: 'Delete document?', message: 'This document will be deleted. You can’t undo this.', action: 'Delete' },
  save: { title: 'Save changes?', message: 'Do you want to save the changes you made to this document?', action: 'Save' },
};
</script>

<template>
<Dialog v-model:open="open" @update:open="(next) => { if (next) name = 'Untitled' }">
  <DialogTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline">Open alert</Button>
  </DialogTrigger>
  <DialogContent mobile-variant="alert" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); field ? $refs.input.$el.querySelector('input').select() : $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })">
    <DialogHeader>
      <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>{{ texts[field ? 'rename' : actions === '1' ? 'notice' : destructive ? 'delete' : 'save'].title }}</DialogTitle></div>
      <DialogDescription v-if="message">{{ texts[field ? 'rename' : actions === '1' ? 'notice' : destructive ? 'delete' : 'save'].message }}</DialogDescription>
    </DialogHeader>
    <DialogBody v-if="field">
      <Input ref="input" v-model:value="name" aria-label="Document name" variant="soft" size="3xl" pill />
    </DialogBody>
    <DialogFooter :mobile-layout="stacked ? 'stacked' : undefined">
      <DialogClose v-if="actions === '2'">
        <Button color="primary" variant="soft">Cancel</Button>
      </DialogClose>
      <Button :color="destructive ? 'danger' : 'primary'" :variant="destructive || preferred ? 'solid' : 'soft'" :disabled="field && !name.trim()" @click="open = false">
        {{ texts[field ? 'rename' : actions === '1' ? 'notice' : destructive ? 'delete' : 'save'].action }}
      </Button>
      <template v-if="actions === '3'">
        <Button color="danger" variant="soft" @click="open = false">Don’t Save</Button>
        <DialogClose>
          <Button color="primary" variant="soft">Cancel</Button>
        </DialogClose>
      </template>
    </DialogFooter>
  </DialogContent>
</Dialog>
</template>
```

### Metadata details
Put labels and values in the rows of a FieldGroup, each value an output: on a phone the Sheet is grouped and the rows are the cells of an iOS list, as Settings' About. A value too long for its row goes under its label there, as iOS lays out a value cell. Use CopyTooltip with a ghost Button. The full value is copied even when the visible ID is truncated. Focus starts at the heading; Tab reaches the controls.
```vue
<script setup>
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Dialog, DialogTrigger, DialogContent, DialogHeader, DialogTitle, DialogDescription, DialogBody } from '@plexui/vue/components/Dialog';
import { Field } from '@plexui/vue/components/Field';
import { FieldGroup } from '@plexui/vue/components/FieldGroup';
import { CopyTooltip } from '@plexui/vue/components/Tooltip';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');

const initialOpen = false;
const open = ref(initialOpen);
const rows = [
  { "label": "Name", "value": "Plex application" },
  { "label": "Client ID", "value": "client_76fb07f7-78e2-4656-abdf-e77b5d7b1ed1", "copy": "Copy client ID" },
  { "label": "Status", "value": "Active" },
  { "label": "Created", "value": "Nov 3, 2026" },
  { "label": "Last used", "value": "Nov 4, 2026" }
];
</script>

<template>
<div class="flex flex-col items-center gap-4">
  <Dialog v-model:open="open">
    <DialogTrigger v-if="!initialOpen || !open"><Button color="secondary" variant="outline">View details</Button></DialogTrigger>
    <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" surface="grouped" :width="640" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })">
      <DialogHeader>
        <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>Application details</DialogTitle></div>
        <DialogDescription class="sr-only">Application metadata.</DialogDescription>
      </DialogHeader>
      <DialogBody>
        <FieldGroup>
          <Field v-for="row in rows" :key="row.label" row :label="row.label" :orientation="row.copy && !wide ? 'vertical' : undefined">
            <CopyTooltip v-if="row.copy" :copy-value="row.value" :side-offset="wide ? 5 : 24" :align="wide ? 'center' : 'start'">
              <Button color="secondary" variant="ghost" size="sm" optically-align="start" class="-my-1 min-w-0 max-w-full" :aria-label="row.copy">
                <span class="flex min-w-0 md:font-mono md:text-xs"><span class="truncate">{{ row.value.slice(0, -6) }}</span><span class="shrink-0">{{ row.value.slice(-6) }}</span></span>
              </Button>
            </CopyTooltip>
            <output v-else>{{ row.value }}</output>
          </Field>
        </FieldGroup>
      </DialogBody>
    </DialogContent>
  </Dialog>
</div>
</template>
```

### Scrolling body
Put long content in DialogBody: only the body scrolls, while the header and footer stay in view. Without it the whole panel scrolls. On a phone, background on DialogHeader and DialogFooter sets how the Sheet's bars meet the body scrolled under them, as a Toolbar's do: by default glass on the header and soft on the footer, or hard or blur.
```vue
<Dialog v-model:open="open">
  <DialogTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline">Review terms</Button>
  </DialogTrigger>
  <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })">
    <DialogHeader>
      <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>Terms of service</DialogTitle></div>
    </DialogHeader>
    <DialogBody>
      <div class="flex flex-col gap-4 text-sm text-secondary max-md:text-[length:var(--dialog-mobile-type-size)] max-md:leading-[var(--dialog-mobile-type-leading)]">
        <p v-for="(term, index) in [...terms, ...terms]" :key="index">{{ term }}</p>
      </div>
    </DialogBody>
    <DialogFooter>
      <DialogClose>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft">Decline</Button>
      </DialogClose>
      <Button color="primary" @click="open = false">Accept</Button>
    </DialogFooter>
  </DialogContent>
</Dialog>
```

### Placement
Use placement to move the panel: top for search and command lists, bottom for a sheet along the bottom edge, start and end for a full-height side panel, fullscreen for the whole screen. On a phone (767px and below) a dialog that is not an alert is a Sheet at the bottom: mobile-placement="fullscreen" fills the screen with it, as a fullscreen dialog does by itself.
```vue
<Dialog v-model:open="open">
  <DialogTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline">Open filters</Button>
  </DialogTrigger>
  <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" surface="grouped" :mobile-placement="placement === 'fullscreen' ? 'fullscreen' : 'bottom'" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })" :placement="placement" :width="placement === 'start' || placement === 'end' ? 360 : 480">
    <DialogHeader>
      <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>Filters</DialogTitle></div>
      <DialogDescription>Narrow the list down.</DialogDescription>
    </DialogHeader>
    <DialogBody>
      <FieldGroup columns>
        <Field row label="Status"><Input placeholder="Any status" /></Field>
        <Field row label="Owner"><Input placeholder="Anyone" /></Field>
        <Field row label="Created after"><Input placeholder="YYYY-MM-DD" /></Field>
      </FieldGroup>
    </DialogBody>
    <DialogFooter>
      <DialogClose>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft">Clear filters</Button>
      </DialogClose>
      <Button color="primary" @click="open = false">Done</Button>
    </DialogFooter>
  </DialogContent>
</Dialog>
```
