With InputGroupTextarea, use block-start and block-end addons for a header and a footer. Utility classes such as ml-auto push a button to the far side.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-full min-w-0 max-w-[400px]">
  <InputGroup>
    <InputGroupTextarea placeholder="console.log('Hello, world!');" :rows="4" class="font-mono" />
    <InputGroupAddon align="block-start">
      <InputGroupText>
        <FileCode />script.js
      </InputGroupText>
      <InputGroupButton uniform aria-label="Copy" class="ml-auto">
        <Copy />
      </InputGroupButton>
    </InputGroupAddon>
    <InputGroupAddon align="block-end">
      <InputGroupText>Line 1, Column 1</InputGroupText>
      <InputGroupButton variant="solid" color="primary" class="ml-auto">
        Run<Play />
      </InputGroupButton>
    </InputGroupAddon>
  </InputGroup>
</div>

<!-- Phone: the header is the row's label, the status line the group's footer. -->
<FieldGroup>
  <template #footer>Line 1, Column 1</template>
  <Field row orientation="vertical" label="script.js">
    <Textarea variant="ghost" placeholder="console.log('Hello, world!');" :rows="4" class="font-mono" autocapitalize="none" spellcheck="false" />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
<Button color="primary" size="3xl" pill block>Run</Button>
```

### Sizes
Every Input size from 3xs to 3xl. Icons, text and InputGroupButton scale with the group: buttons in inline addons fit the control height and keep a 4px inset. On a phone with touch.css, 3xs to xl are all 44px, and in a row the row sets the height: the iOS 26 field is one 52px row, so this example is desktop only.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="flex flex-col gap-2.5 w-[360px]">
  <div v-for="size in sizes" :key="size" class="flex items-center gap-4">
    <span class="w-8 shrink-0 text-right text-sm text-tertiary">{{ size }}</span>
    <InputGroup :size="size" :pill="pill">
      <InputGroupInput placeholder="Search..." />
      <InputGroupAddon>
        <Search />
      </InputGroupAddon>
      <InputGroupAddon align="inline-end">
        <InputGroupButton>Search</InputGroupButton>
      </InputGroupAddon>
    </InputGroup>
  </div>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup columns>
  <Field row label="Domain">
    <template #default="control">
      <InputGroup>
        <InputGroupInput v-bind="control" default-value="plexui.com" inputmode="url" autocapitalize="none" spellcheck="false" />
        <InputGroupAddon>
          <InputGroupText>https://</InputGroupText>
        </InputGroupAddon>
      </InputGroup>
    </template>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Kbd
A key or a keyboard shortcut, such as ⌘K, set inline in text, in a tooltip or at the end of a menu item.
Import: import { Kbd, KbdGroup } from "@plexui/vue/components/Kbd"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/kbd
Props:
  - size ("xs" | "sm" | "md") default: "sm" — Height and text size: 16px, 20px or 24px
  - variant ("soft" | "outline") default: "soft" — soft fills the key, outline draws its edge; both mix the text color
  - class (string) — Applied to the kbd element
  - default slot (VNode[]) — The key

### Overview
Kbd renders a {''} element: the semantic tag for keyboard input. It sits on the text baseline, so it can stand in a sentence.
```vue
<p class="text-sm text-secondary">
  Press <Kbd>/</Kbd> to search, <Kbd>Esc</Kbd> to close.
</p>
```

### Shortcut
Use KbdGroup for keys pressed together. Each key keeps its own frame.
```vue
<KbdGroup>
  <Kbd>⌘</Kbd>
  <Kbd>K</Kbd>
</KbdGroup>
<KbdGroup>
  <Kbd>Ctrl</Kbd>
  <Kbd>Shift</Kbd>
  <Kbd>P</Kbd>
</KbdGroup>
```

### Sizes and variants
Three sizes: xs (16px) for tooltips and menu items, sm (20px, default) for text, md (24px) next to larger controls. soft (default) fills the key, outline draws its edge. Both mix the surrounding text color, so a key reads on a page, in a dark tooltip and in a highlighted menu item alike.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col items-center gap-4">
  <div v-for="variant in ['soft', 'outline']" :key="variant" class="flex items-center gap-4">
    <Kbd size="xs" :variant="variant">⌘K</Kbd>
    <Kbd size="sm" :variant="variant">⌘K</Kbd>
    <Kbd size="md" :variant="variant">⌘K</Kbd>
  </div>
</div>
```

### In a tooltip and a menu
Show a control's shortcut in its tooltip, or at the end of a menu item. Kbd only shows the keys: announce the shortcut to assistive technology with aria-keyshortcuts on the control that has it.
```vue
<Tooltip>
  <template #content>
    <span class="flex items-center gap-2">
      Search
      <KbdGroup>
        <Kbd size="xs">⌘</Kbd>
        <Kbd size="xs">K</Kbd>
      </KbdGroup>
    </span>
  </template>
  <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" uniform aria-label="Search" aria-keyshortcuts="Meta+K">
    <Search />
  </Button>
</Tooltip>
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="soft">Edit</Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="220">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1">Undo</span>
      <Kbd size="xs" variant="outline">⌘Z</Kbd>
    </MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1">Redo</span>
      <Kbd size="xs" variant="outline">⇧⌘Z</Kbd>
    </MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

## Label
Accessible form label that points at any control by its id, so clicking it focuses or toggles the control, with consistent typography across forms.
Import: import { Label } from "@plexui/vue/components/Label"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/label
Props:
  - for (string) — id of the associated control. Clicks on the label focus or activate the control.
  - class (string) — Applied to the root <label> element
  - default slot (VNode[]) — Label content

### Overview
Clicking the label toggles the checkbox it points at. Here accepted starts as true. On Phone the label takes the body type of a list row, 17/22 regular, the same as the labels of Checkbox, Switch and a Field row.
```vue
<div style="display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px">
  <Checkbox id="label-demo-terms" v-model:checked="accepted" />
  <Label for="label-demo-terms">Accept terms and conditions</Label>
</div>
```

## Markdown
Markdown renderer for rich formatted content, with headings, lists, tables, code blocks, and KaTeX math expressions out of the box.
Import: import { Markdown } from "@plexui/vue/components/Markdown"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/markdown
Props:
  - default slot (string) — Markdown source (text only; interpolate a string to keep its newlines)
  - source (string) — Markdown source, used when the default slot is empty
  - include-math (boolean) default: false — Enable LaTeX math rendering
  - break-new-lines (boolean) default: false — Turn single newlines into line breaks
  - copyable-code-blocks (boolean) default: true — Show a copy button on code blocks
  - collapsible-code-blocks (boolean) default: true — Render code blocks in the collapsible view with a View Code button
  - components (Record<string, MarkdownComponent>) — Tag name to component overrides, applied over the built-in ones
  - directives (MarkdownDirective[]) — Custom directives, each rendered by its own component
  - remark-plugins (PluggableList) — Extra remark plugins, after the built-in ones
  - rehype-plugins (PluggableList) — Extra rehype plugins
  - allowed-elements (string[]) — Only render these elements
  - disallowed-elements (string[]) — Never render these elements
  - skip-html (boolean) default: false — Ignore raw HTML in the source
  - url-transform ((url: string) => string) — Rewrites URLs; the default drops unsafe protocols (javascript:) and keeps tel: and sms:
  - class (string) — Applied to the wrapping element

### Overview
Use Markdown to render rich markdown text. The markdown is the text of the default slot: interpolate a string ({"{{ markdown }}"}) so its newlines are kept, or pass it with the source prop.
```vue
<Markdown>{{ markdownContent }}</Markdown>
```

### Math
Use include-math to enable LaTeX rendering. The KaTeX styles and fonts come with @plexui/styles, so formulas need no other setup.
```vue
<Markdown include-math>{{ markdownWithLatex }}</Markdown>
```

## Markdown Editor
Compact rich text editor powered by Tiptap, embedded in a form field, with a markdown formatting toolbar, variants, and an invalid state.
Import: import { MarkdownEditor } from "@plexui/vue/components/MarkdownEditor"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/markdown-editor
Props:
  - variant ("outline" | "soft") default: "outline" — Visual style of the editor
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the editor
  - invalid (boolean) default: false — Mark as invalid
  - placeholder (string) — Placeholder text when empty
  - value / v-model:value (string) — Controlled markdown value; `update:value` fires on every change
  - default-value (string) — Initial markdown for uncontrolled mode
  - @change ((markdown: string) => void) — Called on content change
  - formats (Array<"bold" | "italic" | "orderedList" | "bulletList" | "link">) default: all five — Formats the editor offers, read when it is created. One left out has no button, shortcut or input rule, and markdown with it keeps only its text
  - breaks (boolean) default: false — A single newline is a line break, read and written as one newline (read when the editor is created). Without it a line break is written as a backslash and a newline
  - toolbarEnd slot (VNode[]) — Content rendered at the end of the toolbar
  - rows (number) default: 1 — Minimum visible rows
  - id (string) — id of the editable element (Field sets it)
  - aria-label (string) — Accessible name of the editable element
  - aria-labelledby (string) — Id of the element that names the editor, such as a visible label
  - aria-describedby (string) — aria-describedby of the editable element (Field sets it)
  - aria-invalid (boolean) — Sets aria-invalid on the editable element and the invalid style (Field sets it with error-message)
  - class (string) — Applied to the container

### Placeholder
Use placeholder for empty content. Toggle invalid and disabled without replacing the entered markdown. The invalid state is controlled by this example, not by a validator.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { MarkdownEditor } from '@plexui/vue/components/MarkdownEditor';

const value = ref('');
const invalid = ref(false);
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
  <MarkdownEditor aria-label="Markdown content" placeholder="Enter text..."
    v-model:value="value" :invalid="invalid" :disabled="disabled" />
</template>
```

### All formatting
Example with all supported formatting: bold, italic, links, ordered lists, and unordered lists. Click inside formatted text to see toolbar buttons toggle.
```vue
<div class="w-full min-w-0 max-w-[500px]">
  <MarkdownEditor :default-value="allFormattingMd" />
</div>
```

### Formats and line breaks
Use formats to choose what the editor offers: bold, italic, orderedList, bulletList and link, all five by default. A format left out has no toolbar button, keyboard shortcut or input rule. In pasted text its markers stay text, as if they were typed, and so do those of headings, quotes, code and rules, which the editor never offers. Pasted HTML and incoming markdown keep its text without it, and the markdown the editor writes leaves it out: here the italic and the list of the initial value become plain text. With breaks, a single newline is a line break, read and written as one newline. Both are read when the editor is created.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { MarkdownEditor } from '@plexui/vue/components/MarkdownEditor';

const value = ref(initialMd);
</script>

<template>
  <MarkdownEditor aria-label="Message" :formats="['bold', 'link']" breaks v-model:value="value" />
</template>
```

### With Field
Wrap with Field to add a description and a linked error message. The editor receives id, aria-describedby and aria-invalid automatically. Set an accessible name with aria-label. The errorMessage control demonstrates the error state without validating or replacing the content.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Field } from '@plexui/vue/components/Field';
import { MarkdownEditor } from '@plexui/vue/components/MarkdownEditor';

const value = ref('');
const errorMessage = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
  <Field label="Description" description="Supports bold, italic, lists, and links."
    :error-message="errorMessage ? 'This field is required.' : undefined">
    <MarkdownEditor aria-label="Description" placeholder="Enter description..." v-model:value="value" />
  </Field>
</template>
```

### Toolbar end slot
Use the #toolbarEnd slot to render custom content at the end of the toolbar, for example an &ldquo;Edit translations&rdquo; button. markdownEditorStyles.toolbarButton gives it the style of the built-in toolbar buttons.
```vue
<div class="w-full min-w-0 max-w-[500px]">
  <Field label="Text markdown">
    <MarkdownEditor placeholder="Enter text...">
      <template #toolbarEnd>
        <button
          type="button"
          :class="markdownEditorStyles.toolbarButton"
          aria-label="Edit translations"
          title="Edit translations"
        >
          <Translate />
        </button>
      </template>
    </MarkdownEditor>
  </Field>
</div>
```

### Variants
Use variant to switch between outline (default) and soft styles.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col gap-4 w-full min-w-0 max-w-[500px]">
  <Field label="Outline">
    <MarkdownEditor placeholder="Outline variant..." variant="outline" />
  </Field>
  <Field label="Soft">
    <MarkdownEditor placeholder="Soft variant..." variant="soft" />
  </Field>
</div>
```

## Menu
Dropdown menu for structured actions with grouped items, submenus, checkbox and radio items, and horizontal layouts.
Import: import { Menu, MenuContent, MenuItem, MenuLink, MenuSeparator, MenuTrigger } from "@plexui/vue/components/Menu"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/menu
Props:
  - force-open (boolean) — Keep menu open (controlled)
  - modal (boolean) default: false — Whether menu is modal (focus trap)
  - @open (() => void) — Emitted when menu opens
  - @close (() => void) — Emitted when menu closes
  MenuTrigger:
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Prevent the menu from opening
  MenuContent:
    - width (number | "auto") default: fits content, 250 on a phone — Panel width (numbers are pixels). Without it the panel fits its content, or with touch.css on a phone is 250px, as an iOS menu; "auto" fits the content there too
    - min-width (number | "auto") default: 300 — Minimum panel width
    - max-height (number) — Maximum panel height, in pixels
    - pill (boolean) default: false — Rounder list with fully rounded rows, for menus beside pill controls. MenuSubContent takes it too
    - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "bottom" — Preferred placement. Without it, and without side-offset, a menu on a phone opens upward from a trigger in the lower half of the screen
    - side-offset (number) default: 5 — Distance from the trigger, in pixels. Without it a menu on a phone covers its trigger
    - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Alignment relative to trigger
    - align-offset (number) default: -5 — Offset from the start or end alignment, in pixels. 0 with center alignment, and on a phone when the menu covers its trigger
    - layout ("vertical" | "horizontal") default: "vertical" — Layout direction. Use horizontal for icon toolbars
    - size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Size of horizontal menu items (icon size + padding)
    - @close-auto-focus ((event: Event) => void) — Emitted before focus returns to the trigger; call event.preventDefault() to keep it from moving there
    - @open-auto-focus ((event: Event) => void) — Emitted before focus moves into the menu as it opens; call event.preventDefault() to leave focus where it is, as for a menu that stands open when its screen appears
  MenuItem:
    - description (string) — Second line under the label (or the description slot). The label turns semibold on its own
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the item
    - danger (boolean) default: false — Destructive action: the item is drawn in the danger color
    - @select ((event: Event) => void) — Called when the item is selected; call event.preventDefault() to keep the menu open
    - @click ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Called when the item is clicked; also makes the item interactive
  MenuHeader:
    - description (string) — Line under the title, such as the kind and size of a file (or the description slot)
  MenuHeaderAction:
    - aria-label (string) — Accessible name of the icon-only action (required)
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the action
    - @select ((event: Event) => void) — Emitted when the action is pressed; call event.preventDefault() to keep the menu open
  MenuCheckboxItem:
    - checked / v-model:checked (boolean | "indeterminate") default: false — Checked state
    - @checked-change ((checked: boolean) => void) — Emitted with update:checked when the checkbox toggles
    - @select ((event: Event) => void) — Call event.preventDefault() to keep the menu open
    - indicator-position ("start" | "end") default: "end" — Checkbox position
    - indicator-variant ("solid" | "ghost") default: "solid" — Indicator style
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the item

### Overview
A folder's menu, as Files opens it on iOS: actions with their icons, then the view and the sort order as radio items, Date with its order under it (description) while it is the chosen sort, and Group By in a submenu. Turn off icons for the same menu in plain text, and checkmarks for the folder's actions alone, a shorter menu with no checkmark column. pill on MenuContent makes a rounder list with fully rounded rows, for menus beside pill controls. On a phone each example is a screen whose menu opens from the More button of its top bar, as iOS opens the menu of an album in Photos. There the panel covers the button, its top and end at the button's top and end, and is the 250px panel of iOS menus, so the phone code leaves out width, min-width and size. A pill menu is the same panel there, so the phone view leaves out pill.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost">Downloads <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="220" :pill="pill">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><CheckCircle v-if="icons" /> Select</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FolderPlus v-if="icons" /> New Folder</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileDocument v-if="icons" /> Scan Documents</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Desktop v-if="icons" /> Connect to Server</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <template v-if="checkmarks">
      <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="view">
        <MenuRadioItem value="icons"><VideoGrid v-if="icons" /> Icons</MenuRadioItem>
        <MenuRadioItem value="list"><List v-if="icons" /> List</MenuRadioItem>
      </MenuRadioGroup>
      <MenuSeparator />
      <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="sort">
        <MenuRadioItem value="name">Name</MenuRadioItem>
        <MenuRadioItem value="kind">Kind</MenuRadioItem>
        <MenuRadioItem value="date" :description="sort === 'date' ? 'Newest to Oldest' : undefined">Date</MenuRadioItem>
        <MenuRadioItem value="size">Size</MenuRadioItem>
        <MenuRadioItem value="tags">Tags</MenuRadioItem>
      </MenuRadioGroup>
      <MenuSeparator />
      <MenuSub>
        <MenuSubTrigger>Group By</MenuSubTrigger>
        <MenuSubContent>
          <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="group">
            <MenuRadioItem value="none">None</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="kind">Kind</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="date">Date</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="size">Size</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="shared">Shared by</MenuRadioItem>
          </MenuRadioGroup>
        </MenuSubContent>
      </MenuSub>
    </template>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">View Options</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone: the More button of the top bar opens the menu -->
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Overview</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><CheckCircle v-if="icons" /> Select</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FolderPlus v-if="icons" /> New Folder</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileDocument v-if="icons" /> Scan Documents</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Desktop v-if="icons" /> Connect to Server</MenuItem>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <template v-if="checkmarks">
          <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="view">
            <MenuRadioItem value="icons"><VideoGrid v-if="icons" /> Icons</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="list"><List v-if="icons" /> List</MenuRadioItem>
          </MenuRadioGroup>
          <MenuSeparator />
          <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="sort">
            <MenuRadioItem value="name">Name</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="kind">Kind</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="date" :description="sort === 'date' ? 'Newest to Oldest' : undefined">Date</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="size">Size</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="tags">Tags</MenuRadioItem>
          </MenuRadioGroup>
          <MenuSeparator />
          <MenuSub>
            <MenuSubTrigger>Group By</MenuSubTrigger>
            <MenuSubContent>
              <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="group">
                <MenuRadioItem value="none">None</MenuRadioItem>
                <MenuRadioItem value="kind">Kind</MenuRadioItem>
                <MenuRadioItem value="date">Date</MenuRadioItem>
                <MenuRadioItem value="size">Size</MenuRadioItem>
                <MenuRadioItem value="shared">Shared by</MenuRadioItem>
              </MenuRadioGroup>
            </MenuSubContent>
          </MenuSub>
        </template>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}">View Options</MenuItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Mode switch
Set layout="horizontal" on a MenuRadioGroup at the top of a menu for a mode switch: one mode of several, the options side by side, each an icon over its label, with the chosen one on a fill, above the list, as Photos orders by Added or by Captured. The options are radio items, so the fill moves with the value, no radio indicator is drawn, and choosing one closes the menu, as any item does. On a phone with touch.css they are equal 56px-tall tiles with a 20px icon over a 12px label, 10px from the panel's edges, the chosen one on black at 12%, as in Photos. Phone only: the mode switch is an iOS pattern, which desktop menus do not have; there modes are radio items of the list, as in Radio items below.
```vue
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Mode switch</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuRadioGroup layout="horizontal" v-model:value="sort">
          <MenuRadioItem value="added"><Clock /> Added</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="captured"><CameraPhoto /> Captured</MenuRadioItem>
        </MenuRadioGroup>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuLabel>Filter</MenuLabel>
        <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="filter">
          <MenuRadioItem value="all"><Grid /> All items</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="favorites"><Heart /> Favorites</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="edited"><EditPencil /> Edited</MenuRadioItem>
        </MenuRadioGroup>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Header
Put a MenuHeader first to name what the menu acts on: a picture (the media slot), the name and a line under it, with a round MenuHeaderAction at its end (the action slot), as the Files menu of a file in iOS opens. The header is not an item; its action is, reached with the arrow keys like the items. On a phone with touch.css the picture is 40px, 16px from the panel's edges, the name 15px Semibold and the line under it 13px; the action is a 34px circle with a 44px target. A separator under the header stands 16px below the picture; without one the first item starts 24px below it. Phone only: the header is an iOS pattern, which desktop menus do not have.
```vue
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Header</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuHeader description="Image · 382 KB">
          <template #media><img src="/demo/picture-1.svg" alt="" /></template>
          Sunset.png
          <template #action>
            <MenuHeaderAction aria-label="Share" @select="() => {}"><Share /></MenuHeaderAction>
          </template>
        </MenuHeader>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /> Rename</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Copy /> Copy</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Folder /> Move</MenuItem>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><InfoCircle /> Get info</MenuItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Grouped items
Use MenuGroup and MenuLabel to organize related items into labeled sections. The example is the filter of the message list, as Messages opens it on iOS: the mailboxes, a section labeled Filter By, and Manage Filtering under a separator. Both radio groups hold one value, so one checkmark marks the list shown. On a phone a section label and a row without an icon start after the checkmark column, where the icons of the other rows start, as in iOS menus.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button variant="ghost" color="primary"><Filter /> Filter <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="220">
    <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="filter">
      <MenuRadioItem value="messages"><Chats /> Messages</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="spam"><XSquare /> Spam</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="deleted"><Trash /> Recently Deleted</MenuRadioItem>
    </MenuRadioGroup>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuGroup>
      <MenuLabel>Filter By</MenuLabel>
      <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="filter">
        <MenuRadioItem value="unread"><Chat /> Unread</MenuRadioItem>
        <MenuRadioItem value="drafts"><ChatTripleDots /> Drafts</MenuRadioItem>
      </MenuRadioGroup>
    </MenuGroup>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Manage Filtering</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone: the More button of the top bar opens the menu -->
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Grouped items</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="filter">
          <MenuRadioItem value="messages"><Chats /> Messages</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="spam"><XSquare /> Spam</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="deleted"><Trash /> Recently Deleted</MenuRadioItem>
        </MenuRadioGroup>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuGroup>
          <MenuLabel>Filter By</MenuLabel>
          <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="filter">
            <MenuRadioItem value="unread"><Chat /> Unread</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="drafts"><ChatTripleDots /> Drafts</MenuRadioItem>
          </MenuRadioGroup>
        </MenuGroup>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Manage Filtering</MenuItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Submenus
A list's menu, as Reminders opens it on iOS: Manage Sections and Sort By open submenus, and Sort By shows the order it has chosen under its label (description on MenuSubTrigger). Use MenuSub, MenuSubTrigger, and MenuSubContent for nested menus. On a phone a submenu opens over its menu, as iOS 26 submenus do: its row heads it in Semibold with a separator under it and a chevron that points down, and the menu behind steps back, its rows at half strength and the panel slightly smaller, until the row is tapped again. The menu behind is out of reach while the submenu stands over it: a press on it closes the submenu and chooses nothing, and screen readers skip it, with focus in the submenu. The heading row stays in place and only the items under it scroll in a submenu taller than the screen, so the row that closes it is always in reach.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform aria-label="List actions">
      <DotsHorizontal />
    </Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent align="end" :min-width="220">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Storyboard /> View as Columns</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><InfoCircle /> Show List Info</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><CheckCircle /> Select Items</MenuItem>
    <MenuSub>
      <MenuSubTrigger><List /> Manage Sections</MenuSubTrigger>
      <MenuSubContent>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Plus /> New Section</MenuItem>
      </MenuSubContent>
    </MenuSub>
    <MenuSub>
      <MenuSubTrigger :description="sort"><Sort /> Sort By</MenuSubTrigger>
      <MenuSubContent>
        <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="sort">
          <MenuRadioItem value="Manual">Manual</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="Due Date">Due Date</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="Creation Date">Creation Date</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="Priority">Priority</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="Title">Title</MenuRadioItem>
        </MenuRadioGroup>
      </MenuSubContent>
    </MenuSub>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EyeOff /> Hide Completed</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><UploadDocuments /> Save as Template</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileDocument /> Print</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}" danger><Trash /> Delete List</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone: the More button of the top bar opens the menu -->
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Submenus</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Storyboard /> View as Columns</MenuItem>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><InfoCircle /> Show List Info</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><CheckCircle /> Select Items</MenuItem>
        <MenuSub>
          <MenuSubTrigger><List /> Manage Sections</MenuSubTrigger>
          <MenuSubContent>
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Plus /> New Section</MenuItem>
          </MenuSubContent>
        </MenuSub>
        <MenuSub>
          <MenuSubTrigger :description="sort"><Sort /> Sort By</MenuSubTrigger>
          <MenuSubContent>
            <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="sort">
              <MenuRadioItem value="Manual">Manual</MenuRadioItem>
              <MenuRadioItem value="Due Date">Due Date</MenuRadioItem>
              <MenuRadioItem value="Creation Date">Creation Date</MenuRadioItem>
              <MenuRadioItem value="Priority">Priority</MenuRadioItem>
              <MenuRadioItem value="Title">Title</MenuRadioItem>
            </MenuRadioGroup>
          </MenuSubContent>
        </MenuSub>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EyeOff /> Hide Completed</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><UploadDocuments /> Save as Template</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><FileDocument /> Print</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}" danger><Trash /> Delete List</MenuItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Checkbox items
Use MenuCheckboxItem with v-model:checked and {'@select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"'} to keep the menu open. The example is the view options of a mailbox, as Mail opens them on iOS: three options that are each on or off, and About Categories under a separator. On a phone with touch.css the checkmark leads the row, as in iOS menus, whatever indicatorPosition and indicatorVariant say, so the phone view leaves out those controls. A row without a checkmark keeps its column, so every icon and label lines up, and a long label wraps under itself.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button variant="ghost" color="primary">View <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="220">
    <MenuLabel>View Options</MenuLabel>
    <MenuCheckboxItem
      v-model:checked="view.categories"
      :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
      :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
      @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
    >
      <Storyboard /> Show Categories
    </MenuCheckboxItem>
    <MenuCheckboxItem
      v-model:checked="view.priority"
      :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
      :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
      @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
    >
      <Atom /> Show Priority
    </MenuCheckboxItem>
    <MenuCheckboxItem
      v-model:checked="view.contactPhotos"
      :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
      :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
      @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
    >
      <AvatarProfile /> Show Contact Photos
    </MenuCheckboxItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><InfoCircle /> About Categories</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone: the More button of the top bar opens the menu -->
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Checkbox items</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuLabel>View Options</MenuLabel>
        <MenuCheckboxItem
          v-model:checked="view.categories"
          :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
          :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
          @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
        >
          <Storyboard /> Show Categories
        </MenuCheckboxItem>
        <MenuCheckboxItem
          v-model:checked="view.priority"
          :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
          :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
          @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
        >
          <Atom /> Show Priority
        </MenuCheckboxItem>
        <MenuCheckboxItem
          v-model:checked="view.contactPhotos"
          :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
          :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
          @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
        >
          <AvatarProfile /> Show Contact Photos
        </MenuCheckboxItem>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><InfoCircle /> About Categories</MenuItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Radio items
Use MenuRadioGroup and MenuRadioItem for single-selection options. Bind the selection with v-model:value. On a phone with touch.css the checkmark leads the row, as in iOS menus, whatever indicatorPosition says, so the phone view leaves out that control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost">Radio menu <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent align="start" min-width="auto" width="auto">
    <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="value" :indicator-position="indicatorPosition">
      <MenuRadioItem value="any">Any time</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="today" disabled>Today</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="last7d">Last 7 days</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="last30d">Last 30 days</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="last3m">Last 3 months</MenuRadioItem>
    </MenuRadioGroup>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone: the More button of the top bar opens the menu -->
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Radio items</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="value" :indicator-position="indicatorPosition">
          <MenuRadioItem value="any">Any time</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="today" disabled>Today</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="last7d">Last 7 days</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="last30d">Last 30 days</MenuRadioItem>
          <MenuRadioItem value="last3m">Last 3 months</MenuRadioItem>
        </MenuRadioGroup>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Destructive items
Mark a destructive action (delete, remove, leave) with danger: the label and the highlight take the danger color, and a description keeps its secondary color. MenuLink takes danger too. Put such items last, after a separator.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform aria-label="More actions">
      <DotsHorizontal />
    </Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent align="end" :min-width="200">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /> Rename</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Copy /> Duplicate</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}" danger><Trash /> Delete project</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone: the More button of the top bar opens the menu -->
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarTitle>Destructive items</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent align="end">
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /> Rename</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Copy /> Duplicate</MenuItem>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}" danger><Trash /> Delete project</MenuItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Natural sizing
Menu supports several sizing strategies:Natural sizing: use min-width="auto" to have the menu fit naturally to the size of the content.Fixed size: use width and min-width to set the menu to a fixed size, regardless of content size.Flexible sizing: min-width can be used by itself to describe a menu that must be a given size, but can grow larger depending on its content.Max sizing: apply max-width to the children of MenuContent. maxWidth isn't exposed as a prop because it's reserved for the custom properties used in collision detection.Desktop only: on a phone a menu is the 250px panel of iOS menus, unless it sets a width or a larger minimum width of its own.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost" class="font-semibold gap-1.5"><Tools /> Tools</Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent min-width="auto">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Functions :height="18" :width="18" /> Function</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Search :height="18" :width="18" /> File Search</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Globe :height="18" :width="18" /> Web Search</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Code :height="18" :width="18" /> Code Interpreter</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><ImageSquare :height="18" :width="18" /> Image Generation</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Horizontal (icons only)
Set layout="horizontal" on MenuContent for an icon toolbar. Give each icon-only item a visually hidden label so screen readers can name the action. Desktop only: iOS has no row of icons alone; on a phone a horizontal row is the labelled mode switch at the top of a menu, as in Mode switch above.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost">Actions <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent layout="horizontal" :size="size" min-width="auto">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /><span class="sr-only">Edit</span></MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><SettingsCog /><span class="sr-only">Settings</span></MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Play /><span class="sr-only">Play</span></MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><UnfoldHorizontal /><span class="sr-only">Expand</span></MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Item actions
Use MenuItemActions and MenuItemAction for hover actions (e.g. delete) on an item.Accessibility note: these actions are not keyboard accessible due to how the menu primitive works, so they should not be critical actions with no other parity.Desktop only: the rows of an iOS menu carry no actions of their own.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost" class="font-semibold gap-1.5"><History /> Conversations</Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :width="270">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1 truncate">Sample thread title</span>
      <MenuItemActions>
        <MenuItemAction @click="() => {}"><Trash /></MenuItemAction>
      </MenuItemActions>
    </MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1 truncate">Another sample thread title</span>
      <MenuItemActions>
        <MenuItemAction @click="() => {}"><Trash /></MenuItemAction>
      </MenuItemActions>
    </MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1 truncate">Long thread title that is going to be clipped off</span>
      <MenuItemActions>
        <MenuItemAction @click="() => {}"><Trash /></MenuItemAction>
      </MenuItemActions>
    </MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

## OTP Input
Input for one-time passwords and verification codes, with split digit cells, grouped layouts, error states, and autofill.
Import: import { OTPInput } from "@plexui/vue/components/OTPInput"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/otp-input
Props:
  - length (number) default: 6 — Number of digit cells
  - value / v-model:value (string) — Controlled value; `update:value` fires on every change
  - @change ((value: string) => void) — Fires when the value changes
  - @complete ((value: string) => void) — Fires when all digits are filled
  - grouping (number[]) — Grouping pattern with extra gap (e.g. [3,3])
  - invalid (boolean) default: false — Invalid visual state
  - error-message (string) — Error message below the input
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable all cells
  - auto-focus (boolean) default: false — Auto-focus first cell on mount
  - class (string) — Applied to the root element

### Overview
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { OTPInput } from '@plexui/vue/components/OTPInput';

const value = ref('');
const error = ref(false);
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<OTPInput
  v-model:value="value"
  :invalid="error"
  :error-message="error ? 'Invalid verification code.' : undefined"
  :disabled="disabled"
/>
</template>
```

### Grouped (3+3)
```vue
<OTPInput v-model:value="value" :grouping="[3, 3]" />
```

## Page
"The frame of an app page: header and content share one column, a line comes as the content scrolls, and on a phone the header becomes a floating glass bar."
Import: import { Page, PageActions, PageBar, PageContent, PageFooter, PageHeader, PageHeading, PageLeading, PageSubtitle, PageTitle } from "@plexui/vue/components/Page"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/page
Props:
  - width ("full" | "wide" | "column") default: "full" — Column that header and content share: edge to edge, 1280px or 768px, centred
  - surface ("plain" | "grouped") default: "plain" — Mobile canvas and field-group palette; desktop colors stay unchanged
  - class (string) — Class of the root element
  PageHeader:
    - background ("soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "blur") default: "glass" — How the bar meets the content scrolled under it on a phone, see Background. solid is the earlier name of hard
    - divider (boolean) default: true — Shows a divider once the content has scrolled under the header: on a desktop layout, and on a phone with a hard or glass background
    - class (string) — Class of the band
  PageContent:
    - fill (boolean) default: false — The content fills the page and scrolls inside itself, from 768px up
    - class (string) — Class of the element that scrolls

### Overview
The header has no line at rest. Scroll the content: the line comes after 4px and goes again at the top. Turn divider off to keep the header without it. A page with fill does not scroll under its header, so on desktop the line does not come and the divider is offered without fill; on a phone only a glass or hard header draws the line, so there the divider is offered with those. width belongs to the page, so the line, the title, the buttons and the content always stand in one column. Take full for tables, wide for overviews and column for settings and forms; for any other width set --page-width on the page. This frame is narrower than a screen, so the preview shows both widths at half their size. fill stops the page scrolling from 768px up, so a table can scroll inside itself. On a phone a page takes the width of the screen at every width and scrolls as a whole, so the phone has neither setting. PageHeading groups the title with metadata in PageSubtitle. On a phone the badge and subtitle share a second row below the centered title. The header grows when metadata wraps, and the content keeps the corresponding space below it. PageLeading holds a Back button or the menu button of a sidebar, before the title; on a phone the title stays centred between it and the actions. On a phone background sets how the header and the footer meet the content scrolled under them, as a Toolbar does: glass, the header's default, is a translucent, blurred bar with a hairline, soft, the footer's default, blurs the content under a header behind a thin veil of the canvas color and under the footer's actions the bottom edge of iOS 27.2, a blur behind a veil that grows toward the screen's edge, once content runs on under them, hard is an opaque bar with a hairline, and blur blurs the content under the bar, the more toward the screen's edge, with no fill and no line. Glass, hard and blur show themselves once content is under the bar. surface="grouped" gives the phone canvas the gray of grouped rows; desktop colors stay. The footer and its background stand under the preview, as what sits at the bottom of the screen.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';

const width = ref("full"); // or "wide", "column"; from 768px up
const surface = ref("plain"); // or "grouped"; on a phone
const topBackground = ref("glass"); // or "soft", "hard", "blur"; on a phone
const divider = ref(true); // on desktop without fill; on a phone, under a glass or hard header
const leading = ref(false);
const subtitle = ref(false);
const actions = ref(true);
const fill = ref(false); // from 768px up
const footer = ref(false);
const bottomBackground = ref("soft"); // or "hard", "glass", "blur"; on a phone
</script>

<template>
<Page :width="width" :surface="surface">
  <PageHeader :background="topBackground" :divider="divider">
    <PageBar>
      <PageLeading v-if="leading">
        <NavigationButton action="back" />
      </PageLeading>
      <PageHeading v-if="subtitle">
        <PageTitle>Orders</PageTitle>
        <PageSubtitle>
          <Badge color="info" size="sm" variant="outline">24 open</Badge>
          <span>Updated now</span>
        </PageSubtitle>
      </PageHeading>
      <PageTitle v-else>Orders</PageTitle>
      <PageActions v-if="actions">
        <Button color="primary" pill size="md">New order</Button>
      </PageActions>
    </PageBar>
  </PageHeader>
  <PageContent :fill="fill">
    <div v-if="fill" class="min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-auto">...</div>
    <template v-else>...</template>
  </PageContent>
  <PageFooter v-if="footer" :background="bottomBackground">
    <Button color="primary" pill block>Export orders</Button>
  </PageFooter>
</Page>
</template>
```

## Page Control
A row of dots, one for each page, the current one solid, that shows where a person is in a flat list of pages and moves between them, as the iOS 26 page control does.
Import: import { PageControl } from "@plexui/vue/components/PageControl"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/page-control
Props:
  - count (number) — Number of pages
  - value (number) — Controlled index of the current page, from 0; bind with v-model:value
  - default-value (number) default: 0 — Initial page (uncontrolled)
  - background ("automatic" | "prominent" | "minimal") default: "automatic" — When the capsule behind the dots shows; minimal also turns off scrubbing
  - value-text ((index: number, count: number) => string) default: Page 2 of 5 — Text assistive technology reads for the current page
  - aria-label (string) default: "Pages" — Name of the page control, when no visible label names it
  - aria-labelledby (string) — The id of the visible label of the page control
  - aria-controls (string) — The id of the element that shows the pages
  - id (string) — The id of the page control

### Overview
Tap the leading or trailing side of the current dot for the previous or next page; with a mouse, click a dot. Drag across the dots to scrub, or past either end to reach the first or last page. Scroll the pages without animation while scrubbing, as details.scrubbing tells, and with reduced motion, as motion-reduce:scroll-auto does here.
```vue
<div class="flex w-full max-w-[320px] flex-col items-center gap-3">
  <div
    id="page-control-pages"
    ref="pages"
    class="flex w-full snap-x snap-mandatory overflow-x-auto scroll-smooth motion-reduce:scroll-auto rounded-2xl bg-[var(--color-surface-secondary)] [scrollbar-width:none]"
    @scroll="page = Math.round($event.target.scrollLeft / $event.target.clientWidth)"
  >
    <div v-for="number in 5" :key="number" class="flex h-40 w-full shrink-0 snap-center items-center justify-center text-2xl font-semibold tabular-nums">
      {{ number }}
    </div>
  </div>
  <PageControl
    :count="5"
    v-model:value="page"
    aria-controls="page-control-pages"
    @change="(index, details) => $refs.pages.scrollTo({ left: index * $refs.pages.clientWidth, behavior: details.scrubbing ? 'instant' : 'auto' })"
  />
</div>
```

### Many pages
From seven pages the dots past a window around the current page shrink to 6px and 4px, suggesting more pages, and the rest hide, as the kit variants show.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col items-center gap-2">
  <PageControl :count="12" v-model:value="page" />
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary tabular-nums" aria-hidden="true">Page {{ page + 1 }} of 12</span>
</div>
```

### Background
The capsule behind the dots shows while a pointer presses or scrubs with automatic, always with prominent, for a page control that is the main navigation of the screen, and never with minimal, which also turns off scrubbing since it gives no feedback.
```vue
<div class="grid grid-cols-[auto_auto] items-center gap-x-6 gap-y-4">
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Automatic</span>
  <PageControl :count="4" aria-label="Automatic background" />
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Prominent</span>
  <PageControl :count="4" aria-label="Prominent background" background="prominent" />
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Minimal</span>
  <PageControl :count="4" aria-label="Minimal background" background="minimal" />
</div>
```

## Phone Layout
Lays out the components inside it as on a phone at any window width, such as a phone frame on a desktop page, and keeps their dialogs inside its box.
Import: import { PhoneLayout } from "@plexui/vue/components/PhoneLayout"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/phone-layout
Props:
  - class (string) — Class on the box, such as its size and corners
  - style (string | CSSProperties) — Style on the box

### Overview
The box is the screen of the phone: a Dialog opened inside it portals into the box and covers it instead of the window. Give the box a size; let a child of it scroll rather than the box, so a dialog stays in place.
```vue
<PhoneLayout style="width: 375px; height: 600px; border-radius: 40px; border: 1px solid var(--color-border); background: var(--color-background)">
  <div style="display: flex; height: 100%; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; justify-content: center; padding: 24px">
    <Dialog v-model:open="open">
      <DialogTrigger>
        <Button color="primary">New list</Button>
      </DialogTrigger>
      <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet">
        <DialogHeader>
          <DialogTitle>New list</DialogTitle>
        </DialogHeader>
        <DialogBody>
          <Field label="Name">
            <Input placeholder="Groceries" />
          </Field>
        </DialogBody>
        <DialogFooter>
          <Button color="primary" @click="open = false">Create</Button>
        </DialogFooter>
      </DialogContent>
    </Dialog>
  </div>
</PhoneLayout>
```

### Menu
A menu opened inside the box is a phone menu at any window width: it covers the button that opens it and opens in the box. A submenu opens over its menu, which steps back: a press on the menu behind closes the submenu and chooses nothing, and screen readers skip its rows until then.
```vue
<PhoneLayout style="width: 375px; height: 600px; border-radius: 40px; border: 1px solid var(--color-border); background: var(--color-background)">
  <Page>
    <Toolbar title-style="large">
      <ToolbarTitle>Groceries</ToolbarTitle>
      <ToolbarTrailing>
        <Menu>
          <MenuTrigger>
            <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
          </MenuTrigger>
          <MenuContent align="end">
            <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><CheckCircle /> Select Items</MenuItem>
            <MenuSub>
              <MenuSubTrigger :description="sort"><Sort /> Sort By</MenuSubTrigger>
              <MenuSubContent>
                <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="sort">
                  <MenuRadioItem value="Manual">Manual</MenuRadioItem>
                  <MenuRadioItem value="Due Date">Due Date</MenuRadioItem>
                  <MenuRadioItem value="Title">Title</MenuRadioItem>
                </MenuRadioGroup>
              </MenuSubContent>
            </MenuSub>
            <MenuItem danger @select="() => {}"><Trash /> Delete List</MenuItem>
          </MenuContent>
        </Menu>
      </ToolbarTrailing>
    </Toolbar>
  </Page>
</PhoneLayout>
```

## Popover
A floating panel for contextual UI, with flexible sizing, hover triggers, a controllable open state and nesting.
Import: import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from "@plexui/vue/components/Popover"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/popover
Props:
  - open / v-model:open (boolean) — Controlled open state; uncontrolled when omitted
  - @update:open ((nextState: boolean) => void) — Emitted when the popover opens or closes
  - show-on-hover (boolean) default: false — Open on hover and focus instead of click
  - hover-open-delay (number) default: 150 — Delay before a hover popover opens (ms)
  PopoverContent:
    - width (number | "auto") default: "auto" — Panel width in px
    - min-width (number | "auto") default: 300 — Minimum panel width in px
    - max-width (number) default: 700 — Maximum panel width in px, capped by the space available in the viewport
    - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "bottom" — Preferred placement; flips when it collides
    - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Alignment relative to trigger
    - side-offset (number) default: 8 — Offset from trigger (px)
    - align-offset (number) default: -5 — Offset from the start or end alignment (px)
    - avoid-collisions (boolean) default: true — Reposition to stay inside the viewport and scroll containers
    - collision-padding (number | { top?, right?, bottom?, left? }) default: 20 — Space in px to keep from viewport and scroll container boundaries; an object such as `{ top: 16, right: 32, bottom: 16, left: 32 }` sets it per side, and a side it leaves out gets 0
    - translucent (boolean) default: false — Translucent, blurred surface
    - auto-focus (boolean) default: true — Move focus into the panel when it opens (hover popovers never take focus)
    - data-* (string) — Data attributes land on the panel
    - class (string) — Applied to the panel
    - default slot ({ close, shake }) — Panel content; receives the controller functions

### Overview
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
</script>

<template>
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Generate</Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'">
    <div class="p-4">
      <p class="text-sm text-secondary">Popover content. Use with any floating UI.</p>
    </div>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
</template>
```

### Sizing the popover
The popover can be sized with any combination of width, min-width, or max-width. All popovers default to a min-width of 300px, and a max-width of 700px, capped by the space available in the viewport.
```vue
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="sm" variant="ghost" :pill="false" gutter-size="xs" class="gap-1.5">
      <Bolt /> Actions
    </Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent class="flex p-1.5" min-width="auto" side="top">
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Functions :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Search :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Globe :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Code :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <ImageSquare :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
```

### Using the controller
The default slot of PopoverContent receives {"{ close, shake }"}. Components rendered inside it can get the same two methods from usePopoverController() in their setup(). They do exactly what you think they do.Attempting to submit an empty form calls the shake() methodAfter the simulated two-second request, close() dismisses the popup. Closing or unmounting cancels the pending request; reopening starts a fresh session.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { onBeforeUnmount, ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Sparkles } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { Textarea } from '@plexui/vue/components/Textarea';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
const open = ref(false);
const prompt = ref('');
const submitting = ref(false);
let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
let session = 0;

onBeforeUnmount(() => {
  session++;
  clearTimeout(timer);
});

function setOpen(next: boolean) {
  session++;
  clearTimeout(timer);
  timer = undefined;
  if (next) {
    submitting.value = false;
    prompt.value = '';
  }
  open.value = next;
}

function setPrompt(value: string) {
  prompt.value = value;
}

function submitPrompt(close: () => void, shake: () => void) {
  if (!prompt.value) {
    shake();
    return;
  }
  if (timer !== undefined) return;
  submitting.value = true;
  const currentSession = session;
  timer = setTimeout(() => {
    if (session !== currentSession) return;
    timer = undefined;
    close();
  }, 2000);
}
</script>

<template>
<Popover :open="open" @update:open="setOpen">
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Generate</Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'" v-slot="{ close, shake }">
    <form class="pt-2" @submit.prevent="submitPrompt(close, shake)">
      <Textarea
        :value="prompt"
        @update:value="setPrompt"
        aria-label="Prompt"
        variant="ghost"
        gutter-size="xl"
        :rows="3"
        class="w-full"
        placeholder="Describe what you're using the model for, and we'll generate system instructions."
      />
      <div class="px-3 pb-3 flex items-center justify-between">
        <div class="flex gap-1.5 items-center font-normal text-sm text-tertiary">
          <Sparkles :width="16" :height="16" />
          Free beta
        </div>
        <Button color="primary" size="xs" :pill="false" :loading="submitting" type="submit">
          Create
        </Button>
      </div>
    </form>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
</template>
```

### Confirmation
Confirm a destructive action from the button that asks for it. On a desktop the panel opens above the button. On a phone it covers the button, as iOS presents an action sheet from a bar button: no Cancel button, since a tap anywhere else cancels, the message in the body text and the action as a 48px capsule, with the insets of the iOS 27.2 alert. Opening moves focus to the panel, not to the action. Copy the shared styles with the example.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';
import styles from './PopoverConfirmation.module.css';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
</script>

<template>
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="danger" variant="soft" :size="wide ? 'md' : '3xl'" :block="!wide" :pill="!wide">
      Delete event
    </Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent side="top" :side-offset="wide ? 8 : -34" :class="styles.Confirmation" v-slot="{ close }">
    <p :class="styles.Message">Are you sure you want to delete this event?</p>
    <Button color="danger" variant="soft" block :pill="!wide" :class="styles.Action" @click="close()">
      Delete event
    </Button>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
</template>
```

### Column configuration
A column-visibility panel built with Popover and Switch. Toggling a switch moves the column between shown and hidden groups. For a DataTable, turn the shown ids into its v-model:column-visibility, an object of column id to shown (Table › Column visibility). On a phone the panel is a sheet (Dialog with mobile-variant="sheet") of Field rows with switches, in a fixed order: as in iOS settings, a row stays in place when its switch changes. It opens from the More bar button of the screen's Toolbar, three dots on glass, as iOS apps open their view options.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" size="sm"><SettingsSlider :width="15" :height="15" /> Columns</Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent :min-width="240" side="bottom" align="start">
    <div class="py-1.5">
      <div v-if="shown.length > 0">
        <div
          v-for="col in allColumns.filter((c) => shown.includes(c.id))"
          :key="col.id"
          class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4 px-3 py-1.5 cursor-pointer hover:bg-alpha/5"
          @click="shown = shown.filter((c) => c !== col.id)"
        >
          <span class="text-sm">{{ col.label }}</span>
          <Switch :checked="true" :aria-label="col.label" />
        </div>
      </div>
      <div v-if="shown.length < allColumns.length">
        <div
          v-for="col in allColumns.filter((c) => !shown.includes(c.id))"
          :key="col.id"
          class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4 px-3 py-1.5 cursor-pointer hover:bg-alpha/5"
          @click="shown = [...shown, col.id]"
        >
          <span class="text-sm text-tertiary">{{ col.label }}</span>
          <Switch :checked="false" :aria-label="col.label" />
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>

<!-- Phone: a bar button of the top bar opens a sheet of switch rows in a fixed order. -->
<Page>
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarTitle>Members</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <Dialog>
        <DialogTrigger>
          <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Columns"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
        </DialogTrigger>
        <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })">
          <DialogHeader>
            <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>Columns</DialogTitle></div>
          </DialogHeader>
          <DialogBody>
            <FieldGroup>
              <Field v-for="col in allColumns" :key="col.id" row :label="col.label">
                <Switch color="accent" :checked="shown.includes(col.id)" @update:checked="(on) => (shown = on ? [...shown, col.id] : shown.filter((c) => c !== col.id))" />
              </Field>
            </FieldGroup>
          </DialogBody>
        </DialogContent>
      </Dialog>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
```

### Open on hover
Use show-on-hover to create popovers that appear on hover and focus events. This should be used sparingly and with clear intention. A phone has no hover: a tap opens it, as in the Overview, so this example is desktop only.By default, hover popovers will not alter the focused element on the page, nor restore focus to the trigger on close. However, if interactive content is present in the popover, and tab is used to navigate into the popover, focus will be restored to the trigger on close.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
</script>

<template>
<div class="flex flex-col gap-4">
  <Popover show-on-hover>
    <PopoverTrigger>
      <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Static content</Button>
    </PopoverTrigger>
    <PopoverContent :width="320" :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'">
      <div class="p-3">
        <h3 class="mb-0.5 font-semibold">Popover Title</h3>
        <p class="text-sm text-secondary">
          This is an example of a hoverable popover containing presentational content.
        </p>
        <footer class="mt-2 text-xs text-tertiary">Last updated June 20, 2025</footer>
      </div>
    </PopoverContent>
  </Popover>
  <Popover show-on-hover>
    <PopoverTrigger>
      <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Interactive content</Button>
    </PopoverTrigger>
    <PopoverContent :min-width="230" :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'" v-slot="{ close }">
      <div class="p-3">
        <h3 class="mb-0.5 text-sm font-semibold">Popover with actions</h3>
        <p class="text-sm text-secondary">Try using tab to navigate</p>
        <div class="mt-3 flex gap-2">
          <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" :pill="false" @click="close">
            Cancel
          </Button>
          <Button color="primary" :pill="false" @click="close">
            Confirm
          </Button>
        </div>
      </div>
    </PopoverContent>
  </Popover>
</div>
</template>
```

## Progress
A horizontal bar that shows task completion, with determinate and indeterminate modes, labelled layouts, and smooth value transitions.
Import: import { Progress } from "@plexui/vue/components/Progress"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/progress
Props:
  - value (number | null) default: null — Current progress between 0 and max. Pass null for indeterminate.
  - max (number) default: 100 — Maximum progress value
  - get-value-label ((value: number, max: number) => string) — Accessible value text (aria-valuetext). Defaults to the rounded percentage, e.g. "67%".
  - class (string) — Applied to the root <div>

### Overview
```vue
<script setup>
import { onMounted, onUnmounted, ref } from "vue"
import { Progress } from "@plexui/vue/components/Progress"

const value = ref(13)
let timer
onMounted(() => {
  timer = setTimeout(() => (value.value = 66), 500)
})
onUnmounted(() => clearTimeout(timer))
</script>

<template>
  <Progress :value="value" aria-label="Loading" />
</template>
```

### With label
Pair a Label with a value readout above the bar, the typical upload or onboarding progress pattern.
```vue
<div style="display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 8px; width: 100%; max-width: 320px">
  <div style="display: flex; align-items: baseline; justify-content: space-between; gap: 16px">
    <Label id="progress-upload-label">Uploading plex-ui.zip</Label>
    <span style="color: var(--color-text-tertiary); font-size: 14px">3.2 / 4.8 MB</span>
  </div>
  <Progress aria-labelledby="progress-upload-label" :value="67" />
</div>
```

### Controlled
Bind value to any source, here a Slider through v-model:value.
```vue
<div style="display: flex; width: 100%; max-width: 320px; flex-direction: column; gap: 16px">
  <Progress :value="value" aria-label="Progress" />
  <Slider v-model:value="value" :min="0" :max="100" :step="1" aria-label="Progress value" />
</div>
```

## Progress Steps
Stepper for multi-step flows like checkouts and onboarding, with horizontal or vertical layout, icons, navigation, and color tokens.
Import: import { ProgressSteps, ProgressStepsStep, ProgressStepsTitle, ProgressStepsDescription } from "@plexui/vue/components/ProgressSteps"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/progress-steps
Props:
  - current (number) — Required. Current active step (1-based)
  - orientation ("horizontal" | "vertical") default: "horizontal" — Layout direction
  - size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Step indicator size
  - color ("default" | "success") default: "default" — Color scheme for completed/active steps
  - connector-style ("solid" | "dashed") default: "solid" — Connector line style
  - variant ("default" | "minimal") default: "default" — "minimal" renders a compact bar (requires total)
  - total (number) — Total steps for minimal variant
  - class (string) — Class applied to the container
  ProgressStepsStep:
    - icon (Component | VNode | string) — Custom icon instead of step number; the icon slot takes precedence
  ProgressStepsTitle:
    - visually-hidden (boolean) default: false — Hides the title from view and keeps it for screen readers, for steps that show their indicators alone; the step then takes no room under its indicator

### Overview
current is the active step, counted from 1: the steps before it are completed and show a check. variant="minimal" draws a compact bar of segments instead, one per step, and takes the count as total; it has no orientation, connector, icons or text, so those settings are for the default variant, and it draws sm as md, so it offers md and lg. orientation="vertical" stacks the steps, for a sidebar or a multi-step form. size sets the scale of the indicators, and color the color scheme of completed and active steps. connector-style draws the lines ahead of the current step solid or dashed; a completed line is solid, so at the last step the setting is not offered. The icon slot (or the icon prop) of a ProgressStepsStep shows a custom icon instead of its number. A step shows a ProgressStepsTitle and a ProgressStepsDescription; leave out the description, or show the indicators alone when the step's details are shown elsewhere. Keep the title then for screen readers with visually-hidden: an icon or a check has no name, and the step takes no room under its indicator.On a phone the four columns of a horizontal row cannot hold a step's description, so the steps stand vertically there and the phone has no orientation setting: useBreakpoint("md") tells which to use. Progress steps have no iOS control of their own; With Navigation shows a compact composition for a phone screen.
```vue
<script setup>
import { ref } from "vue"
import { Bolt, Email, Globe, Users } from "@plexui/vue/components/Icon"
import { ProgressSteps, ProgressStepsStep, ProgressStepsTitle, ProgressStepsDescription } from "@plexui/vue/components/ProgressSteps"
import { useBreakpoint } from "@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints"

const variant = ref("default")
const current = ref(2)
const orientation = ref("horizontal")
const size = ref("md") // the minimal bar: "md" or "lg"
const color = ref("default")
const connectorStyle = ref("solid") // dashes the connectors ahead of current
const icons = ref(false)
const text = ref("full") // "full", "title" or "none"

// A phone shows the steps vertically: four columns cannot hold a description
const wide = useBreakpoint("md")

const steps = [
  { title: "Your details", description: "Please provide your name and email", icon: Email },
  { title: "Company details", description: "A few details about your company", icon: Globe },
  { title: "Invite your team", description: "Start collaborating with your team", icon: Users },
  { title: "Add your socials", description: "Share posts to your social accounts", icon: Bolt },
]
</script>

<template>
<ProgressSteps v-if="variant === 'minimal'" variant="minimal" :current="current" :total="steps.length" :size="size" :color="color" />
<ProgressSteps
  v-else
  :current="current"
  :orientation="wide ? orientation : 'vertical'"
  :size="size"
  :color="color"
  :connector-style="connectorStyle"
  class="w-full"
>
  <ProgressStepsStep v-for="step in steps" :key="step.title">
    <template v-if="icons" #icon><component :is="step.icon" /></template>
    <ProgressStepsTitle :visually-hidden="text === 'none'">{{ step.title }}</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription v-if="text === 'full'">{{ step.description }}</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
</ProgressSteps>
</template>
```

### With Navigation
Example of integrating with navigation controls.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col items-center gap-6">
  <ProgressSteps variant="minimal" :current="step" :total="4" />
  <div class="text-center">
    <h3 class="text-lg font-semibold">Step {{ step }} of 4</h3>
    <p class="text-secondary mt-1">{{ steps[Math.min(step - 1, 3)].title }}</p>
  </div>
  <div class="flex gap-3">
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" class="w-28" :disabled="step === 1" @click="step = Math.max(1, step - 1)">
      Previous
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" class="w-28" :disabled="step === 4" @click="step = Math.min(4, step + 1)">
      Next
    </Button>
  </div>
</div>
```

## Radio Group
Radio group for single-choice selection with row or column layout, right-aligned indicators, rich item content, and card-style options.
Import: import { RadioGroup, RadioGroupItem } from "@plexui/vue/components/RadioGroup"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/radio-group
Props:
  - value (string) — Selected value. Bind with v-model:value; leave unset for an uncontrolled group
  - default-value (string) — Initial selected value (uncontrolled)
  - direction ("col" | "row") default: "row" — Item flow direction
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the whole group
  - name (string) — Name of the hidden radio inputs submitted with the owning form
  - required (boolean) default: false — Marks the group and every hidden radio input as required
  - aria-label (string) — Accessible group label
  - class (string) — Extra classes on the group element
  - default slot (VNode[]) — RadioGroupItem children
  - @update:value ((value: string) => void) — Emitted when the selected option changes; powers v-model:value
  - @change ((value: string) => void) — Emitted with update:value, named after the React onChange prop
  RadioGroupItem:
    - value (string) — Item value
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable this item only
    - required (boolean) — Marks this item as required
    - block (boolean) default: false — Stretch item to fill available width
    - orientation ("left" | "right") default: "left" — Position of the radio indicator relative to the label
    - description (string) — Second line below the option's label, linked via aria-describedby (or the description slot)
    - class (string) — Extra classes on the label element
    - default slot (VNode[]) — Label content (text or rich content)

### Overview
direction sets whether the items flow in a column or a row; the gap follows it. orientation="right" on RadioGroupItem places the indicator after the label, for settings lists and card-style selections where the content should lead; in a column, also set block on the items so they fill the group and the indicators line up. Use description (or the description slot) on an item for a second line under its label. It sits outside the label, so the radio keeps the label as its name and gets the description through aria-describedby; clicking it does not select the option. disabled on RadioGroup disables every option; on a single RadioGroupItem it disables only that one, here Monthly, so that setting is offered only while the group is enabled. On a phone each option is a row and the selected row ends with a checkmark. A phone list is always a column and the checkmark always ends the row, so direction and orientation are desktop settings. The row's description takes the item's place, and the label of a disabled option is dimmed with it.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div>
  <h3 class="font-semibold text-sm mb-3">Notification frequency</h3>
  <RadioGroup
    :direction="direction"
    :class="direction === 'col' && orientation === 'right' ? 'w-56' : undefined"
    v-model:value="frequency"
    :disabled="disabled"
    aria-label="Notification frequency"
  >
    <RadioGroupItem
      value="daily"
      :orientation="orientation"
      :block="direction === 'col' && orientation === 'right'"
      :description="description ? 'A summary every morning.' : undefined"
    >
      Daily
    </RadioGroupItem>
    <RadioGroupItem
      value="weekly"
      :orientation="orientation"
      :block="direction === 'col' && orientation === 'right'"
      :description="description ? 'A digest every Monday.' : undefined"
    >
      Weekly
    </RadioGroupItem>
    <RadioGroupItem
      value="monthly"
      :orientation="orientation"
      :block="direction === 'col' && orientation === 'right'"
      :description="description ? 'A recap on the first of the month.' : undefined"
      :disabled="itemDisabled"
    >
      Monthly
    </RadioGroupItem>
    <RadioGroupItem
      value="never"
      :orientation="orientation"
      :block="direction === 'col' && orientation === 'right'"
      :description="description ? 'Only security alerts.' : undefined"
    >
      Never
    </RadioGroupItem>
  </RadioGroup>
</div>
```
