  - prominent ({ value: string; label: string }) — A prominent tab in a glass circle of its own at the trailing end, in place of the search circle; its symbol is the prominent-icon slot, and search then joins the capsule
  - minimize-behavior ("never" | "onScrollDown") default: "never" — Minimize to the selected tab while the Page content scrolls down
  - minimized (boolean) — Controlled minimized state; bind with v-model:minimized
  - default-minimized (boolean) default: false — Minimized state (uncontrolled)
  - background ("soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "blur") default: "soft" — How the bar meets the content scrolled under it, see Background. solid is the earlier name of hard
  - aria-label (string) default: "Tabs" — Name of the navigation
  TabBarItem:
    - value (string) — Value of the tab
    - label (string) — Label under the symbol and the name of the tab
    - badge (string | number) — A count or a short word in a red capsule over the symbol; 0 shows none
    - href (string) — Makes the tab a link
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — The tab cannot be selected

### Overview
Put the bar last in a Page: it reserves its height there. Up to three tabs sit in the middle, 85px apart, as iOS 27 sets them; four and five share the width. The selected tab sits on a pill in the primary text, the others in a lighter gray, as Sidebar items are. With search the search tab takes a glass circle of its own at the trailing end, as the iOS 26 search tab does, and the capsule keeps to the leading end at the width of its tabs; show the search screen when its value is selected. With prominent one key destination, such as a cart or a library, takes that circle instead, as the prominent tab of iOS 27: it is still a tab, the circle shows the symbol from the prominent-icon slot only and label names it, and with both the search tab joins the capsule as its last tab. The bar holds five tabs at most, the search and prominent tabs counted, as in the kit's Tab Bar - iPhone: next to a circle the capsule keeps four, so the example disables the counts that would not fit. With minimize-behavior="onScrollDown" the bar shrinks while the content scrolls down to a circle of the selected tab at the leading end, the search or prominent circle staying at the trailing end, as the minimized tab bar of the kit; it comes back when the content scrolls up, and a tap on the minimized tab brings the others back. A tab's badge shows a count or a short word in a red capsule over its symbol, as an iOS tab badge, and the minimized circle keeps it; a count of 0 shows none. The accessory slot puts the app's own content, a now playing row or a status, in a glass capsule above the tabs, as the bottom accessory of iOS 27: minimized, it moves between the two circles, and the bar keeps its height so the content does not move. background sets how the bar meets the content scrolled under it, as on a Toolbar: soft (the default) leaves the content clear around the capsule, as native iOS 26 does, glass lays a translucent, blurred band with a hairline under it, hard an opaque band with a hairline on top, and blur blurs the list under the bar, the more toward the screen's bottom edge, with no fill and no line. The top bar sets its own background, glass by default, so the example sets the two apart.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarTitle>Home</ToolbarTitle>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
  <TabBar
    v-model:value="tab"
    :search="{ value: 'search' }"
    :prominent="{ value: 'bag', label: 'Bag' }"
    minimize-behavior="onScrollDown"
    background="soft"
  >
    <template #accessory><NowPlaying /></template>
    <TabBarItem value="home" label="Home">
      <template #icon><Home /></template>
    </TabBarItem>
    <TabBarItem value="calendar" label="Calendar" :badge="3">
      <template #icon><Calendar /></template>
    </TabBarItem>
    <TabBarItem value="photos" label="Photos">
      <template #icon><Images /></template>
    </TabBarItem>
    <template #prominent-icon><ShoppingBag /></template>
  </TabBar>
</Page>
```

## Table
Styled table primitives for structured data, with header, body, footer, row, cell and caption parts on native table elements.
Import: import { Table, TableHeader, TableBody, TableRow, TableHead, TableCell } from "@plexui/vue/components/Table"
Import: import { DataTable, DataTableColumnHeader } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/table
  Table Primitives:
    - Table (table) — Root table container with horizontal scroll wrapper; attributes go to the <table>
    - TableHeader (thead) — Table header group
    - TableBody (tbody) — Table body group
    - TableFooter (tfoot) — Table footer group
    - TableRow (tr) — Table row
    - TableHead (th) — Table header cell
    - TableCell (td) — Table data cell
    - TableCaption (caption) — Table caption
  DataTable:
    - columns (ColumnDef<TData>[]) — Column definitions following TanStack Table v8 API; header and cell renderers return VNodes (required)
    - data (TData[]) — Data array to display (required)
    - variant ("default" | "bordered") default: "default" — Visual variant: bordered adds a rounded border and tinted header
    - page-size (number) default: 10 — Rows per page. Set 0 or Infinity to disable pagination. The rows-per-page select offers 10, 20, 50, 100 and this size
    - initial-sorting (SortingState) — Initial column sorting state
    - global-filter (string) — Controlled search/filter string. Bind with v-model:global-filter
    - column-visibility (VisibilityState) — Controlled column visibility state. Bind with v-model:column-visibility
    - enable-row-selection (boolean) default: false — Adds a first column (id select) with a checkbox per row and a header checkbox that selects every row on all pages, mixed when some are selected. A click on a checkbox is not a row click
    - row-selection (RowSelectionState) — Controlled row selection state. Bind with v-model:row-selection
    - get-row-id ((row: TData) => string) — Custom row ID accessor; selection keys use it
    - empty-message (string | number | VNode) default: "No results." — Shown when no rows; the emptyMessage slot takes precedence
    - show-pagination (boolean) default: true — Whether to show pagination controls. They hide on their own when every row fits on one page
    - class (string) — Class applied to outermost wrapper
    - emptyMessage slot (VNode[]) — Rich content shown when no rows
    - @row-click ((row: TData) => void) — Click handler for rows. Makes rows interactive: they take Tab and call it on Enter or Space
    - @update:global-filter ((value: string) => void) — Emitted with the next filter when the table changes it; powers v-model:global-filter
    - @update:column-visibility ((visibility: VisibilityState) => void) — Emitted with the next visibility; powers v-model:column-visibility
    - @update:row-selection ((selection: RowSelectionState) => void) — Emitted with the next selection; powers v-model:row-selection
  DataTableColumnHeader:
    - column (Column<TData, TValue>) — TanStack Table column object (required)
    - title (string) — Display text for the header (required)
    - class (string) — Class applied to the header button (or text when the column cannot sort)
  Column helpers:
    - idCell(accessor) (CellComponent) — Monospace ID with copy-on-click tooltip
    - dateTimeCell(accessor, options?) (CellComponent) — Formatted datetime with Intl.DateTimeFormat in the reader's time zone. It hydrates without a mismatch: the server writes the time in UTC (or in the timeZone of the options), and the reader's zone and wording follow once the page is in the browser. An ISO date or time written without a zone (2023-01-15, 2023-01-15T09:00) shows as written in every zone; a value that is no date shows as it is
    - statusCell(accessor, colorMap?) (CellComponent) — Badge cell with automatic status-to-color mapping
    - tooltipHeader(label, description) (HeaderComponent) — Header with dashed underline and info tooltip

### Overview
A complete table with header, body, footer, and caption showing invoice data.
```vue
<Table>
  <TableCaption>A list of your recent invoices.</TableCaption>
  <TableHeader>
    <TableRow>
      <TableHead>Invoice</TableHead>
      <TableHead>Status</TableHead>
      <TableHead>Method</TableHead>
      <TableHead class="text-right">Amount</TableHead>
    </TableRow>
  </TableHeader>
  <TableBody>
    <TableRow
      v-for="invoice in [
        { invoice: 'INV001', status: 'Paid', method: 'Credit Card', amount: '$250.00' },
        { invoice: 'INV002', status: 'Pending', method: 'PayPal', amount: '$150.00' },
        { invoice: 'INV003', status: 'Unpaid', method: 'Bank Transfer', amount: '$350.00' },
        { invoice: 'INV004', status: 'Paid', method: 'Credit Card', amount: '$450.00' },
        { invoice: 'INV005', status: 'Paid', method: 'PayPal', amount: '$550.00' },
      ]"
      :key="invoice.invoice"
    >
      <TableCell class="font-medium">{{ invoice.invoice }}</TableCell>
      <TableCell>{{ invoice.status }}</TableCell>
      <TableCell>{{ invoice.method }}</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">{{ invoice.amount }}</TableCell>
    </TableRow>
  </TableBody>
  <TableFooter>
    <TableRow>
      <TableCell colspan="3">Total</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">$1,750.00</TableCell>
    </TableRow>
  </TableFooter>
</Table>
```

### With footer
A table with a footer row displaying subtotal information.
```vue
<Table>
  <TableHeader>
    <TableRow>
      <TableHead>Product</TableHead>
      <TableHead class="text-right">Qty</TableHead>
      <TableHead class="text-right">Price</TableHead>
      <TableHead class="text-right">Total</TableHead>
    </TableRow>
  </TableHeader>
  <TableBody>
    <TableRow>
      <TableCell class="font-medium">Pro Plan</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">3</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">$29.00</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">$87.00</TableCell>
    </TableRow>
    <TableRow>
      <TableCell class="font-medium">Storage Add-on</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">1</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">$9.00</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right">$9.00</TableCell>
    </TableRow>
  </TableBody>
  <TableFooter>
    <TableRow>
      <TableCell colspan="3">Subtotal</TableCell>
      <TableCell class="text-right font-semibold">$96.00</TableCell>

  </TableFooter>

```

### Minimal
A simple table without caption or footer, showing a list of people.
```vue
<Table>
  <TableHeader>
    <TableRow>
      <TableHead>Name</TableHead>
      <TableHead>Title</TableHead>
      <TableHead>Email</TableHead>
    </TableRow>
  </TableHeader>
  <TableBody>
    <TableRow
      v-for="person in [
        { name: 'Lindsay Walton', title: 'Front-end Developer', email: 'lindsay@example.com' },
        { name: 'Courtney Henry', title: 'Designer', email: 'courtney@example.com' },
        { name: 'Tom Cook', title: 'Director of Product', email: 'tom@example.com' },
      ]"
      :key="person.email"
    >
      <TableCell class="font-medium">{{ person.name }}</TableCell>
      <TableCell>{{ person.title }}</TableCell>
      <TableCell>{{ person.email }}</TableCell>
    </TableRow>
  </TableBody>
</Table>
```

### Variant
Use variant="bordered" for a card-like table with a rounded border and tinted header background. Toggle empty to supply no rows without resetting sorting or changing columns. emptyMessage selects the default message, custom text through empty-message, or rich content through the emptyMessage slot. Filtering and its no-results state are shown separately.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h, ref } from "vue"
import { DataTable, DataTableColumnHeader, statusCell, type ColumnDef } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const variant = ref<"default" | "bordered">("bordered")
const empty = ref(false)
const emptyMessage = ref<"default" | "text" | "rich">("text")
const variantUsers = users.slice(0, 6)
const emptyUsers: User[] = []

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  {
    accessorKey: "name",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Name" }),
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name),
  },
  { accessorKey: "role", header: "Role" },
  { accessorKey: "department", header: "Department" },
  { accessorKey: "status", header: "Status", cell: statusCell<User>((row) => row.status) },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable :columns="columns" :data="empty ? emptyUsers : variantUsers"
    :variant="variant" :page-size="Infinity"
    :empty-message="emptyMessage === 'default' ? undefined : 'No users found.'">
    <template v-if="emptyMessage === 'rich'" #emptyMessage>
      <strong>No users found.</strong>
    </template>
  </DataTable>
</template>
```

### Sorting
Click column headers to toggle sort direction. Columns use DataTableColumnHeader for sort controls.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h } from "vue"
import { DataTable, DataTableColumnHeader, type ColumnDef } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  {
    accessorKey: "name",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Name" }),
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name),
  },
  {
    accessorKey: "role",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Role" }),
  },
  {
    accessorKey: "status",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Status" }),
  },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable
    :columns="columns"
    :data="users"
    :page-size="5"
    :initial-sorting="[{ id: 'name', desc: false }]"
  />
</template>
```

### Pagination
Automatic pagination with rows-per-page selector and navigation controls. Set page-size to control the initial page size; the select offers 10, 20, 50 and 100 plus that size. The controls hide when every row fits on one page.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { DataTable, type ColumnDef } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  { accessorKey: "id", header: "ID" },
  { accessorKey: "name", header: "Name" },
  { accessorKey: "department", header: "Department" },
  { accessorKey: "status", header: "Status" },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable :columns="columns" :data="users" :page-size="5" />
</template>
```

### Row click
Listen to @row-click to make rows clickable. Clickable rows also take Tab and call it on Enter or Space.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h, ref } from "vue"
import { DataTable, DataTableColumnHeader, type ColumnDef } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const selectedUser = ref<User | null>(null)

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  {
    accessorKey: "name",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Name" }),
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name),
  },
  { accessorKey: "email", header: "Email" },
  { accessorKey: "role", header: "Role" },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable
    :columns="columns"
    :data="users.slice(0, 8)"
    :page-size="4"
    @row-click="selectedUser = $event"
  />
  <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-secondary">
    {{
      selectedUser
        ? "Clicked: " + selectedUser.name + " (" + selectedUser.id + ") - " + selectedUser.department
        : "Click a row to see selected user details."
    }}
  </p>
</template>
```

### Row selection
Set enable-row-selection to add a first column of checkboxes. The header checkbox selects every row on all pages and shows a mixed state when only some are selected. Bind v-model:row-selection to read the selection; get-row-id keys it by your own ids.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h, ref } from "vue"
import {
  DataTable,
  DataTableColumnHeader,
  statusCell,
  type ColumnDef,
  type RowSelectionState,
} from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const rowSelection = ref<RowSelectionState>({})
const getRowId = (user: User) => user.id

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  {
    accessorKey: "name",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Name" }),
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name),
  },
  { accessorKey: "role", header: "Role" },
  { accessorKey: "status", header: "Status", cell: statusCell<User>((row) => row.status) },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable
    v-model:row-selection="rowSelection"
    :columns="columns"
    :data="users"
    :page-size="5"
    enable-row-selection
    :get-row-id="getRowId"
  />
  <p class="mt-3 text-sm text-secondary">
    {{ Object.keys(rowSelection).length }} of {{ users.length }} rows selected
  </p>
</template>
```

### Cell variants
Column helpers render common cells: idCell (monospace ID with copy-on-click), statusCell (status badge), dateTimeCell (formatted date) and tooltipHeader (header with an info tooltip). Other formats, like the salary below, are plain cell renderers; meta.align aligns a column.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h } from "vue"
import { DataTable, dateTimeCell, idCell, statusCell, tooltipHeader, type ColumnDef } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const money = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { style: "currency", currency: "USD", maximumFractionDigits: 0 })

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  { accessorKey: "id", header: "ID", cell: idCell<User>((row) => row.id) },
  { accessorKey: "name", header: "Name", cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name) },
  { accessorKey: "status", header: "Status", cell: statusCell<User>((row) => row.status) },
  { accessorKey: "joined", header: "Joined", cell: dateTimeCell<User>((row) => row.joined) },
  {
    accessorKey: "salary",
    header: tooltipHeader("Salary", "Annual base salary in USD."),
    meta: { align: "right" },
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, money.format(row.original.salary)),
  },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable :columns="columns" :data="users" :page-size="6" />
</template>
```

### Filtering
Control search with v-model:global-filter. The filter searches across all columns.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h, ref } from "vue"
import { DataTable, DataTableColumnHeader, statusCell, type ColumnDef } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

// Bind the same ref to a search field: <Input v-model:value="globalFilter" />
const globalFilter = ref("")

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  {
    accessorKey: "name",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Name" }),
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name),
  },
  { accessorKey: "role", header: "Role" },
  { accessorKey: "department", header: "Department" },
  { accessorKey: "status", header: "Status", cell: statusCell<User>((row) => row.status) },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable
    :columns="columns"
    :data="users"
    v-model:global-filter="globalFilter"
    :page-size="5"
  />
</template>
```

### Column visibility
Toggle column visibility with v-model:column-visibility.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { h, ref } from "vue"
import { DataTable, DataTableColumnHeader, statusCell, type ColumnDef, type VisibilityState } from "@plexui/vue/components/DataTable"
import { users, type User } from "./data"

const money = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { style: "currency", currency: "USD", maximumFractionDigits: 0 })

const columnVisibility = ref<VisibilityState>({
  role: true,
  department: true,
  status: true,
  salary: true,
})

const columns: ColumnDef<User>[] = [
  {
    accessorKey: "name",
    header: ({ column }) => h(DataTableColumnHeader, { column, title: "Name" }),
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", { class: "font-medium" }, row.original.name),
  },
  { accessorKey: "role", header: "Role" },
  { accessorKey: "department", header: "Department" },
  { accessorKey: "status", header: "Status", cell: statusCell<User>((row) => row.status) },
  {
    accessorKey: "salary",
    header: "Salary",
    meta: { align: "right" },
    cell: ({ row }) => h("span", money.format(row.original.salary)),
  },
]
</script>

<template>
  <DataTable
    :columns="columns"
    :data="users"
    :page-size="5"
    v-model:column-visibility="columnVisibility"
  />
</template>
```

## Tag Input
Input for entering multiple unique values as chip-style tokens, with suggestions and keyboard editing.
Import: import { TagInput } from "@plexui/vue/components/TagInput"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/tag-input
Props:
  - value / v-model:value (Tag[]) — Controlled value; update:value fires on every change
  - default-value (Tag[]) — Uncontrolled initial tags
  - @change ((tags: Tag[]) => void) — Fires when the tags change
  - id (string) — Id of the text input, so a label can target it with for
  - placeholder (string) — Input placeholder
  - validator ((value: string) => boolean) — Validates each tag when it is created; invalid tags shown in red
  - max-tags (number) — Maximum number of tags. At the limit the text input hides and a count shows below; the container then takes the tab stop, so arrow keys and Backspace still select and remove tags
  - rows (number) default: 1 — Min rows
  - size ('sm' | 'md' | 'lg' | 'xl' | '2xl' | '3xl') default: xl — Control height when rows=1; matches Input height of the same size
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully-rounded container and chips; matches Input/SelectControl pill
  - delimiters (string[]) default: [",", " "] — Characters that commit the current text as a tag
  - disabled (boolean) — Disables the input: tags cannot be added or removed, and pending text stays as typed
  - auto-focus (boolean) default: false — Focus on mount

### Overview
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-full min-w-0 max-w-[480px]">
  <TagInput placeholder="Add a tag..." :size="size" :rows="Number(rows)" :pill="pill" />
</div>

<!-- Phone: the tags are the value of a row, under its label. -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row orientation="vertical" label="Tags">
    <TagInput variant="ghost" placeholder="Add a tag..." :rows="Number(rows)" />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Inline filter row
When rows=1, TagInput matches the height of an Input or SelectControl of the same size, so it composes cleanly into horizontal filter rows. Toggle pill on both controls to keep the pair visually consistent.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="flex w-full max-w-2xl items-start gap-2">
  <Field label="Where" class="w-[160px] shrink-0">
    <Menu>
      <MenuTrigger>
        <SelectControl :size="size" :pill="pill" :selected="!!field" class="w-full">
          {{ fieldOptions.find((option) => option.value === field)?.label ?? '' }}
        </SelectControl>
      </MenuTrigger>
      <MenuContent :min-width="160" @close-auto-focus="(event) => event.preventDefault()">
        <MenuCheckboxItem
          v-for="option in fieldOptions"
          :key="option.value"
          :checked="field === option.value"
          indicator-variant="ghost"
          @update:checked="field = option.value"
        >
          {{ option.label }}
        </MenuCheckboxItem>
      </MenuContent>
    </Menu>
  </Field>
  <Field label="Is in" class="flex-1">
    <TagInput :size="size" :pill="pill" v-model:value="tags" placeholder="Add value, press Enter…" />
  </Field>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Where">
    <template #default="control">
      <Menu>
        <MenuTrigger>
          <SelectControl v-bind="control" :selected="!!field">
            {{ fieldOptions.find((option) => option.value === field)?.label ?? '' }}
          </SelectControl>
        </MenuTrigger>
        <MenuContent :min-width="160" align="end" @close-auto-focus="(event) => event.preventDefault()">
          <MenuCheckboxItem
            v-for="option in fieldOptions"
            :key="option.value"
            :checked="field === option.value"
            indicator-position="start"
            indicator-variant="ghost"
            @update:checked="field = option.value"
          >
            {{ option.label }}
          </MenuCheckboxItem>
        </MenuContent>
      </Menu>
    </template>
  </Field>
  <Field row orientation="vertical" label="Is in">
    <TagInput variant="ghost" v-model:value="tags" placeholder="Add a value" />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Suggestions
Combine TagInput with clickable buttons to let users add tags by clicking suggestions or typing manually. Suggestions disappear once selected.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<script setup lang="ts">
import { computed, ref } from "vue"
import { Button } from "@plexui/vue/components/Button"
import { TagInput, type Tag } from "@plexui/vue/components/TagInput"

const SUGGESTIONS = ["React", "TypeScript", "Next.js", "Tailwind CSS", "Node.js", "GraphQL", "PostgreSQL", "Redis", "Docker", "AWS"]

const tags = ref<Tag[]>([
  { value: "React", valid: true },
  { value: "TypeScript", valid: true },
])

const available = computed(() => {
  const selected = new Set(tags.value.map((t) => t.value))
  return SUGGESTIONS.filter((s) => !selected.has(s))
})
</script>

<template>
  <div class="flex flex-col gap-3">
    <TagInput v-model:value="tags" placeholder="Add a skill..." size="md" />
    <div v-if="available.length > 0" class="flex flex-wrap gap-1.5">
      <Button
        v-for="s in available"
        :key="s"
        size="xs"
        color="secondary"
        variant="soft"
        :pill="false"
        @click="tags = [...tags, { value: s, valid: true }]"
      >
        {{ s }}
      </Button>
    </div>
  </div>
</template>

<!-- Phone: each suggestion is a row with its Add button. -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row orientation="vertical" label="Skills">
    <TagInput variant="ghost" v-model:value="tags" placeholder="Add a skill..." />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
<FieldGroup v-if="suggestions.some((s) => !tags.some((t) => t.value === s))" title="Suggestions">
  <template v-for="s in suggestions" :key="s">
    <Field v-if="!tags.some((t) => t.value === s)" row :label="s">
      <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :aria-label="'Add ' + s" @click="tags = [...tags, { value: s, valid: true }]">
        Add
      </Button>
    </Field>
  </template>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Text Link
Text link for inline internal and external links with router integration, color inheritance, underline control, and weight options.
Import: import { TextLink } from "@plexui/vue/components/TextLink"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/text-link
Props:
  - default slot (VNode[]) — Link content
  - href (string) — URL. An http:// or https:// URL is external
  - to (string | Record<string, unknown>) — Router link destination (internal), such as a vue-router location
  - primary (boolean) default: false — Primary color, no default underline
  - underline (boolean) default: true — Underline (default true unless primary)
  - force-external (boolean) — Force external link behavior
  - as (Component) — Link component (e.g. RouterLink, NuxtLink)
  - target (string) — Where to open the link. External links default to _blank
  - class (string) — Extra classes on the link

### Overview
A TextLink sits in running text. By default it takes the surrounding text color (currentcolor) and an underline, a subtle difference from the text around it; on hover it fades the color, with special cases in text-secondary and text-tertiary text. Change the paragraph's color class to see it. primary colors the link instead and drops the underline by default. underline turns the underline on or off; unset, it is on unless the link is primary. An http:// or https:// URL is external: the link opens it in a new tab with target="_blank" and rel="noopener noreferrer", and an icon such as ArrowUpRight can mark it. A font-weight class in class sets the weight.
```vue
<p :class="colorClassName || undefined">
  You can use the
  <TextLink href="#" :primary="primary" :underline="underline === 'auto' ? undefined : underline === 'on'" :class="weight || undefined">responses endpoint</TextLink>
  to generate text. You can either use the API directly from an HTTP client of your choice, or use one of the
  <TextLink
    :href="external ? 'https://example.com' : '#'"
    :primary="primary"
    :underline="underline === 'auto' ? undefined : underline === 'on'"
    :class="weight || undefined"
  >official SDKs <ArrowUpRight v-if="external" /></TextLink>
  for your preferred language.
</p>
```

## Textarea
Multi-line text input that can grow with its content, with a size scale, autofill extension control, and invalid state styling.
Import: import { Textarea } from "@plexui/vue/components/Textarea"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/textarea
Props:
  - value / v-model:value (string | number) — Controlled value; uncontrolled when omitted
  - default-value (string | number) — Initial value when uncontrolled
  - variant ("outline" | "soft" | "ghost") default: "outline" — Visual style of the textarea; ghost has no border and no fill, for a row or a container that frames it
  - size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl" | "3xl") default: "md" — Control height for single-line equivalent
  - gutter-size ("2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl") — Horizontal padding; defaults from size
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the textarea
  - read-only (boolean) default: false — Make the textarea read-only (the native readonly attribute works too)
  - invalid (boolean) default: false — Mark as invalid
  - allow-autofill-extensions (boolean) default: !!name — Allow autofill extensions
  - auto-select (boolean) default: false — Select all contents when mounted
  - rows (number) default: 3 — Initial row count
  - auto-resize (boolean) default: false — Grow/shrink with content
  - max-rows (number) — Max rows when auto-resize; defaults to the larger of rows and 10
  - class (string) — Applied to the container <div>
  - @update:value ((value: string) => void) — Emitted on every input
  - @autofill (() => void) — Emitted when the browser autofills the textarea

### Overview
Use variant to stylize the textarea; ghost has no border and no fill, for a field its container already frames. size sets the control height for a single-line equivalent, with its own gutter-size (horizontal padding), font-size and border-radius; gutter-size overrides the padding. Bind the text with v-model:value. rows sets the initial height; with auto-resize the textarea grows and shrinks as the user types, up to max-rows, never fewer than rows (10 when unset). auto-select selects all its text, useful with copy actions; it acts when toggled and leaves the selection free to change. Autofill extensions are prevented by default; allow-autofill-extensions lets them in, and a name turns it on by default. disabled greys the textarea out and blocks interaction. invalid marks it invalid, with styles for each variant and [data-invalid] on the container; you decide how the state is removed (@focus, @input, a submit handler).On Phone the row is the frame and sets the type and insets: the textarea is ghost under the row's label, takes no size or gutter-size, grows the row with it, and the error is the row's error-message line. Browser autofill extensions are a desktop matter: on iOS autofill comes from the system above the keyboard, so that setting is desktop only.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-full min-w-0 max-w-[400px]">
  <Textarea
    :key="String(allowAutofillExtensions)"
    v-model:value="value"
    name="notes"
    placeholder="Add a note"
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :gutter-size="gutterSize === 'auto' ? undefined : gutterSize"
    :rows="rows"
    :max-rows="maxRows"
    :auto-resize="autoResize"
    :auto-select="autoSelect"
    :allow-autofill-extensions="allowAutofillExtensions"
    :disabled="disabled"
    :invalid="invalid"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone: the textarea is the value of a row, under its label; the row is its frame. -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row orientation="vertical" label="Notes" :error-message="invalid ? 'Keep the note to two lines.' : undefined">
    <Textarea
      v-model:value="value"
      variant="ghost"
      name="notes"
      placeholder="Add a note"
      :rows="rows"
      :max-rows="maxRows"
      :auto-resize="autoResize"
      :auto-select="autoSelect"
      :disabled="disabled"
      :invalid="invalid"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Tip
A short message that points out a feature people might miss, inside the page or anchored to a control, as TipKit tips do.
Import: import { Tip, TipPopover } from "@plexui/vue/components/Tip"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/tip
Props:
  - title (string) — The tip's title: what the feature is (or the title slot)
  - description (string) — One or two sentences on how to use it (or the description slot)
  - on-close (() => void) — Shows the Close button; called when it is clicked
  - arrow-edge ("top" | "bottom" | "left" | "right") — Draws an arrow at this edge, pointing at what the tip explains
  - mobile-actions ("rows" | "buttons") default: "rows" — How the actions show at 767px and below: rows of text under a separator, or the buttons as given, each a full-width capsule
  TipPopover:
    - open (boolean) — Shows the tip (controlled); bind with v-model:open
    - default-open (boolean) default: false — Shows the tip at first (uncontrolled)
    - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "bottom" — The side of the control the tip shows on, turned when it does not fit
    - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — The alignment against the control
    - side-offset (number) default: 4 — The distance from the control to the arrow's point, in pixels
    - close-button (boolean) default: true — Shows the Close button

### Overview
An inline tip takes its place in the layout. A symbol in the icon slot leads the message in the secondary color; on-close shows Close in the trailing corner. On a phone the tip is the opaque card of a grouped list, so place it on a grouped background. Actions in the actions slot sit side by side below the message, each as wide as the others; use small pill buttons, as the kit does. On a phone each action is a row of text in its color under a separator, one under another, starting under the text, as iOS cards show them. With mobile-actions="buttons" they keep their buttons there: each is a capsule as wide as the tip, 48px tall with a 17px label, in its own color and variant, as the iOS card in Settings that opens the Support app shows. Use it for a card that asks for a permission, with one soft primary button. It changes nothing on a desktop, so only the Phone preview offers it, and only with actions. With arrow-edge the tip points at what it explains with the kit's arrow, centered on that edge, and leaves room for the arrow on that side. On a phone the tip stands over or under its control, as TipKit's inline tip does: no iOS screen sets a control beside a card of a grouped list, so the Phone preview offers no sideways arrow.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Bell } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';
import { Tip } from '@plexui/vue/components/Tip';

const shown = ref(true);
const icon = ref(true);
const onClose = ref(true);
const actions = ref<"none" | "one" | "two">("none");
const mobileActions = ref<"rows" | "buttons">("rows"); // a phone only: rows of text, or the buttons
const arrowEdge = ref<"none" | "top" | "bottom" | "left" | "right">("none"); // a phone: none, top or bottom
</script>

<template>
<div :class="['flex w-full md:max-w-[360px] items-center gap-1', arrowEdge === 'left' || arrowEdge === 'right' ? 'flex-row' : 'flex-col']">
  <Button v-if="arrowEdge === 'top' || arrowEdge === 'left'" color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Notifications" class="shrink-0">
    <Bell />
  </Button>
  <Tip
    v-if="shown"
    title="Turn on notifications"
    description="Get a message when someone replies to your post or mentions you."
    :on-close="onClose ? () => (shown = false) : undefined"
    :mobile-actions="actions === 'none' ? undefined : mobileActions"
    :arrow-edge="arrowEdge === 'none' ? undefined : arrowEdge"
    :class="arrowEdge === 'left' || arrowEdge === 'right' ? 'min-w-0 flex-1' : 'w-full'"
  >
    <template v-if="icon" #icon><Bell /></template>
    <template v-if="actions !== 'none'" #actions>
      <Button v-if="actions === 'two'" color="primary" variant="soft" size="sm" pill>Not now</Button>
      <Button color="primary" :variant="actions === 'two' ? 'solid' : 'soft'" size="sm" pill>Turn on</Button>
    </template>
  </Tip>
  <Button v-else color="secondary" variant="soft" @click="shown = true">Show the tip again</Button>
  <Button v-if="arrowEdge === 'bottom' || arrowEdge === 'right'" color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Notifications" class="shrink-0">
    <Bell />
  </Button>
</div>
</template>
```

### Popover
A popover tip floats next to its control, the default slot, with an arrow that points at it. The control stays usable and keeps the focus. Close, Escape or a press outside the tip and its control closes it, through v-model:open. Show it when the control appears; here the button shows it.
```vue
<TipPopover
  v-model:open="open"
  title="Filter the list"
  description="Show only the items from the last week, month or year."
>
  <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" @click="open = !open">
    <Filter />
    Filter
  </Button>
</TipPopover>
```

## Toast
Toast notifications for transient feedback, with semantic variants, descriptions, action buttons, promise integration, and persistent toasts.
Import: import { Toaster, toast } from "@plexui/vue/components/Toast"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/toast
  Toaster:
    - position ("top-left" | "top-center" | "top-right" | "bottom-left" | "bottom-center" | "bottom-right") default: "top-center" — Where toasts anchor on the viewport
    - duration (number) default: 3000 — Default auto-dismiss duration in ms
    - offset (string | number | { top?; right?; bottom?; left? }) default: 32px — Distance from the viewport edge. Without a top or bottom edge here, the page's --toast-offset-top or --toast-offset-bottom sets it.
    - mobile-offset (string | number | { top?; right?; bottom?; left? }) default: 16px top and bottom, 14px at the sides, past the safe area — Gutter under 600px, where a toast fills the width between the side gutters. Explicit edges override their defaults; omitted edges keep them, and the page's --toast-mobile-offset-top or --toast-mobile-offset-bottom sets the top or bottom one.
    - gap (number) — Gap between stacked toasts in px

### Overview
Call toast with a message, or toast.success, .error, .warning and .info for the semantic types, which map to PlexUI's success, danger, warning and info tokens. Each of them shows its icon; icon: null leaves it out, and icon takes a component of your own instead. No richColors is passed, so the types retheme with data-theme. The options add a description under the message, an action button and a cancel one (either closes the toast after its onClick), and closeButton. toast.promise ties one toast to an async operation: it shows a loading state, then swaps to success or error, and takes the same options; here saveProfile() resolves with {"{ name: \"Profile\" }"} after 1.5 seconds. Spread compact into the options for a centered toast: on a desktop a toast shrinks to its content anyway, and on a phone, where a toast fills the width, a compact one keeps the width of its content. duration: Infinity keeps a toast open until the user closes it, so give it a labelled action or a close button: a way to close it from the keyboard. An error that waits like this is an alert (see Screen readers). On a desktop the message and its controls share a row: the buttons stand 16px after the message and 8px apart, small 28px buttons with 6px corners (the action solid, the cancel soft), and the close button is the same 28px button, plain, at the end. With a description the buttons go under the message, at the end, and the close button stays on the title's line. On a phone a toast takes the corners, padding and text of an iOS notification banner, the system's own message at the top of the screen: 24px corners and 15pt text, with its buttons drawn as a medium phone Button, a 34px capsule in a 44px target. One button stands at the end of the message's row, where a banner shows its time, and the message wraps in its own column; a cancel and an action together stand under the message, and so does a button that would leave the message less than half the row (8em in a toast as wide as its content). Under 600px it fills the width between 14px gutters past the safe area, as notifications do on an iOS phone; a compact toast keeps its own width. It stays a message of the page, with an opaque surface.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { compact as compactOptions, toast } from '@plexui/vue/components/Toast';

const type = ref("default"); // or "success", "error", "warning", "info", "promise"
const icon = ref(true); // the type's icon; false passes icon: null
const description = ref("none"); // or "short", "long"
const action = ref("none"); // or "action", "cancel" (an action and a cancel)
const closeButton = ref(false);
const compact = ref(false);
const persistent = ref(false); // with an action or a close button

// Each type's title, its short description and its action's label
const messages = {
  default: { title: "Changes saved", description: "Your profile is live for everyone on the team.", action: "Undo" },
  success: { title: "Inquiry ID copied", description: "Paste it into the support form.", action: "Open" },
  error: { title: "Failed to assign owner", description: "The owner has left the workspace.", action: "Retry" },
  warning: { title: "Session expires in 2 minutes", description: "Unsaved changes will be lost.", action: "Stay" },
  info: { title: "New version available", description: "Reload to get the latest features.", action: "Reload" },
};
const longDescription = "This is a long message to show how the toast handles multi-line content without clipping or awkward wrapping. The title and description should both wrap cleanly.";

// Resolves with the saved profile after 1.5 seconds
const saveProfile = () => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(() => resolve({ name: "Profile" }), 1500));

// The template passes the settings; a promise ends as the default type's message
function show(settings) {
  const message = messages[settings.type === "promise" ? "default" : settings.type];
  const options = {
    description: settings.description === "none" ? undefined : settings.description === "short" ? message.description : longDescription,
    action: settings.action === "none" ? undefined : { label: message.action, onClick: () => {} },
    cancel: settings.action === "cancel" ? { label: "Cancel", onClick: () => {} } : undefined,
    closeButton: settings.closeButton,
    // Open until closed, once there is a button to close it
    duration: settings.persistent && (settings.action !== "none" || settings.closeButton) ? Infinity : undefined,
    ...(settings.compact ? compactOptions : {}),
  };
  if (settings.type === "promise") {
    toast.promise(saveProfile(), { loading: "Saving…", success: (data) => `${data.name} saved`, error: "Save failed", ...options });
  } else {
    // The type's icon; null leaves it out (a promise keeps its loader)
    (settings.type === "default" ? toast : toast[settings.type])(message.title, { ...options, icon: settings.icon ? undefined : null });
  }
}
</script>

<template>
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="show({ type, icon, description, action, closeButton, compact, persistent })">
  Show toast
</Button>
</template>
```

## Toggle Group
A row of buttons that each stay pressed or not, any number at a time, such as the days an alarm repeats on, as the round weekday buttons of the iOS Clock app.
Import: import { ToggleGroup, ToggleGroupItem } from "@plexui/vue/components/ToggleGroup"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/toggle-group
Props:
  - value (string[]) — Controlled values of the pressed items; bind with v-model:value
  - default-value (string[]) default: [] — Values of the items pressed at first (uncontrolled)
  - color ("primary" | "secondary" | "danger" | "success" | "info" | "discovery" | "caution" | "warning") default: "primary" — Fill of a pressed item, a solid button in this color
  - size ("3xs" | "2xs" | "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg" | "xl" | "2xl" | "3xl") default: "md" — Size of the items, as the Button sizes
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded items; with uniform, circles
  - uniform (boolean) default: false — Items as wide as they are tall, for a letter or an icon
  - block (boolean) default: false — Fills the width of its container, the items spread across it
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disables every item
  - dir ("ltr" | "rtl") default: "ltr" — Reading direction of the items, which the arrow keys follow
  - aria-label (string) — Name of the group, when no visible label names it
  - aria-labelledby (string) — The id of the visible label of the group
  - id (string) — The id of the group
  ToggleGroupItem:
    - value (string) — Value of the item, in the group's value while it is pressed
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disables the item
    - aria-label (string) — Name of the item, when its content does not say it in full, such as a weekday's letter

### Overview
Round items spread across their row are the weekday buttons of the iOS Clock app's Repeat row, as in Add Alarm. On a phone with touch.css each is a 35pt circle with its letter at 17pt Semibold, as the native ones are. A narrow letter alone does not name a day: give each item the day's full name with aria-label. The summary beside the label is the app's to write; the example reads as the Clock app's does. The group's color fills a pressed item, a solid button; an item that is not pressed stays a soft secondary button whatever the color. size sizes every item as it sizes a Button, from 3xs (22px) to 3xl (48px). pill rounds the items fully, circles here, as uniform makes them as wide as they are tall, for a letter or an icon. block spreads them across the width of their container; without it the group is as wide as its items, 8px apart. disabled disables the whole group. On a phone with touch.css every item is fully rounded, as iOS draws its buttons, and a letter is the Clock app's 35pt circle at every size, so the Phone preview offers neither pill nor size. Items of text there take the three iOS button heights.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { ToggleGroup, ToggleGroupItem } from '@plexui/vue/components/ToggleGroup';

const week = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7].map((day) => {
  // 1 January 2024 is a Monday
  const date = Date.UTC(2024, 0, day);
  const format = (weekday: 'narrow' | 'long') =>
    new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', { weekday, timeZone: 'UTC' }).format(date);
  return { value: String(day), letter: format('narrow'), name: format('long') };
});

const days = ref(['3', '4', '5']);
const color = ref<'primary' | 'secondary' | 'danger' | 'info' | 'discovery' | 'success' | 'caution' | 'warning'>('primary');
const size = ref<'3xs' | '2xs' | 'xs' | 'sm' | 'md' | 'lg' | 'xl' | '2xl' | '3xl'>('md');
const pill = ref(true);
const block = ref(true);
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
  <span id="repeat-label">Repeat</span>
  <ToggleGroup
    aria-labelledby="repeat-label"
    v-model:value="days"
    :color="color"
    :size="size"
    :pill="pill"
    uniform
    :block="block"
    :disabled="disabled"
  >
    <ToggleGroupItem v-for="day in week" :key="day.value" :value="day.value" :aria-label="day.name">
      {{ day.letter }}
    </ToggleGroupItem>
  </ToggleGroup>
</template>
```

### Text
Items of text take the width of their label, here filters that can be on together. Without block the group is as wide as its items, 8px apart.
```vue
<ToggleGroup aria-label="Show" :default-value="['unread']">
  <ToggleGroupItem value="unread">Unread</ToggleGroupItem>
  <ToggleGroupItem value="starred">Starred</ToggleGroupItem>
  <ToggleGroupItem value="mentions">Mentions</ToggleGroupItem>
</ToggleGroup>
```

### Disabled
disabled on an item keeps it as it is: Email stays pressed, as account emails cannot be turned off, and SMS stays released until there is a phone number. A disabled item shows at half opacity, pressed or not, as iOS dims a disabled segmented control, and it can be neither pressed nor reached with the arrow keys.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col items-start gap-3">
  <span id="notify-label">Notify me by</span>
  <ToggleGroup aria-labelledby="notify-label" :default-value="['email', 'push']">
    <ToggleGroupItem value="email" disabled>Email</ToggleGroupItem>
    <ToggleGroupItem value="push">Push</ToggleGroupItem>
    <ToggleGroupItem value="sms" disabled>SMS</ToggleGroupItem>
  </ToggleGroup>
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Account emails are always on. Add a phone number to get SMS.</span>
</div>
```

## Toolbar
"The top and bottom bars of a phone screen: navigation, a title, a search field and actions over the content. Desktop layouts have their own composition, so a toolbar never renders from 768px up."
Import: import { Toolbar, ToolbarLeading, ToolbarTitle, ToolbarTrailing } from "@plexui/vue/components/Toolbar"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/toolbar
Props:
  - placement ("top" | "bottom") default: "top" — Top bar with 44px targets, or bottom bar with 48px targets
  - titleStyle ("inline" | "large" | "compact-large" | "compact-medium") default: "inline" — Size and place of ToolbarTitle in a top bar
  - titleAlign ("center" | "start") default: "center" — Alignment of an inline title. Large and compact titles always start at the leading edge
  - background ("soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "blur") default: "glass" at the top, "soft" at the bottom — How the bar meets the content scrolled under it, see Background. edge is the earlier name of soft and solid of hard
  - class (string) — Class of the bar
  ToolbarTitle:
    - subtitle (string) — Second, smaller line below the title. The subtitle slot takes precedence
    - as ("h1" | "h2" | "h3") default: "h1" — Heading level of the title

### Overview
Leading and trailing items take 44px targets; an inline title is centered across the whole width. title-style sets the size and place of the title: inline in the row of controls, large on a row of its own below them (on a page it stands at the top of the content and scrolls with it, overscroll included; once it has gone under the controls, they show it inline), and compact-large or compact-medium at the leading edge of the row, on a screen with no Back button. subtitle adds a second line under an inline or large title. title-align="start" puts an inline title after the leading items. Scroll the content: the bar's background meets the rows under it, and the glass starts to cast a shadow. ToolbarGroup puts related actions in one capsule; a single action, as Back or Close, keeps a capsule of its own. Without ToolbarTitle the bar holds its controls alone. A ButtonGroup in a toolbar is the same capsule. Name each icon button with aria-label. ToolbarContent fill stretches a search field across the row in the place of the title, up to the trailing items; the field takes the row height and the material of the bar buttons. That is the open search: until a tap opens it, the search waits in a field across a row under the controls (search row) or behind a Search button in the bar (search button), and Close puts it back, as iOS 27.2 raises the field of the bar's drawer into the bar and hides the title and the buttons; a segmented row stays under the open field. A Search button can open its field at the bottom instead (field bottom), as SwiftUI's search button opens it over the keyboard: the field and Close take a bottom bar of their own, the top bar's title and buttons go, and a segmented row moves up in their place. ToolbarRow adds a row under the controls, part of the bar: a SegmentedControl that switches the screen's views, as a chat list shows its folders under its bar, or a search field. It spans the bar between its gutters, 10px under the controls, and the bar's background and line take it in. Several rows stand in their order, 10px apart: a search field over a segmented control, as iOS 27.2 sets them under the search field of a bar's drawer. A segmented control in it is glass at its default size, as a chat list draws its folders under its bar in iOS 27: a 40px capsule, the gray selection 34px and 3px inside it, 15px labels 12px from their segments' ends. It fills the row: its segments share the width and scroll when they do not fit, and a badge is not drawn, its count staying in the segment's accessible name. size="sm" gives the small plain one (32px, as iOS 27.2 draws a segmented Picker under a bar) and size="lg" the large glass one (48px). A search field in it is the bar's: 44px of glass. Under a large title on a page the rows stand under the title at the top of the content, 16px under its baseline; scrolled up to the bar they stay pinned under its controls, and the bar's background, edge and line reach under them, as iOS 27.2 pins them. placement="bottom" sets the bar at the bottom of the page with 48px targets and the device's bottom safe area. ToolbarContent centers a main action; with fill a search field or a single action takes the whole row. The 48px items stand 28px from the sides and above the home indicator, 12px apart, whatever the background: iOS 27.2 keeps them in place over every scroll edge, and a band (glass or hard) only starts 10px above them. A search field holds SearchSm, set in Regular; a plus next to it takes icon-size="sm", as Contacts draws it smaller than the other symbols. The page content scrolls under both bars. A screen holds one search field, so with one in the top bar the bottom bar offers neither its own field nor its Search button. background sets how a bar meets the content scrolled under it. glass, the default of a top bar, is a translucent, blurred bar with a hairline. soft, the default of a bottom bar, is the scroll edge effect of iOS 26 and draws no line: once content is under a top bar with a title, a blur hides it under the controls and lets go of it past the bar, under a thin veil of the canvas color (iOS 27.2: 55% at the top, 20% at the bar's bottom, none 24px below), while a top bar of controls alone on a page covers only the status bar, and once content runs on under a bottom bar on a page, a blur hides it under the controls behind a veil of the canvas color that grows toward the screen's edge, with no band and no line (iOS 27.2: none 34px above the items, 15% at their top, 46% from 43px into them); each glass control stands out over it. A Sheet's bottom bar fades its content into the panel. hard is an opaque bar of the canvas color with a hairline. blur has no fill and no line: the content under the bar is blurred a little over the bar and past it, and heavily toward the screen's edge. Glass, hard and blur show themselves once content is under the bar: under the top bar after the content has scrolled, over the bottom bar while it continues below. PageHeader, PageFooter, the bars of a Sheet and TabBar take the same values. The top and the bottom bar each take their own; the bottom bar's controls stand under the preview.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Button, NavigationButton } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { DotsHorizontal, Filter, Plus, PlusLg, Search, SearchSm, Translate } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';
import { Input } from '@plexui/vue/components/Input';
import { Page, PageContent } from '@plexui/vue/components/Page';
import { SegmentedControl, SegmentedControlOption } from '@plexui/vue/components/SegmentedControl';
import { Toolbar, ToolbarContent, ToolbarGroup, ToolbarLeading, ToolbarRow, ToolbarTitle, ToolbarTrailing } from '@plexui/vue/components/Toolbar';

const search = ref<"none" | "row" | "button">("none"); // where the search waits: a field under the controls or a button in the bar
const searching = ref(false); // a tap opens it: its field in place of the title, with Close
const field = ref<"top" | "bottom">("top"); // where a Search button opens its field: "top" (in the bar) or "bottom"
const title = ref(true); // false: the bar holds its controls alone
const titleStyle = ref<"inline" | "large" | "compact-large" | "compact-medium">("inline");
const titleAlign = ref<"center" | "start">("center"); // an inline title only
const subtitle = ref(false); // not with a compact title
const group = ref(false);
const segmented = ref(false); // a row of the bar with the views, under the search field
const rowSize = ref<"sm" | "md" | "lg">("md"); // "sm": the small plain control, "lg": the large glass one
const view = ref("all");
const topBackground = ref<"soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "blur">("glass");
const bottom = ref<"none" | "buttons" | "search" | "action">("none"); // "search": one search field to a screen
const bottomBackground = ref<"soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "blur">("soft");
</script>

<template>
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar :title-style="!(searching && search !== 'none') && title ? titleStyle : ..." :title-align="!(searching && search !== 'none') && title && titleStyle === 'inline' ? titleAlign : undefined" :background="topBackground">
    <ToolbarContent v-if="searching && search !== 'none' && !(search === 'button' && field === 'bottom')" fill>
      <Input variant="soft" pill auto-select placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders">
        <template #startAdornment>
          <SearchSm />
        </template>
      </Input>
    </ToolbarContent>
    <ToolbarLeading v-if="!(searching && search !== 'none') && !(title && titleStyle.startsWith('compact'))">
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle v-if="!(searching && search !== 'none') && title" :subtitle="subtitle && !titleStyle.startsWith('compact') ? '24 orders' : undefined">Orders</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing v-if="!(searching && search === 'button' && field === 'bottom')">
      <NavigationButton v-if="searching && search !== 'none'" action="close" aria-label="Close search" @click="searching = false; $nextTick(() => { const opener = search === 'button' ? $refs.searchButton?.$el : $refs.searchField?.input; if (opener) { opener.dataset.restoring = ''; opener.focus(); delete opener.dataset.restoring } })" />
      <template v-else>
        <Button v-if="search === 'button'" ref="searchButton" color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Search" @click="searching = true"><Search /></Button>
        <ToolbarGroup v-if="group" aria-label="Order actions">
          <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Translate"><Translate /></Button>
          <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
        </ToolbarGroup>
        <NavigationButton v-else :action="title && titleStyle === 'large' ? 'confirm' : 'close'" />
      </template>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
    <ToolbarRow v-if="search === 'row' && !searching" @focusin="'restoring' in $event.target.dataset || (searching = true)" @click="searching = true">
      <Input ref="searchField" variant="soft" pill placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders">
        <template #startAdornment>
          <SearchSm />
        </template>
      </Input>
    </ToolbarRow>
    <ToolbarRow v-if="segmented">
      <SegmentedControl v-model:value="view" :size="rowSize" aria-label="Orders">
        <SegmentedControlOption value="all">All</SegmentedControlOption>
        <SegmentedControlOption value="paid">Paid</SegmentedControlOption>
        <SegmentedControlOption value="unpaid" :badge="{ content: 3, pill: true }">Unpaid</SegmentedControlOption>
        <SegmentedControlOption value="refunds">Refunds</SegmentedControlOption>
      </SegmentedControl>
    </ToolbarRow>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>
    undefined
  </PageContent>
  <Toolbar v-if="searching && search === 'button' && field === 'bottom'" placement="bottom" :background="bottom === 'none' || bottom === 'search' ? 'soft' : bottomBackground">
    <ToolbarContent fill>
      <Input variant="soft" pill auto-select placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders">
        <template #startAdornment>
          <SearchSm />
        </template>
      </Input>
    </ToolbarContent>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <NavigationButton action="close" aria-label="Close search" @click="searching = false; $nextTick(() => $refs.searchButton?.$el?.focus())" />
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <Toolbar v-else-if="bottom !== 'none' && !(search !== 'none' && bottom === 'search')" placement="bottom" :background="bottomBackground">
    <template v-if="bottom === 'buttons'">
      <ToolbarLeading>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Filter"><Filter /></Button>
      </ToolbarLeading>
      <ToolbarContent>
        <Button color="primary" pill>New order</Button>
      </ToolbarContent>
      <ToolbarTrailing v-if="search === 'none'">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Search"><Search /></Button>
      </ToolbarTrailing>
    </template>
    <template v-else-if="bottom === 'search'">
      <ToolbarContent fill>
        <Input variant="soft" pill placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders">
          <template #startAdornment>
            <SearchSm />
          </template>
        </Input>
      </ToolbarContent>
      <ToolbarTrailing>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill icon-size="sm" aria-label="New order"><PlusLg /></Button>
      </ToolbarTrailing>
    </template>
    <ToolbarContent v-else fill>
      <Button color="primary" pill block><Plus /> New order</Button>
    </ToolbarContent>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
</template>
```

### Search in the content
A search field in the content, under a large title or at the top of a sheet, is an Input with type="search": on Phone it is the bar's search field wherever it stands: the same magnifier, text and placeholder, and with variant="soft" pill the same glass capsule. It stands at the top of PageContent and scrolls up with the title; the content starts 16px under the title's baseline, and a list 10px under the field. With @clear it shows the filled clear circle of iOS while it holds text, in place of the browser's own clear button.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar title-style="large">
    <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>
    <Input v-model:value="query" type="search" variant="soft" pill placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders" class="mb-2.5" @clear="query = ''">
      <template #startAdornment>
        <SearchSm />
      </template>
    </Input>
    ...
  </PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Disabled
A disabled bar button keeps its glass capsule and draws its symbol or label in the tertiary label color, as the iOS 26 bar buttons do. Inside ToolbarGroup the capsule belongs to the group, so the enabled actions next to it keep their look. A prominent action, NavigationButton action="confirm" or a solid primary Button, drops its color for a gray capsule, with a white label in light and a dim one in dark. Cancel and More stay enabled.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" pill>Cancel</Button>
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>New order</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <NavigationButton action="confirm" disabled />
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
  <Toolbar placement="bottom">
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Filter" disabled><Filter /></Button>
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarContent>
      <Button color="primary" pill disabled>Place order</Button>
    </ToolbarContent>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <ToolbarGroup aria-label="Order actions">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Translate" disabled><Translate /></Button>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </ToolbarGroup>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
```

### Sheet
In a Sheet the toolbar sits at the top edge and takes the place of the Sheet's own bar and Close button on a phone. Keep DialogHeader with DialogTitle: it still names the dialog for assistive technology, and it is the header from 768px up.
```vue
<Dialog v-model:open="open">
  <DialogTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline">Show orders</Button>
  </DialogTrigger>
  <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" surface="grouped">
    <Toolbar>
      <ToolbarLeading>
        <DialogClose>
          <NavigationButton action="close" />
        </DialogClose>
      </ToolbarLeading>
      <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
    </Toolbar>
    <DialogHeader>
      <DialogTitle>Orders</DialogTitle>
    </DialogHeader>
    <DialogBody>
      ...
    </DialogBody>
  </DialogContent>
</Dialog>
```

## Tooltip
Brief informative text shown on hover or focus, for any trigger element, with conditional enabling and a compact variant.
Import: import { Tooltip, TooltipTriggerDecorator } from "@plexui/vue/components/Tooltip"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/tooltip
Props:
  - default slot (VNode) — The trigger: a single element. Wrap plain text in TooltipTriggerDecorator
  - content (string | number | VNode | null) — Tooltip content, or the #content slot (null = no tooltip)
  - compact (boolean) default: false — Short 1-3 word tooltips, inverse styling
  - interactive (boolean) default: false — Content is interactive, stays open when hovered
  - max-width (number | "none") default: 300 — Max width of tooltip
  - open-delay (number) default: 150 — Delay before showing (ms)
  - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "top" — Preferred side
  - side-offset (number) default: 5 — Distance from trigger
  - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Alignment
  - align-offset (number) default: 0 — Alignment offset
  - gutter-size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Content padding
  - content-class-name (string) — Class for tooltip content
  - force-open (boolean) — Force open or closed
  - prevent-unintentional-click-to-close (boolean) default: false — Keeps the tooltip open when the trigger is clicked right after it opened
  - content slot (VNode[]) — Rich tooltip content; takes precedence over content

### Overview
Any element can be the trigger: trigger shows text, a button and an icon button. Text is no tab stop of its own, so wrap it in TooltipTriggerDecorator, which makes it one and marks it with a dotted underline; a plain span shows its tooltip on hover only, and a keyboard never reaches it. An icon button names itself with aria-label, and its tooltip says the same in words. compact is for short tooltips of one to three words: little padding (2px by 8px) and inverted colors, a dark fill with light text in the light theme and the other way round in the dark one. side is the side the tooltip prefers; it turns to the other side when there is no room. A disabled button can tell why in its tooltip on hover (a disabled button is no tab stop): pass the reason as content while it is disabled. To show a tooltip only under a condition, pass null otherwise.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Copy } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';
import { Tooltip, TooltipTriggerDecorator } from '@plexui/vue/components/Tooltip';

const trigger = ref<'text' | 'button' | 'icon'>('text');
const compact = ref(false);
const side = ref<'top' | 'right' | 'bottom' | 'left'>('top');
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
  <!-- Text: TooltipTriggerDecorator makes it a trigger -->
  <Tooltip v-if="trigger === 'text'" content="This is additional context that appears when the trigger is hovered or focused" :compact="compact" :side="side">
    <TooltipTriggerDecorator>Simple text with tooltip</TooltipTriggerDecorator>
  </Tooltip>
  <!-- A button -->
  <Tooltip v-else-if="trigger === 'button'" :content="disabled ? 'This action is disabled for reasons' : 'Tooltip on button'" :compact="compact" :side="side">
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" :disabled="disabled">
      Sample button
    </Button>
  </Tooltip>
  <!-- An icon button -->
  <Tooltip v-else :content="disabled ? 'This action is disabled for reasons' : 'Copy to clipboard'" :compact="compact" :side="side">
    <Button color="secondary" variant="ghost" size="md" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" :disabled="disabled">
      <Copy />
    </Button>
  </Tooltip>
</template>
```
